# LINE3 local decisions (design §8.2)

Every local decision recorded in the code so far, copied from the code's own words. Nothing here is an owner decision; none changes meaning-level behaviour. Meaning-level questions are listed at the end as open points.

## Geometry (verantyx/line3/geometry.py)

| id | decision | location |
|---|---|---|
| L-15 | Composition convention: (g*h)(a) = g(h(a)), i.e. h acts first. | geometry.py, class Rotation docstring |
| L-16 | G24 index order = lexicographic perm; an index is a label, never a tie-break winner. | geometry.py, _build_group |
| L-17 | Opaque cell values limited to int, str or None so that serialisation is exact and hash-seed independent (L-02: no floats). | geometry.py, _check_cell |
| L-18 | Contents are not moved by a rotation; the orientation g in G24 is stored (design §3.3 `orientation : g`). | geometry.py, class Cross docstring |
| L-19 | Cross serialisation is exact, deterministic bytes including orientation. | geometry.py, Cross.serialize |
| L-20 | swap(p, p) is the identity (allowed, so the move is total). | geometry.py, swap |

## Space (verantyx/line3/space.py, module docstring)

| id | decision | location |
|---|---|---|
| L-30 | RUN content runs come from verantyx.lang.ja_content_runs (design 3.1 default); the text between consecutive runs (incl. runs that function dropped: digits, dates, stop words) is cut at every non-letter/number/mark character and each piece is a unit (design 3.1: "は", "の", "にある"). | space.py module docstring |
| L-31 | Surfaces are kept as written (no Unicode normalisation, no case folding). | space.py module docstring |
| L-32 | A character is "letter-like" iff its Unicode category starts with L, N or M; everything else (punctuation P*, symbols S*, separators Z*, controls C*) is dropped in every tier. A WORD token is a unit iff it contains at least one letter-like character; a unit is its whole surface (L-31). | space.py module docstring |
| L-33 | Sentence id = position in the input sequence. Duplicate sentences are stored separately and each counts in N (no silent de-duplication). | space.py module docstring |
| L-34 | n(u,v) and p(u,v) are computed on demand from postings / stored word order (L-13) and never cached; the serialisation stores units, postings, sentence unit lists and r0 only. | space.py module docstring |
| L-35 | Serialisation = canonical JSON (sorted keys, compact, UTF-8, units sorted by code point; sorting is for byte identity, never to pick a winner). | space.py module docstring |
| L-36 | A sentence with no unit in a tier stays a sentence (counts in N). | space.py module docstring |
| L-40 | Every sentence has a `kind` in KINDS (base / memory_query / memory_answer / memory_user, design L-19) and a `source`. Missing kind = "base"; an unknown kind raises ValueError (no silent coercion). `Space.sentences` stays (text, source) pairs; kinds live in the parallel tuple `Space.kinds`. | space.py module docstring |
| L-41 | Append (`Space.append`, `TierSpace.append`) returns a NEW immutable object; old sids, old postings (as prefixes) and old sentence_units are unchanged, new sentences get sids N..N+k-1. Appending == building from scratch on all sentences, byte for byte (tested). | space.py module docstring |
| L-42 | `postings_union(tier, units)` = sorted distinct sids of the union of the units' postings (M-1(c): a bundled state's quantity). A unit not in the tier raises KeyError (no silent skip); an empty set gives (). | space.py module docstring |
| L-43 | FORMAT bumped to line3.space.v2 because every sentence entry now carries "kind"; the serialisation of the unchanged part is otherwise untouched. | space.py module docstring |
| L-44 | `strip_attribution` is applied to every sentence text BEFORE it is split into units (build_space, Space.append); the stored sentence text keeps the original, only the units are computed from the stripped text. | space.py module docstring |

Note: L-44 is the number given to the `strip_attribution`-before-splitting step (space.py `build_space` and `Space.append`; previously unnumbered).

## Energy (verantyx/line3/energy.py, module docstring)

| id | decision | location |
|---|---|---|
| L-50 | n(q,q) = n(q): a query unit shares every sentence with itself, so a placed query unit gets +n(q)/N (literal reading of the N-01 formula). | energy.py module docstring |
| L-51 | The query is a set: repeated query units count once. | energy.py module docstring |
| L-52 | Query units or placed units absent from the space have n = 0 (energy 0, no shared sentences); they are neither errors nor evidence. | energy.py module docstring |
| L-53 | Placed unit absent from the space: r0 = 0 (same rule as L-52). | energy.py module docstring |
| L-54 | A section looks along every arm visible in its window (I-09); each arm is walked separately; the section points to the terminus with the strictly greatest energy (same unit from several arms is one candidate; a tie between different units, or no terminus, points nowhere). | energy.py module docstring |
| L-55 | Walk (design 4.4 R1): outer end k=0 -> ... -> k=L-1 -> centre. A step x -> y is taken iff y is not an empty seat, the edge is evidenced (n(x,y) > 0) and E_s(y) >= E_s(x) ("does not drop"). The unit where the walk stops is the terminus (the centre unit if the centre is reached). A walk starting on an empty seat has no terminus. | energy.py module docstring |
| L-56 | A unit placed on several seats gets the sum of its per-seat F and B. Pairs of seats holding the same unit contribute nothing. | energy.py module docstring |
| L-57 | A "working" section is one whose pointer is not None. The section ratio R1 points to u iff there is at least one working section and all working sections point to u. Working sections that point to different units give status "section_disagreement" (design 4.8); R1 is then None. | energy.py module docstring |
| L-58 | A maximum must be strictly positive to point at a unit: an all-zero quantity carries no evidence and points nowhere. | energy.py module docstring |
| L-59 | A section's energy is E_q when a query unit q is attached to it (I-07, design 4.1) and E_Q otherwise. | energy.py module docstring |

## Placement (verantyx/line3/placement.py, module docstring)

| id | decision | location |
|---|---|---|
| L-60 | Pool = the units admitted to the cross. Moves = the 23 non-identity rotations and the swap of any two seats (empty seats included, geometry.moves_swap). No replacement by units outside the cross (design 3.4 "pool の外の単位とも" is NOT implemented; the T4 brief says rotations + seat swaps). | placement.py module docstring |
| L-61 | (kept; now exactly the owner's "区別しない" decision) State identity for ties = (centre, multiset of legs read outer to inner, empty seats kept). The key cannot see which leg is which, so leg permutations are one state; rotations only change orientation (key is rotation-invariant). The concrete Cross puts the legs in canonical sorted order with identity orientation (a label, not a winner). | placement.py |
| L-62 | **SUPERSEDED by the owner's decision after the T4 measurement (2026-10-06: tied arrangements are grown together as one state; see L-70). Kept for the record; no longer implemented.** Result status: STABLE = exactly one terminal state and no single move gives a different state of equal key; TIED = several terminals; PLATEAU = one terminal but a different state of equal key is one move away; BUDGET = branch budget exceeded. Only STABLE lets a cross grow (N-05). | placement.py |
| L-63 | Growth starts from the seed alone at the centre (L=1), stable by definition. Candidates = units v != seed with n(seed,v) > 0, grouped by n(seed,v) descending (M-1(b)); the anchor of the order is the SEED, not the current centre. | placement.py |
| L-64 | (kept, applied to every member of the class, see L-72) A group is inserted simultaneously and order-free: every step scores all (member, empty seat) pairs, best gain wins, ties branch; then local search to fixed points branching on all tied best improving moves. | placement.py |
| L-65 | L = minimal with 6L+1 >= units; growing L prepends an empty outer seat to every leg (edges unchanged, empties toward the outer end, design L-09). | placement.py |
| L-66 | **SUPERSEDED by L-73** (the 8-branch / 64-state budget). Kept for the record. | placement.py |
| L-67 | Energy log record per placed unit: r0, E_Q, E_Q/r0 (None if r0 = 0), in seat order (centre, AXES, k ascending), plus the three-ratio verdict; query as a set (L-51). | placement.py |
| L-68 | (stop reasons are now "exhausted" / "budget" / "max_groups"; the centre is now a set `centres`) Placement.capacity = number of units in the last stable state (== size); stop reason and the breaking group (units, share, status) are recorded. The same unit set may sit in the cross with a different centre than the seed (recorded: centre, centre_moved). | placement.py |

## Placement T4b: tied arrangements grown as one state (verantyx/line3/placement.py)

Owner's decisions after the T4 measurement (binding): tied arrangements are kept together as ONE state and grown; arm assignment is not distinguished; no replacement with units outside the cross; growth order by shares with the original seed.

| id | decision | location |
|---|---|---|
| L-70 | A state is a CLASS: a set of arrangements of one key, closed under single seat swaps that keep the key, and with no member from which a single move improves the key ("stable" = this fixed point of the class). No member is picked by order; the representative `Placement.cross` is the first member in canonical sorted order (a label, not a winner). Arm assignment is quotiented out (L-61). | placement.py |
| L-71 | Settling: level by level every state moves by ALL its best improving single moves (all tied results kept); terminals of the best key are taken, their closure under equal-key swaps is the class; if any member of the closure has an improving move, those moves' results (from every member) are climbed again until none has. Branches ending at the same key are the same class (union). | placement.py `_settle` |
| L-72 | Growth: the next share-group (shares with the original seed, equal shares together) is inserted into EVERY member (L-64 greedy insertion, all tied placements kept), all results are settled (L-71), the best-key class is kept. A step cannot "break" any more; it can only hit the budget. The lone seed's class is built like any other (seed at the centre or at an arm end: 2 members at L=1). | placement.py `build_cross` |
| L-73 | Budget (explicit, never a silent cut; replaces L-66): `Budget(max_class, max_states)`; max_class = most arrangements in one class, max_states = most distinct states handled in one growth step (insertion + climbing + closure). Exceeding either = BUDGET: growth stops, the previous class is restored (N-05), `Placement.stop == "budget"`, `Step.reason` names the budget, and the budget used is stored in every Placement (to_json_obj "budget"). Defaults 1000 / 20000. | placement.py |
| L-74 | Verification: `verify_class` (independent of the search; geometry's swap/rotate/edges) checks one key, every member a fixed point against all single moves, and closure under equal-key moves. `verify_fixed_point` is applied to every member in the tests. | placement.py |
| L-75 | `observe_class` takes the energy log on every member of the class (members in canonical order); `observe` / `energy_log` use the representative. Which member (or all) the later stages read is not decided here (see open points). | placement.py |

### T4b measurement (S300, 240 evenly spaced seeds per tier, budget max_class 1000 / max_states 20000 / max_moves 400000; every sampled 1 in 8 verified with verify_class: 0 failures)

| tier | capacity min / median / mean / max | stop: exhausted / budget | which budget | class size median / mean / max | time |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RUN | 1 / 1 / 2.90 / 27 | 45 / 195 | max_moves 173, max_states 22 | 2 / 4.04 / 100 | 99.5 s |
| WORD | 1 / 1 / 3.80 / 32 | 12 / 228 | max_moves 221, max_states 7 | 2 / 4.47 / 354 | 77.3 s |
| CHAR | 1 / 14 / 25.17 / 140 | 1 / 239 | max_moves 235, max_states 4 | 2 / 1.66 / 8 | 179.9 s |

(T4 for comparison, strict tie rule: medians RUN 1, WORD 1, CHAR 3.) S3000 RUN/WORD were NOT run: at the S300 rate (0.3-0.4 s per seed) the 16193 / 13132 seeds need over an hour each, not under 10 minutes.

What the numbers mean: stability is now always reached (no step "breaks"); capacity is limited only by the explicit budget. A first group of many units with the same share (typical for RUN/WORD: 15-24 units with n(seed,v)=1) has an astronomically large tied class (units without mutual edges may float to any seat; the class is closed under those moves), so it cannot be enumerated and the step ends as BUDGET with the lone-seed class restored (class size 2 = seed at the centre or at an arm end; capacity 1). Symmetric toys grow (e.g. "A B C": capacity 3; "A B"/"B A": class of 2, centres A and B). Class sizes of kept states are reported per Step.class_size and Placement.class_size.


## Placement T4c: five budget levels, no empty centre (verantyx/line3/placement.py)

Owner's decisions after the T4b measurement (binding): (1) the search budget is selectable in five levels low..max, max is a compromise (finite); (2) an arrangement with an EMPTY CENTRE is not a state; (3) (query stage, NOT implemented) all members of a class are read and the one most stable after the query is adopted.

| id | decision | location |
|---|---|---|
| L-76 | Named levels `placement.LEVELS` / `LEVEL_ORDER` / `budget_level(name)` / `level_name(Budget)`, each 4x the previous in max_moves, max_states and max_class: low 63/1250/25000, mid-low 250/5000/100000, mid 1000/20000/400000 (== the T4b default `Budget()`), high 4000/80000/1600000, max 16000/320000/6400000 (max_class/max_states/max_moves). The status BUDGET stays explicit at every level; the Budget used is recorded in every Placement (to_json_obj "budget" unchanged: three numbers; the level name is derived by `level_name`). max is a compromise, not infinity. | placement.py |
| L-77 | A swap that would leave the centre empty is not a move; no arrangement with an empty centre exists in any class. The lone seed's class is the seed at the centre only (class size 1, was 2). `settle_class` rejects a start with an empty centre (ValueError); `verify_fixed_point` / `verify_class` ignore such swaps and `ClassReport.centres_nonempty` (part of `is_stable_class`) checks every member. | placement.py |

### T4c measurement (S300; experiments/line3/budget_curve/; same 240 evenly spaced seeds per tier as T4b = units[(i*n)//240])

max was NOT run on 240 seeds (it would exceed 15 min per tier: RUN ~24, WORD ~25, CHAR ~38 min extrapolated): RUN/WORD max on 48 seeds (every 5th of the 240), CHAR max on 20 (every 12th). Paired table = every level restricted to those seeds.

| tier | level | seeds | capacity min / median / mean / max | exhausted / budget | class size median / mean / max | wall s |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RUN | low | 240 | 1 / 1 / 2.10 / 16 | 32 / 208 | 1 / 1.08 / 11 | 6 |
| RUN | mid-low | 240 | 1 / 1 / 2.32 / 16 | 39 / 201 | 1 / 1.85 / 100 | 25 |
| RUN | mid | 240 | 1 / 1 / 2.92 / 16 | 56 / 184 | 1 / 10.8 / 807 | 102 |
| RUN | high | 240 | 1 / 1 / 4.02 / 16 | 82 / 158 | 1 / 26.8 / 945 | 433 |
| RUN | max | 48 | 1 / 1 / 5.10 / 28 | 18 / 30 | 1 / 131 / 5385 | 285 |
| WORD | low | 240 | 1 / 1 / 3.23 / 43 | 3 / 237 | 1 / 1.23 / 24 | 6 |
| WORD | mid-low | 240 | 1 / 1 / 3.58 / 44 | 11 / 229 | 1 / 2.47 / 91 | 21 |
| WORD | mid | 240 | 1 / 1 / 3.91 / 56 | 12 / 228 | 1 / 4.53 / 358 | 88 |
| WORD | high | 240 | 1 / 1 / 4.47 / 56 | 21 / 219 | 1 / 7.76 / 363 | 372 |
| WORD | max | 48 | 1 / 1 / 5.42 / 56 | 3 / 45 | 1 / 9.31 / 178 | 303 |
| CHAR | low | 240 | 1 / 9 / 14.77 / 70 | 1 / 239 | 1 / 1.28 / 7 | 13 |
| CHAR | mid-low | 240 | 1 / 11 / 18.81 / 89 | 1 / 239 | 1 / 1.44 / 16 | 44 |
| CHAR | mid | 240 | 1 / 11 / 21.65 / 108 | 1 / 239 | 1 / 1.41 / 16 | 152 |
| CHAR | high | 240 | 1 / 12.5 / 23.82 / 129 | 2 / 238 | 1 / 1.45 / 16 | 599 |
| CHAR | max | 20 | 1 / 16 / 20.40 / 99 | 1 / 19 | 1 / 1.55 / 3 | 188 |

Paired mean capacity (low, mid-low, mid, high, max; same seeds): RUN 2.29, 2.58, 3.04, 4.38, 5.10 (n=48); WORD 3.46, 3.98, 4.90, 5.42, 5.42 (n=48); CHAR 14.65, 18.85, 19.90, 20.40, 20.40 (n=20). Seeds whose capacity rose from the previous level: RUN 2, 3, 6, 3 of 48; WORD 3, 3, 2, 0 of 48; CHAR 4, 1, 1, 0 of 20. Ceiling (units sharing a sentence with the seed + 1; the capacity an unlimited budget gives, since a step never breaks): paired means RUN 26.0, WORD 77.6, CHAR 109.8. Every budget-stopped seed (RUN 30/30, WORD 45/45, CHAR 19/19 at max) stops at a step whose group has >= 2 equal-share units (median 17 / 23 / 45). Full table and trajectories: experiments/line3/budget_curve/results/curve.md.

Reading: a 4x budget costs ~4x time at every level (e.g. RUN 1.4, 5.0, 22, 83, 285 s on the paired seeds) but moves only a few seeds (0-6 of 48) up. WORD and CHAR show no gain from high to max (paired); RUN still gains (4.38 to 5.10) with the gain coming from 3 seeds. Mean class size grows steeply with budget in RUN (1.04 to 131; max 5385) while capacity grows slowly: the budget is spent on holding big classes of tied arrangements (RUN, WORD), not on adding units. CHAR classes stay small (mean 1.5): there the budget goes (max_moves) into the growth of an already large cross. Nothing here says anything about answer quality (needs the query stage). Compared with T4b at the same budget (= mid) after excluding empty centres: RUN mean 2.92 (T4b 2.90), WORD 3.91 (3.80), CHAR 21.65 (25.17, lower; not explained).

## Placement T4d: interchangeable units held as one (verantyx/line3/placement.py)

Owner's decision after the T4c measurement (binding): 「入れ替えても同じ語どうしは並べ方を数えない」. Stability values and the set of states do not change; only the counting.

| id | decision | location |
|---|---|---|
| L-90 | Interchangeable (twins): u, v of the candidate pool P (the seed and every unit with n(seed,.) > 0) are interchangeable iff the transposition (u v) is an automorphism of the edge weights w = (n, p) (outer -> inner) on P: w(u,z) == w(v,z) and w(z,u) == w(z,v) for all z in P \\ {u,v}, and w(u,v) == w(v,u). Then swapping u and v cannot change the I-04 key of any arrangement of units of P (hence of the cross). It is an equivalence relation ((u x) = (u v)(v x)(u v)). `find_twins` (bucketed by the multiset of a unit's weights, then pairwise `are_interchangeable`) gives {unit: smallest unit of its class}. | placement.py |
| L-91 | The search runs on LABELS (one label per twin class; two slots with the same label hold two different twins and use w(u,v)). The held class is a class of label arrangements (`Placement.members`, `class_size`); the EXPANDED class (= the T4c class) is every assignment of the placed units to the label slots, up to arm assignment (`expanded_members()`, `crosses()`, `twin_sets`), counted without enumeration by `expanded_count` / `expanded_size` (also per Step). `Placement.cross` is the first expansion of the first member; member labels are the smallest placed unit of the class; `centres` lists every unit that may sit at the centre. Budget numbers (max_class, max_states, max_moves) count quotient items; levels unchanged. `quotient=False` is the T4c search exactly. | placement.py |

### T4d measurement (same seeds/levels as T4c; experiments/line3/budget_curve/results/*_q.jsonl, curve_q.md, run_level_q.py, summarize_q.py)

Result: on S300, in all 3 tiers and all 5 levels (RUN/WORD/CHAR 240 seeds, max 48/48/20), NO seed has any interchangeable pair (twins = 0). Capacity, stop reason and class size are identical to T4c on every seed (expanded size == T4c class size on all 3 x 5 sets); the quotient saves nothing; wall time is higher by the twin detection (O(|P|^2) weight lookups per seed; runs were executed 11-12 in parallel on 10 cores, so absolute times are not comparable with T4c, ratio about 1.2x-4x). A check at the budget-breaking steps (low level, 34 / 39 / 40 budget-stopped seeds of 40 per tier RUN / WORD / CHAR) with the weaker, step-local definition (invariance only on the units already in the cross plus the breaking group) also found no twin. Reason: units that share a sentence with the seed differ in word-order weights p (and in n) towards the other pool units, so no transposition is an automorphism; the huge T4c classes come from DIFFERENT arrangements whose key sums happen to coincide, not from swapping equivalent units. The quotient is proved only on toys that have genuine twins (e.g. "A B"/"B A": held 1, expanded 2; "X A".."X H": held 1 class, 840 expanded arrangements, T4c cannot count it under the same budget) and on S300 seeds (expansion == T4c class where T4c fits).

## Query cycle T5 (verantyx/line3/cycle.py)

None of these is an owner decision. M-2 (a) and M-5 (c) are the design's RECOMMENDED defaults, used because the owner's answers (M-2: "全体のノードの数など" ; M-5: the auditor's recommendation) leave the detail open; both are listed again under OPEN POINTS.

| id | decision | location |
|---|---|---|
| L-100 | Search state = the world view as a flat tuple (centre, arm0 k=0..L-1, ...) with identity orientation (the world-visible legs are all a section sees). A rotation permutes the six legs by the table taken from `geometry.rotate` (`rotation_tables`); a swap exchanges two flat entries (same pair order as `geometry.moves_swap`). | cycle.py |
| L-101 | Move key (I-12) = (-(distinct votes - 1), working sections, F(unit at the centre)). Votes = the units the working sections point to + the edge unit R2 + the placement unit R3; a ratio that points nowhere is its own distinct vote, so disagreements == 0 <=> the three ratios AGREE (I-11). F = `energy.edge_flow` under E_Q. A move is accepted iff its key is strictly greater; all tied best moves are branches (design 4.5). | cycle.py |
| L-102 | Exact integers: N*E_Q(u) = n(u) + sum n(q,u); F and B scaled by D = lcm of all n(u) (so F*N*D, B*D are ints) and compared as ints. Differentially tested equal (as Fractions) to `energy.three_ratios` / `edge_flow` (`slow_key`). | cycle.py, tests |
| L-103 | The RESULT of a state = its agreed unit iff the three ratios agree AND N-03 holds, else None. Stability inv (I-15, newest decision) = (moves whose resulting state differs and whose result equals the current one) / (moves whose resulting state differs); moves that leave the state identical (rotations of identical legs, swaps of equal cells) are not counted (otherwise a lone seed would be 100% stable); swaps that would empty the centre are not moves (L-77). No move at all: inv = 1. | cycle.py |
| L-104 | N-03 grounding: a section counts as "with a query unit attached" iff its query unit is in the space (n >= 1). A query unit absent from the space is neither error nor evidence (L-52). If no query unit of the first layer is in the space the verdict is UNKNOWN_NO_EVIDENCE. | cycle.py |
| L-105 | Search budget per start `QueryBudget(max_states, max_ends)`: states expanded (each = one scan of all moves) and fixed points kept. Default 512 / 64 (the design L-05 value 64 / 8 turned about a third of RUN class members into NO_FIXED_POINT, see measurement below). Exceeded -> that start is NO_FIXED_POINT with counts, never silent. Expansion order is by sorted state (a label); the set of fixed points does not depend on it unless the budget is hit, and then the whole start is NO_FIXED_POINT. | cycle.py |
| L-106 | Outcome of one class member: CANDIDATE (all fixed points of its tied branches give the same grounded answer; stability = the minimum over them), NONE (no answer; typed by the fixed points' status or "ungrounded" = agreed without N-03), AMBIGUOUS (tied branches end with different results, including answer vs none: abstain, no order winner), NO_FIXED_POINT. | cycle.py |
| L-107 | Choosing among the members of a class: flag `member_rule`. `stable_any` (default; literal "adopt the most stable member", over members that reached a fixed point whether or not they answer) or `answering_only`. Equal best stability: every answering member at that stability enters the question's pool (a list). Both verdicts are always computed; the other rule's verdict is reported as `alt_member_rule_verdict`. | cycle.py |
| L-108 | Question level (per tier): pool = adopted candidates; the best stability wins; one unit -> ANSWER; several units at the best stability -> CHOICE (list, no cap, I-14/N-20); the same unit from several crosses is one answer. Without a candidate: AMBIGUOUS if any member is ambiguous, else the most specific failure seen in this reporting precedence (not a winner choice): NO_FIXED_POINT, RATIO_DISAGREEMENT, SECTION_DISAGREEMENT, NO_EVIDENCE. | cycle.py |
| L-109 | Energy scope: the first layer's units (default `first_layer`, M-5(c)) or all units (`whole`). A section without an attached unit reads with the set of that scope (I-07). | cycle.py |
| L-110 | A tier is read on its own (I-16); combining tiers is T7. | cycle.py |
| L-111 | Class members are read as the expanded class (`placement.crosses()` order: canonical), each with its legs in canonical order; `member_cap` (default None = all) is reported (members read / total). | cycle.py |
| L-112 | Neighbours of a state are evaluated incrementally (only the sections of changed legs, and the cells around the two swapped seats; rotations keep F and B); differentially tested equal to the full evaluation. | cycle.py, tests |
| L-113 | M-2(a) read order for a reduced read (`amount` = number of crosses): group 1 = crosses of query units; group 2 = crosses holding a placed unit that shares a sentence with a query unit; group 3 = the rest; inside a group by N*E_Q(seed) descending; equal values are read together or not at all ("まとめて読まない" side, a tie is never split), reading stops at the first group that does not fit. A reduced read is `partial_read = {read, unread, total, amount, tied_group_not_split}`. The default amount is the whole space. | cycle.py |
| L-114 | `answer` = verdict, units, stability, partial_read, trace (tier, seed, member, end state, key); `thought` = query layers (incl. inner layers pending), read counts and groups, search counts, budget, stacking marker (N-11), energy log of the adopted states (design L-20), state version (0: T5 changes no state), digest of the full search state. The full search state (`search_state_obj`, format line3.search_state.v1, per cross / member start, end states, stability) is available as `TierResult.state` for T10/T11 to store (M-3); nothing is stored by T5. Wall time is kept out of the output bytes. | cycle.py |

### T5 measurements

(see experiments/line3/t5/ ; budget experiment on S300 RUN, every 20th unit, 520 class members: 64/8 gives candidate 379 / none 104 / ambiguous 32 / no_fixed_point 257 (239 by max_ends); 512/64 gives 498 / 105 / 151 / 18; 4096/512 gives 498 / 105 / 169 / 0. So the budget changes results; the larger the budget the more tied branches are followed and the more starts become AMBIGUOUS rather than NO_FIXED_POINT.)

90-question run on S300 (experiments/line3/t5/results/*.jsonl, summarize.py; placement level mid; QueryBudget 64/8; 9 worker processes on a loaded 10-core machine; NO grading): RUN all 90: ANSWER 56 / CHOICE 19 / UNKNOWN_NO_EVIDENCE 15 (wall 4749 s; per question min/median/max 408/474/538 s); WORD 30 questions (every 3rd): ANSWER 30 (1094 s wall; 224/288/334 s per question); CHAR 10 questions (every 9th): ANSWER 10 (1204 s wall; 533/731/761 s). All ANSWERs are a single particle character/word (は). 90/90 RUN questions, 30/30 WORD and 10/10 CHAR have at least one start that hit the search budget (N-11 stacking marker). S3000 was not run (the placements alone need about 2 CPU hours, and a question about 10x the S300 cost).

## Read-out T6 (verantyx/line3/readout.py, trace_check.py)

None of these is an owner decision. The owner's binding points used: decision 7 (read the words out in order along the section -> centre paths, adopt the most stable candidate or present a choice), I-13, N-13, I-14, I-15, N-14, N-20, M-4 (the recommended (a), which the owner accepted), and the T5-measurement answer "read the paths first".

| id | decision | location |
|---|---|---|
| L-120 | Section path = the cells a working section walks from the outer end of a leg it sees toward the centre, exactly as `cycle.Reader` walks them (edge evidenced n > 0, section's energy not dropping), up to and including the unit it points to (d_s). The query unit attached beyond the outer end is NOT a path word (it is the question, not the structure). | readout.py |
| L-121 | A section sees up to three legs (window 1: previous, own, next arm). Its path = the legs whose walk ends at the unit the section points to, read in ring order, each leg outer -> inner, the shared end unit written once at the end. Picking one leg would be an order tie-break; the legs each stay a separate "segment" for the trace. | readout.py |
| L-122 | Only working sections (those that point to a unit) have a path; a state without one yields no sentence (counted as `states_without_path`). Sentence = path words joined with no separator (Japanese data). | readout.py |
| L-123 | A candidate = one (state, ordering of its k whole section paths) (I-13, N-13). `orderings` counts them exactly (k! per state, summed over the adopted states, k <= 6 so <= 720 per state). The LIST is over distinct texts: orderings or states that read out the same words are one sentence that keeps all its origins (state index, order). Orderings are generated in lexicographic order of section index (a label, never a winner). | readout.py |
| L-124 | Stability of a sentence = the best stability among its origins (stability of a candidate = inv of its state, I-15, exact Fraction); the list = every sentence at the best stability; sentences below are counted (`below_best_stability`), not listed. | readout.py |
| L-125 | Verdicts: ANSWER (one sentence at the best stability: adopt), CHOICE (several: the list, sorted by text = a label), UNKNOWN_NO_PATH (no sentence). | readout.py |
| L-126 | "Too many" flag = list size > `too_many` (default 20, design L-21): a display value only; the list is never shortened and the counts (states, orderings, distinct sentences, listed) are in `thought`. | readout.py |
| L-127 | Intake of the user's choice (M-4 (a)): `choose(readout, index_or_text)` (anything not on the list is refused), `adopt(readout)` for the automatic ANSWER; both return an `Adoption` whose `memory_record()` is a design-L-19 `memory_answer` record (source `auto` / `user_choice`, question, answer, words, stability, origins, offered, too_many, choice_index, `base_changed: false`). Nothing is stored or changed here (memory = T11). | readout.py |
| L-128 | The adopted states the read-out takes = the cycle's adopted candidates at the best stability (`readout.states_from_result` / `states_from_answer_obj` from T5's stored `answer.trace`: end state flat, seed, member, stability). T5's stored results hold what is needed; T5 is not re-run. | readout.py |
| L-129 | Trace check (independent of the Reader): for every word occurrence of every listed sentence: (a) the state holds the word at the named seat, (b) the word is a unit of the tier with a sentence that has it (first sid kept), (c) unless it opens a leg, its leg predecessor is a neighbouring seat and some sentence holds both (first edge sid kept), (d) the sentence text/words are exactly the section paths in the stated order for every origin. Report = words traced / checked as an exact Fraction. | trace_check.py |
| L-130 | Experiment-side only (not in the module): the "function-word" classification used to count function-only paths = every UniDic token of the unit is a particle / auxiliary / conjunction / adnominal / pronoun / adverb / symbol / light verb (pos2 非自立可能), plus a few fixed compounds (における, として, ...); CHAR tier: a hiragana character. `experiments/line3/t6/run_readout.py` `func_pos`. | experiments/line3/t6 |
| L-131 | Grading core for the read-out: the first query unit attached to a working section that exists in the space (n >= 1); correct needs a gold word in the adopted sentence and that core in the subject (T0 grader, 7.2). | experiments/line3/t6/run_readout.py |

## Diagnostic variants T6v (options; defaults unchanged)

Owner's choice after the T6 grading (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md, 「T6 の採点後の決定」): test three query-effect changes as OPTIONS, the original decisions stay the default. None of these is an owner decision about the product; they are diagnostics. V123 = all three together (the auditor's addition). Proof that defaults are byte-identical: tests/line3/test_variants.py (golden hashes made with the code of commit 8b92ec7).

| id | decision | location |
|---|---|---|
| L-140 | V1 `ask_tier(read_rule="query_crosses")` / `plan_read(query_crosses_only=True)`: read only the crosses whose unit set (centre, any seat, twin sets included) contains at least one unit of the query's first layer (`ctx.energy_units`, the units the energies read). A query unit that is not in the space is in no cross. No cross holds one: nothing is read and the verdict is UNKNOWN_NO_EVIDENCE. The read is marked partial (`partial_read`), the per-cross reading is the same function as the whole read (tested equal member by member). Not combinable with `amount`. | cycle.py |
| L-141 | V2 `build_space(unit_filter=pred)` / `build_tier(..., unit_filter)` (pred may be a {tier: pred} mapping): units for which pred is True are removed from every sentence's unit list (no posting, no word-order slot, so the neighbours of a removed unit become adjacent in the order); sentences stay and count in N. `ask_tier(unit_filter=pred)` also drops the filtered units from the QUESTION (a function / question word is not a query unit either). The predicate used in the experiment is T6's L-130 `func_pos`, imported (every UniDic token of the unit is a particle / auxiliary / conjunction / adnominal / pronoun-interrogative / adverb / symbol / light verb, plus the fixed compounds; RUN and WORD tiers). That also removes question words (どこ, 何, ...) and mixed strings such as 「はどこにありますか」 (all tokens function). `Space.append` of a filtered space is not supported. Placements of the V2 space are rebuilt (experiments/line3/t6v/precompute_v2.py, level mid). | space.py, cycle.py, t6v |
| L-142 | V3 `ask_tier(state_rule="query_share")`: candidate states = every end state (fixed point) of every answering member (kind CANDIDATE, an answer grounded by N-03) of every cross read; the same flat state reached from several crosses is one candidate. Score = number of sentences that hold a unit of the state (any seat) AND a unit of the query's first layer (exact integer; |union of the state's postings AND union of the query units' postings|, M-1(c) style). The adopted states are all those at the maximum score (ties kept: a list, no order winner, N-14). Verdict: one answer unit -> ANSWER, several -> CHOICE, no candidate -> the L-108 failure typing. Reported stability = the largest stability among the adopted states (information only; it no longer selects). `thought.variant` records read_rule, state_rule, filter, the query after filtering, the state count / best share / tied states. | cycle.py |
| L-143 | V3 read-out: `read_out(..., by_stability=False)` lists the sentences of every given state (no "best stability only" cut, since the states were chosen by another rule); `below_best` is 0. Default True = L-124 unchanged. | readout.py |
| L-144 | Experiment: the variants of one family share the per-question crosses they read through a memo of `read_cross` (deterministic, a memo hit equals a fresh read): family F1 (original space: baseline re-run check, V1, V3), F2 (V2 space: V2, V123). The baseline re-run is compared with the stored T5 answer (verdict, units, adopted-state trace). | experiments/line3/t6v/run_family.py |
| L-145 | Grading of every variant = T6 (experiments/line3/grade.py): strict uses the read-out verdict (ANSWER only for one sentence; a list = abstention), core = the first query unit attached to a working section path that exists in the space; diagnostics (gold in some sentence / some single path / union of path words), distinct content-word sets across questions (content = not L-130 function), share of function-only section paths (the T6 metric over the first 3 adopted states of a question, and over all states for the variants). | experiments/line3/t6v/summarize.py |

### T6v measurement (S300, mid placement, search budget 64/8, 90 fixed questions; experiments/line3/t6v/results/summary.md)

RUN, 90 questions unless noted. Baseline re-run through the new code equals the stored T5 answer (verdict, units, adopted-state trace) on 90/90. Strict (adopted read-out candidate, list = abstention): 0 correct / 0 wrong / 60 abstain for baseline, V1, V2 (33 q), V3, V123 (all read-outs are lists: I-13 + N-13 give k! orderings). Gold in some single section path (60 answerable): baseline 1, V1 4 (only 15 of 60 have an adopted state), V2 3 of 24 (33-question subset), V3 23, V123 28 (33 have an adopted state; median 720 listed sentences). Chance control (gold of question i against the read-out of question j != i): V3 0.36 vs matched 0.38 (no question dependence; its lists hold ~1.9M sentences), V2 0.15 vs 0.12, V123 0.01 vs 0.85. Distinct content-word sets across questions: baseline 2 (75 q), V1 11 (30 q), V2 3 (28 q), V3 23 (75 q), V123 34 (42 q). Function-only section paths (first 3 states): baseline 74.7%, V1 76.1%, V2 0%, V3 0.8%, V123 0%. WORD tier V123 (30 q): gold in a path 3 of 20, 12 distinct content-word sets (baseline 3). V2 RUN was run on 33 of 90 questions only (about 4.5 times as many class members as the original space: 84512 vs 18587; about 1700 s CPU per question under load): the 30 questions of every 3rd id first, then 3 more; the rest was not run.

## Other local labels in the design (docs/LINE3_DESIGN.md §8.2)

L-01 to L-14, L-19 (kind; also used as L-40 in space.py), L-20 (observation record; a different item from geometry L-20) and L-21 to L-23 are design defaults, not yet recorded by code. Note the number clash: design L-19/L-20 (kind, observation record) versus geometry L-19/L-20 (serialisation, swap identity).

## OPEN POINTS (owner questions; behaviour not changed)

- RUN tier: question words are not separate units (`units_run('半田岩はどこにありますか')` gives `['半田岩', 'はどこにありますか']`), unlike the WORD and CHAR tiers. The T1 docstring now states this; whether the RUN tier should split them is the owner's decision.
- T4b (class growth): (a) With the owner's rule, "stable" is always reachable, so capacity is decided only by the budget; for groups of many equal-share units the full tied class cannot be enumerated (RUN/WORD median capacity is still 1, now because of BUDGET, not because of a tie). Whether to (i) raise the budget, (ii) represent the class implicitly (e.g. also treat arrangements that differ only by permuting interchangeable units - same share and same pair weights to every cross member - as one arrangement), or (iii) restrict the class to arrangements where every placed unit has a non-zero edge, is a meaning-level choice left to the owner. (b) RESOLVED by the owner (T4c, L-77): empty centre is not an arrangement. (c) Owner: read all members, adopt the one most stable after the query (query stage; the energy log is still taken on the representative / observe_class until then). (d) The class members have different centres, so "the centre" is a set (I-02, centres). 
- T4 placement (see report): (1) leg-to-leg arrangement: the I-04 key is identical for all 30 rotation classes of leg permutations, so the stability definition does not fix which arm holds which leg; sections (I-09 windows) do see adjacency. Left undetermined (L-61). (2) Budget values (L-66) change the capacity of some seeds (CHAR, S300: 2 of 233 sampled). (3) Design 3.4 "pool の外の単位とも" replacement not implemented (L-60). (4) Order anchor of M-1(b) is the fixed seed although the search may move the centre (L-63/L-68). (5) A lone seed is declared stable by definition (L-63); with moves into empty seats it has equal-key alternatives.

- T4c: (a) Per-tier reachability: even at max, 62-95% of RUN/WORD/CHAR seeds (30/48, 45/48, 19/20) stop by BUDGET with a capacity far below the ceiling (candidates+1); whether a different representation of the tied group (e.g. interchangeable units as one arrangement, option (ii) above) is wanted, or the ceiling-limited small capacity is acceptable, is the owner's choice. (b) CHAR mid capacity fell from T4b (mean 25.17 to 21.65) after the empty-centre exclusion at identical budget; the cause is not analysed. (c) The "stable after the query" comparison for owner point (3) needs a definition of query stability over a class (which energy / ratio; ties among members) - left to the query stage. (d) Default `Budget()` is "mid"; which level is the product default is undecided.

- T4d: (a) The strict interchangeability (L-90) never occurs in S300, so the budget problem of T4c is unchanged (62-95% BUDGET at max). The tied classes are made of arrangements that are different and merely have equal key sums. Whether to (i) accept this, (ii) define a weaker equivalence that is still exact for the key, e.g. arrangements equal after ignoring edges that carry (0,0) weight, or arrangements with the same multiset of edge weights (a coarser class: NOT the same set of states, owner decision), or (iii) restrict the class to arrangements where every placed unit has a non-zero edge, is a meaning-level choice. (b) Twin classes are computed over the whole candidate pool (strict, as specified); a step-local definition (twins only relative to units already placed) would be more permissive but needs splitting a held label when a later group distinguishes its members; it also found no twin on the measured budget-stopped steps. (c) Which of the twins is shown as the label of a class (smallest placed unit) is a label only. (d) Query stage: reading "all members" means reading the expansion; with twins held as one this can be done per quotient member and mapped to units; not implemented.

- T5 (query cycle) open points, nothing below is decided by the code beyond the flagged default:
  (1) M-2(a): amount counted in crosses; tied equal-E_Q groups are never split (reading stops before a group that does not fit); the three groups (query-unit crosses / crosses holding a unit that shares a sentence with a query unit / the rest) are the design's recommended reading. The owner's option "compress lower-structure results vs keep granularity" is not specified by the T5 row (it needs layers): NOT implemented, belongs to T8.
  (2) M-5(c): only the first query layer (6 units) enters the energies and sections; units from the 7th on (for WORD/CHAR most question words such as "ます か") are only recorded as inner layers. Flag `scope="whole"` reads all units with the same sections; which is meant is the owner's choice.
  (3) Leg-to-arm assignment: placement does not distinguish which arm holds which leg (T4b), but at query time the query units sit on fixed world sections, so the assignment matters. Each class member is read with its legs in canonical order; other assignments are reachable only through the 24 rotations and the seat swaps. Reading all assignments (up to 720 per member) is not done.
  (4) I-12 key: the exact meaning of "指す先の種類数 − 1" when a ratio points nowhere (L-101: its own vote) and of "エネルギーが中心へ" (F of the unit at the centre, not of the agreed unit) is a reading.
  (5) Stability inv (L-103) counts only moves that change the state; its "result" is the grounded agreed unit. Under this definition a member that never answers can be perfectly stable; the member choice (L-107) therefore has two rules and the run reports both (identical on S300 for every verdict count).
  (6) Ties: tied best moves branch; end states that differ in result (even answer vs no answer) are AMBIGUOUS, not ranked by key. About 5 percent of RUN class members end AMBIGUOUS at 64/8 (up to about a third at larger budgets). The search budget changes results (see measurement); which budget is the default is undecided.
  (7) Question-level failure typing (L-108 precedence) and the integration of the three tiers (I-16) are not decided; T5 reports per tier.
  (8) N-11: only "no fixed point within the budget" is marked as a place where stacking would occur; the design gives no threshold for "stability collapses" in terms of inv.
  (9) Particle answers: every ANSWER in the run is は (owner: "そのまま組んで測る"); not changed.
  (10) Cost: the whole-space read is about 400-540 s per RUN question in pure Python (incremental evaluation already in); the T5b skip and T5c speed-ups are separate tickets.

- T6 (read-out) open points, nothing below is decided by the code beyond the flagged default:
  (1) Section path with several legs (L-121): a section sees up to three legs, and in the S300 states two or three of them usually end at the same unit. The code reads all legs that reach the pointed unit in ring order (so e.g. section 4 reads "であるのをは"). Alternatives: read only the section's own arm; read each leg as its own path (then more than 6 items would be ordered, N-13's 720 bound would not hold); read the longest.
  (2) Whether the query unit sitting at the outer end belongs to the read path (L-120: not). Including it would put the question words in the answer text.
  (3) Sentence = joined words. Whether the whole path including the repeated end unit is written per section (the code does: the centre は is written k times), or the shared centre once at the end, is a meaning choice; N-13's "whole section paths" was read literally.
  (4) What "candidate" is when two orderings read out identical text (L-123): counted as orderings (k!), listed as one sentence. With k = 6 sections the list is always large (CHOICE): the owner already knew that I-13 + I-15 make every multi-section read-out a list; the measured sizes are in the report (typically 720 per state, 9360 for 13 tied states on RUN).
  (5) All tied adopted states of a question (up to 13 on RUN) are read out and their sentences pooled; the alternative reading is one "the" stable state (not defined when the cycle adopts several seeds at one stability).
  (6) Stability of a sentence is the stability of the state and is the same for all k! orderings; there is no finer stability between orderings, so ordering itself can never produce a single adopted sentence (N-14: "そのまま組んで影響を見る").
  (7) The "too many" limit (20) is only a display value (design L-21); which number the owner wants is open.
  (8) Observation: the read-out words depend on the adopted states, which on S300 are the same few states for almost every question (RUN: 2 distinct content-word sets over 75 read-out questions). Whether the question should shape which paths are read (e.g. read only the sections whose attached query unit is grounded) is open.

- T6v (diagnostic variants) open points, nothing below is decided by the code beyond the flagged option:
  (1) V3: "candidate states" = fixed points of answering members; whether non-answering fixed points (which also have working sections and paths) belong to the pool, and whether the sharing score should be normalised by the state's own sentence count (the raw count favours states made of frequent units: with function words kept it favours hubs) is not decided.
  (2) V3: with ties kept, the pool is huge (median 5310 states, about 1.9M listed sentences on RUN): the lists then contain the gold at the chance rate. A tie rule (e.g. also by stability, then list) is the owner's.
  (3) V1: "contains at least one query unit" counts any seat of the placed cross; 60 of 90 questions then have no adopted state (ratio disagreement / no fixed point / no evidence) because only the crosses of the question's units are read. Whether crosses sharing a sentence with a query unit (design M-2 group 2) should also be read is open.
  (4) V2: the function-word rule is T6's L-130 (UniDic POS), applied also to question words (どこ, 何, ...) and to mixed RUN strings such as 「はどこにありますか」; it removes only 142 of 2175 RUN units but ~4.5 times as many class members appear in the placements (more tied arrangements without the function-word hubs). Whether the rule is the owner's meaning of "助詞を外す" (e.g. keep copula / verbs of 非自立可能) is open. Removing units makes former neighbours adjacent in the word order (L-141): not asked, not decided.
  (5) Strict 0 remains for every variant: I-13 + N-13 (every ordering of 6 section paths is a candidate) and I-15 (one stability per state) make the read-out a list whenever the paths differ; no variant changes that. The cycle's own unit answer (not a read-out) is correct on 3 of 60 for V123 (RUN), 0 for the others.
  (6) Cost: V2 needs its own placements and a whole-space read at about 4.5 times the class members; the full 90 questions were not run.

## T6w: V1 + V2 + V3 become the defaults; the answer is the agreed centre plus the section-path words

Owner (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md, 「T6v の測定後の決定」): all three variants become the new defaults (I-08, I-22 and the way the adopted states are chosen change; the original decisions and the measurements stay on record), and when the sections agree the answer is the agreed centre plus the words of each path (no sentence is composed, nothing reordered).

| id | decision | location |
|---|---|---|
| L-150 | New defaults. `ask_tier(read_rule=None -> "query_crosses", state_rule="query_share", unit_filter="default")`; `build_space/build_tier/build_from_jsonl(unit_filter="default")`. "default" = the function-word rule of funcwords.py (RUN / WORD / CHAR); `None` = keep every unit (the old I-22 space); a predicate or a {tier: predicate} mapping = own rule. The old behaviour is the explicit options `read_rule="whole"`, `state_rule="stability"`, `unit_filter=None`, read-out `form="sentences"`; with them every output is byte-identical to the T6v-era code (GOLDEN hashes in tests/line3/test_variants.py). An explicit `amount` (M-2) selects `read_rule="whole"` when `read_rule` is not given (the amount is an ordered partial read of the whole space). `thought.variant` is recorded whenever the effective options differ from the old behaviour (so always with the new defaults). `python -m verantyx.line3.space PATH --keep-function-words` prints the old space counts. S300 default space: RUN 2033, WORD 2278, CHAR 1094 units (old: 2175 / 2444 / 1164). | cycle.py, space.py |
| L-151 | funcwords.py = the T6 rule L-130 moved into the module (UniDic via fugashi: every token of the unit is 助詞 / 助動詞 / 接続詞 / 連体詞 / 代名詞 / 補助記号 / 感動詞 / 接頭辞 / 副詞 or pos2 非自立可能; fixed compounds にて; CHAR: a hiragana character). `experiments/line3/t6/run_readout.py` imports it. A space built with a filter remembers it (`TierSpace.unit_filter`, not part of equality) and `append` applies the same filter, so appending equals building from scratch (tested). | funcwords.py, space.py |
| L-152 | Default answer form `PathAnswer` (`readout.read_out(..., form="centre_paths")`, `read_answer`, `read_out_result`, `read_out_stored`): per adopted state an ITEM = (centre, paths). centre = the unit the working sections agreed on (the cycle's answer unit of the state = the end unit of every path); paths = every working section in section order, each the ordered word list as read (L-120 / L-121), with the section index and the attached query unit as labels. No sentence is built, no ordering enumerated (the k! of L-123 is gone from this form). Items are DISTINCT by (centre, word lists of the paths in section order): adopted states that read out the same are one item (all origin states kept); items are listed sorted by (centre, words) (a label, no winner); stability of an item = the best among its origin states (information). The T6 sentence read-out stays as `form="sentences"` (L-120..L-127 unchanged). | readout.py |
| L-153 | Verdict of the default form: ANSWER = exactly one item; CHOICE = a list of several items (only when states genuinely differ in centre or in a path's words; never shortened, N-20; `too_many` = list size > 20 is a display flag, L-126); UNKNOWN_NO_PATH = no adopted state has a working section path. No adopted state at all is the cycle's own verdict (UNKNOWN_*, AMBIGUOUS) and no read-out exists. `answer_obj()` carries `centre` / `paths` for ANSWER and all `items` always. | readout.py |
| L-154 | Intake for the default form: `adopt_item(answer)` (single item, source auto) and `choose_item(answer, index)` (user's choice by index; anything else refused) give an `AnswerAdoption` whose `memory_record()` is a `memory_answer` record {centre, paths, source, offered, choice_index, base_changed False}; nothing is stored here (T11). | readout.py |
| L-155 | Trace of the default form: `trace_check.trace_answer` (what `trace_readout` dispatches to for a `PathAnswer`): every word of every path of every item must (a) sit at its seat in the origin state, (b) be a unit of the tier, (c) be joined to the previous word of its leg by an edge some sentence evidences; every origin state of an item must read exactly the item's words; every path must end at the centre and the centre must be a unit of the tier. 100 % required. | trace_check.py |
| L-156 | Experiment grading of T6w (experiments/line3/t6w/summarize.py, grader = experiments/line3/grade.py): rule A answer string = the agreed centre of the single item; rule B answer string = the section-path texts (each path's words joined, paths separated by a line break so a gold string cannot span two paths), correct iff a gold string occurs in them (and, as the T0 grader requires, the core = the first query unit attached to a working section path that exists in the space is in the subject; the count without that check is reported too). A list of differing items, UNKNOWN_*, AMBIGUOUS, no adopted state = abstention; an unanswerable question (fict / attr) answered = wrong. Question dependence = gold of question i against the path texts of question j. Run: experiments/line3/t6w/run_all.py (all 90 questions, S300 RUN, V2 placements of T6v reused, 9 worker processes). | experiments/line3/t6w |

### T6w open points (meaning-level, not decided by the code)
  (1) Item identity: items are equal iff the centre and the word lists of the paths agree in section order. Two states that read the same words on rotated sections are different items; whether the section order should be canonicalised (or paths treated as a set) is not decided. A list of up to 1056 items occurs (S300 RUN, median list 159.5) because the states differ only in which words a section reads.
  (2) Centre: the agreed centre is the unit all sections point to (the cycle's answer unit). It is very often a generic category word (景勝地, 道路, ギリシア, 県) that is in the subject's sentences but is not what the question asks for; grading rule A therefore scores 2 of 60 although some path holds the gold in 28 of the 33 answerable questions that have a read-out (60 answerable). Whether the centre should be what is answered at all, or only the paths, is the owner's.
  (3) Abstention: 48 of 90 questions have no adopted state under V1 (only crosses holding a query unit are read: 33 UNKNOWN_NO_EVIDENCE, 13 UNKNOWN_NO_FIXED_POINT, 2 AMBIGUOUS), and 18 more answer as a list. How a list / no-answer should be presented to the user (the intake of L-154 takes a choice by index) and whether crosses that share a sentence with a query unit (M-2 group 2) should be read as well are open (carried from T6v (3)).
  (4) Rule B counts the gold string anywhere in the path words, including words that are the question's own units; the matched/mismatched control (question dependence) is the evidence that the gold is there because of the question, but a path that merely contains the subject's neighbouring words can hold the gold without "answering".
  (5) V2's function-word rule (L-151) is applied to the question too (どこ, 何 are removed). Whether it is the owner's meaning of "助詞を外す" and what the neighbours of a removed unit should become (they become adjacent in the word order, L-141) remains open (T6v (4)).
  (6) The default `ask_tier` now drops function words from `units=` passed explicitly as well (the question's first layer); a caller that wants a unit kept must pass `unit_filter=None` or its own predicate.
  (7) Placements: the default space has its own placements (one cross per unit of the filtered space); the T5 placements of the old space cannot be reused with the new defaults (the S300 RUN level-mid placements of the default space are the T6v V2 ones, scratch: placements_V2_S300_RUN_mid.pkl, cost ~4.5x the old class members).

## T6x (2026-10-07): M-2 group 2 as a read rule, capacity diagnosis, official answer object
| ID | Decision | Where |
|---|---|---|
| L-160 | Official answer object (owner: "経路の語を答えとし、中心は参考として付ける"; rule B is the official grading). `PathAnswer.answer_obj()["form"] == "path_words"`; for ANSWER `answer = {path_words: [text of each section path], source_sids: [...], reference_centre: centre}`; `centre`, `paths`, `items`, `listed`, `too_many`, `verdict` keep their T6w meaning and values. Each path of an item carries `edges` = the steps of its walk (leg by leg, outer -> inner; the step into the centre included): (from, to, sids) with from = the attached query unit for the first step of a leg of an attached section and the previous word otherwise; sids = every sentence of the tier holding both units. A section without an attached query unit has no opening step. Each item has `source_sids` (sorted union); the answer has `sentences` = {sid: the tier's unit list of that sentence joined} for every sid used (a tier keeps unit lists only: with the function-word filter the shown text lacks those words; the original text is sentence `sid` of the input). Items stay distinct by (centre, word lists) only; edges are derived from the words and legs and are not part of the key. `thought_obj()`, the cycle result and every other byte are unchanged (tested). `AnswerAdoption.memory_record()` is not changed (still form "centre_paths", centre + paths). | readout.py |
| L-161 | `ask_tier(read_rule="query_share_crosses")` (option, NOT the default; default stays `query_crosses`): V1 crosses (hold a query unit) plus the crosses holding a unit u != query units with n(x, u) > 0 for some query unit x of the first layer (shares >= 1 sentence; M-2 group 2). One read set, marked partial if any cross is unread; read plan groups are `contains_query_unit` and `shares_sentence_with_query_unit`; not combinable with `amount`; adoption by V3 as before. The only change of the code path is the extra crosses in the read set (the default is byte-identical, tested against recorded hashes). | cycle.py |
| L-162 | Capacity diagnosis (experiments/line3/t6x/diag_capacity.py; no module change): for a question with no adopted state, Q = the first-layer query units after the filter; (i) no cross of the space holds a Q unit; (ii) the only crosses holding a Q unit are the Q unit's own seed cross with capacity 1; (iii) other crosses hold Q units (the cycle ended without a fixed point / ambiguous). A cross that stopped by `exhausted` cannot grow with any budget; only a cross with stop == "budget" is rebuilt at high and max (placement.build_cross), put in the store in place of the mid one, and the question is asked again with the same rule. | experiments/line3/t6x |

### T6x open points (meaning-level, not decided by the code)
  (1) What "source sentence" means: here the sentences that hold both ends of a step of the walk (the evidence the walk used). A path word has no single source sentence; the answer lists all of them per step (may be long, never shortened). Whether the answer should show one (e.g. the first / the most shared) is the owner's.
  (2) The text of a source sentence is the unit list of the filtered tier, not the original sentence (function words are removed by V2).
  (3) Group-2 crosses are read as full crosses (all members, same budget); whether crosses that merely share a sentence with a query unit should be read with the same weight as crosses holding the query unit, or only as fall-back when V1 is empty, is not decided (the experiment reports both effects).
  (4) `AnswerAdoption.memory_record()` still stores the centre and the paths; whether the memory record should store the source sids too is for T11.


## T6y (2026-10-07): merge of section-assignment-only differences, on-demand budget raise
| ID | Decision | Where |
|---|---|---|
| L-170 | `read_answer/read_out/read_out_result(merge_sections=True)` (option, NOT the default; owner (a): items that differ ONLY by which section reads which words are one item, same as "arm assignment not distinguished"). Item key = (centre, the sorted multiset of the section paths' word sequences). The first state (state order) keeps its paths, edges, sources; `origins` = every state of the merged items, `stability` = the best. `PathAnswer.merge_sections` is True and `answer_obj()["merge_sections"] = True` only when the option is on; default bytes unchanged (tested). A list that collapses to one item has verdict ANSWER. `trace_check.trace_answer` compares an origin state with its item as a multiset when the option is on. Nothing else is merged: no similarity rule is applied (owner (b) first wants to see examples). | readout.py, trace_check.py |
| L-171 | `ask_tier(raise_budget="on_demand", raise_levels=("high","max"), weights=None)` (option, default None = the old `ask_tier`, now `_ask_tier_once`, byte-identical). Owner: "問いで必要になったときだけ上げる". A query "needs" a raise when it has no adopted state and a cross it read has stop == "budget" (an "exhausted" cross cannot grow). Those crosses are rebuilt with placement.build_cross at each level of `raise_levels` above their current one, the question is asked again on the stored placements with the rebuilt crosses in place (stored placements unchanged), until a state is adopted or the levels end. The result's `thought_obj()["variant"]["budget_raise"]` = {mode, levels, needed, steps: [{level, raised: [{seed, capacity_before, capacity_after, stop_after}], verdict, states_adopted, crosses_read}], final_level}; present (needed False) whenever the option is on. | cycle.py |
| L-172 | Experiments: experiments/line3/t6y/merge_stats.py (list sizes and strict rule B on the 90 S300 RUN answers with merged lists), make_examples.py (three example lists with candidate similarity measures; none chosen), budget_raise.py (the 9 questions with a budget-stopped cross). Results in experiments/line3/t6y/results and examples/. | experiments/line3/t6y |

### T6y open points
  (1) "Similar" (owner (b)) is undecided; the example files give candidate measures only (same word set, Jaccard cut, same centre, subset, one path differs) and how many items each would remove (greedy from the head of the list, which keeps the first item: the result depends on the list order).
  (2) The merge is exact on section assignment only; the many remaining items differ by which words a path holds (e.g. different one-word paths of the same shape) and by the centre.
  (3) "Needs a raise" is taken as: no adopted state and a read cross stopped by budget. Raising every budget-stopped read cross even when a state exists (more complete) is not done. Levels tried: high then max; the stopping point (max is a compromise level) is the owner's.
  (4) Raising helps only 3 of 9 (a06, a19, a32); a06 and a19 have the gold in the path words (lists of thousands of items), a32 does not; the other six stay without a state (budget-stopped crosses remain capacity 1 at max, or exhausted without a fixed point / ambiguous). Cost: up to 120 s per question.

## T6z (2026-10-07): owner's defaults — merge by section assignment, collapse by same word set, on-demand budget raise
Owner (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md, 「T6y の後の決定」: 「Iで進めて」) made three things defaults.
| ID | Decision | Where |
|---|---|---|
| L-180 | `read_answer/read_out/read_out_result(merge_sections=True, similar="word_set")` are now the DEFAULTS. The old behaviour is the explicit options `merge_sections=False, similar=None` (byte-identical to T6x: tests/line3/test_t6x.py hashes, test_t6z.py). `similar=None, merge_sections=True` is the T6y behaviour (list of items, `merge_sections: true` in the object). | readout.py |
| L-181 | "Similar" = the items use the SAME SET of words, ignoring order, which path and which section holds a word. The word set of an item = the words of all its section paths ∪ {centre} (the centre is the last word of every non-empty path, so the union changes nothing in practice; it is stated so an empty-path item would still count its centre). Items with the same set are ONE list entry (`AnswerEntry`): word set (sorted by code point, a label), every arrangement (the merged items, in item-key order, none promoted to representative), `count`, the union of origin states and of source sentences, the best stability (information only), and the arrangements' centres (all of them; they may differ). Entries are listed in the order of their word sets (a label, never a winner). Verdict from the number of ENTRIES: 1 = ANSWER, several = CHOICE (list), none = UNKNOWN_NO_PATH; `too_many` counts entries. The answer of a one-entry list: `answer = {path_words: the word set, arrangements: n, source_sids, reference_centre (if one centre, else null), reference_centres}`; `paths` is given only when the entry has exactly one arrangement. The object lists `entries` (each with its `arrangements` in the previous item form), `arrangements` (total items) and `listed` (= entries). Order independence is tested (any order of the states gives the same entries). | readout.py |
| L-182 | Intake: `choose_item(ans, i)` / `adopt_item(ans)` take an index into the ENTRIES. The memory record of a word-set entry (`form: "word_set"`) holds the word set, all arrangements (centre + paths), the centres, the union of origins, `offered` = number of entries. With `similar=None` the record is the old `centre_paths` record. | readout.py |
| L-183 | `trace_check.trace_answer`: every arrangement (item) is traced as before (every path word: seat, sentence, edge, path ends at the centre; every origin state reproduces the words as a multiset). An entry is ok iff all its arrangements are traced items, its word set is exactly the union of the arrangements' words and centres, its arrangements are exactly ALL the items with that word set (none missing, none foreign), its origins are the union of the arrangements' origins, its stability is their best, and the entries cover every item exactly once. The report counts entries (`sentences_checked/ok`) and the path words of the arrangements; 100% required; tampering with a word set, an arrangement, origins or the coverage is detected (tested). | trace_check.py |
| L-184 | `cycle.ask_tier(raise_budget="on_demand")` is the DEFAULT (L-171 unchanged). `raise_budget=None` is exactly the previous default (`_ask_tier_once`, byte-identical, tested). With the default the thought carries `variant.budget_raise` (`needed: false` when nothing was raised), so default thought bytes differ from before only by that record. | cycle.py |
| L-185 | Experiments: experiments/line3/t6z/run_all.py (the 90 S300 RUN questions with the defaults, nothing but the search budget passed), summarize.py (grading, list sizes, collapse, gold, question dependence, time), make_examples.py (a59 a33 a27 a47 entries). Results in experiments/line3/t6z/results (the large jsonl is git-ignored) and examples/. | experiments/line3/t6z |

Grading of a single entry (rule B, official): the answer text = the entry's word set, one word per line (a gold string cannot span two words any more); the sensitivity row B' grades on every arrangement's path texts (as T6w..T6y). Rule A: the entry's reference centres (all, one per line).

### T6z open points
  (1) Whether the centre belongs to the word set: the examples count path words, and the centre is already the last word of every path, so it changes nothing here; for a state whose paths hold other words but not the centre it would. Stated, not asked.
  (2) An entry can carry arrangements with DIFFERENT centres (45 entries on S300, e.g. a33 #5: five words are each the centre of some arrangement). Whether such arrangements are "similar" (same words, different role of the words) is the owner's; the centre is a reference only, so they are grouped.
  (3) The answer of a one-entry list is the word set (sorted by code point, no order); the original word order / path structure is only in the arrangements. A gold string spanning two words can no longer match; on S300 this made no difference (rule B and B' equal).
  (4) No list collapsed to an answer by the word-set rule on S300 (the one list that collapsed by the section-assignment merge, a52, was already an answer in T6y). The word-set rule shrinks the 20 remaining lists from 13246 arrangements to 680 entries (median entries 25.5), but the lists are still long; "similar" by the same set does not make a user choice short.
  (5) Rule B grading of a list is unchanged (a list = abstention); with entries the owner may want to credit a list whose entries ALL contain a gold word, or a list of small size; not done.


## T6aa (2026-10-07): the answer of a list = the words common to all its entries
Owner (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md, 「T6z の後の決定」): (1) when the result is a list of entries, the ANSWER is the set of words common to ALL entries, and the list is kept for the user to choose from; if the common words are only the query's own units and/or centres the result stays a plain list (abstention for grading); (2) entries with the same word set but different centres stay one entry (as in L-181).
| ID | Decision | Where |
|---|---|---|
| L-190 | Definitions. For a list of >= 2 entries: **intersection** = the words that are in the word set of EVERY entry; **query's own units** = the units of the question after the function-word filter as attached in the cycle = the non-empty entries of `QueryContext.attached` (the first layer; a unit that is not in the tier, e.g. a filtered word, may be among them and is removed all the same); **centres** = the reference centres of the entries = the union of `entry.centres` over all entries (every arrangement's centre); **common answer** = intersection minus query units minus centres, sorted by code point (a label, no order). A single entry (ANSWER) has no common answer: its answer is the entry's word set as in L-181. | readout.py (`CommonAnswer`, `make_common`) |
| L-191 | `read_answer/read_out/read_out_result(common="intersection")` is the DEFAULT; `common=None` is exactly the T6z behaviour (byte-identical: tests/line3/test_t6x.py hashes, test_t6z.py, test_t6aa.py, and on the 45 S300 RUN read-outs the sha256 of the old answer object equals the stored T6z one). Object for a list with the option on: `common_mode: "intersection"`, `common` = {intersection, query_units, centres, words, sources: {word: [sids of entry 0, sids of entry 1, ...]}, source_sids}; verdict stays CHOICE and `entries`, `listed`, `too_many` are unchanged (the list is kept for the user's choice); when `common.words` is non-empty `answer` = {form "common_words", path_words = the common words, source_sids = the union, reference_centres, entries}, otherwise `answer` is None (stays a list = abstention). `centre` / `paths` stay None for a list. `thought_obj()` and the intake (`choose_item`, memory record) are unchanged. | readout.py |
| L-192 | Traceability: the sources of a common word in an entry = the sentences of the tier used by a step into or out of that word in any arrangement of the entry (`entry_word_sources`); one tuple per entry, so the word is traced in EVERY entry; the union over entries is the answer's `source_sids`. `trace_check.trace_answer` additionally checks: the intersection is exactly the words common to all entries; the centres are the union of the entries' centres; every unit attached in a state is among the query units; the answer words are exactly intersection - query units - centres; for every common word, in every entry: it is in the entry's word set, has a non-empty source set equal to the arrangements' edges, and each source sentence holds the word in the tier; a list without its common record, a record on a non-list, or a record without the option is a failure (tampering tested). 100 % required. | trace_check.py |
| L-193 | Experiment experiments/line3/t6aa (run_all.py = the 90 S300 RUN questions with only the defaults and the budget, also stores the sha of the `common=None` object; summarize.py; make_examples.py). Rule B official: single entry as before; list with a non-empty common answer = answered, correct iff a gold string is in the common answer words (+ T0 core-in-subject), else wrong; list with an empty common answer = abstention; unanswerable answered = wrong. | experiments/line3/t6aa |

Result (S300 RUN, 90 questions): T0 legacy a1 45/7/8 (unanswerable answered 11), T6z 18/3/39 (4), T6aa 18/4/38 (6; the answered unanswerable: fict 3 + attr 3). Of the 20 lists only 3 get a non-empty common answer (a59 "起", u04 "ダム", u26 "政府"); none contains a gold string; 17 stay lists (10 with an empty intersection, 7 with an intersection that is only query units / centres, e.g. a47 {山}, a48 {橋}, a55 {発生}).

### T6aa open points
  (1) The words of a common answer are the words every entry shares, which on S300 are mostly the centre itself or generic function-like fragments (起, のこと, に指す): the intersection over 7 to 89 entries is almost always empty or a centre; the gold words are in many entries (a47 7 of 7, a27 41 of 42) but in different ones. A less strict common (e.g. words in most entries, or per-centre-group) is not implemented; the owner's rule is the strict intersection.
  (2) "Query's own units" includes attached units that were filtered out of the tier (they never occur in an entry anyway); a query unit that is NOT attached (a question unit beyond the 6 sections, or of a later layer) is not removed from the intersection. Whether all units of the question should be removed is not decided.
  (3) The centre is removed as a word even when the same word is also a path word of an arrangement that has another centre (centres are the union over all arrangements of all entries), so a word that is the centre of any arrangement can never be a common answer.
  (4) Sources of a word: sentences used by a step into or out of the word in any arrangement of an entry; the first word of a path in a section without an attached query unit has no step into it (only the step out). A word with no step at all in an entry has no source and would fail the trace check; this did not occur on S300.
  (5) The verdict of a list with a common answer is still CHOICE with `answer` filled; a consumer that reads only the verdict sees a list. Whether it should be a separate verdict (e.g. ANSWER_WITH_CHOICE) is open; the grader here treats a non-empty common answer as answered.
  (6) The three non-empty common answers are wrong or unanswerable answered; the rule therefore adds 1 wrong and 2 wrong-by-unanswerable and no correct. Whether a common answer should be shown when it is not the centre-free remainder of a generic fragment (e.g. a minimum length or a share of the sentence) is not decided.


## T6ab (2026-10-07): revert the list answer to T6z; simulate a user choosing from the lists
Owner (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md, 「T6aa の後の決定」): go back to T6z (the list is for choosing, grading = abstention; the common-words rule stays as an option); then simulate a user choosing from the lists.
| ID | Decision | Where |
|---|---|---|
| L-200 | `read_answer/read_out/read_out_result(common=None)` is the DEFAULT again (T6z behaviour); `common="intersection"` (L-190..L-192) stays an option, unchanged. Proof: experiments/line3/t6ab/check_default.py rebuilds the default read-out of every stored S300 RUN state (`states_from_answer_obj` + `make_context(query_used)`) and compares `answer_obj()` with the stored T6z object by sha256: 45 / 45 equal. Tests of the option now pass `common="intersection"` explicitly. | readout.py, tests/line3/test_t6ab.py, test_t6aa.py, test_t6z.py |
| L-201 | Blind export (experiments/line3/t6ab/export_lists.py): for each of the 20 questions whose T6z default answer is a list: `user_lists/<qid>.json` = {question, entries[{index (0-based), words, arrangements, sentence}]}. Representative sentence = among the entry's source sentences the one that holds most of the entry's words (ties: smallest sid); the text is the tier's unit string (function words removed), not the original sentence. No gold, no grading info. The file name is the qid (as specified) whose prefix a/u reveals answerable/unanswerable, so a blind agent gets the neutral copies `user_lists/blind/Q<nn>.json` and `user_lists/ALL.md` (numbers by sha256 of the qid; map in t6ab/blind_map.json, outside user_lists). | experiments/line3/t6ab |
| L-202 | Oracle (oracle.py, results/oracle.md): rule B on an entry = a gold string occurs in one of its words (no core-in-subject condition, as the owner's task states). A gold-knowing user picks a gold entry if one exists, else abstains; an unanswerable list is rejected. | experiments/line3/t6ab |
| L-203 | grade_user.py CHOICES.json: {qid or Q-number: index | "none"}; rule B as in L-202; "none"/missing = abstain; a chosen entry of an unanswerable question = wrong; non-list questions keep their T6z grade. Every chosen entry goes through `choose_item` -> `memory_record()` (checked against the stored default read-out by sha256) into choices_records.jsonl (one line per choice: qid, choice_index, grade, record) for T11. `none` writes no record. | experiments/line3/t6ab |

### T6ab open points
  (1) Rule B for a user's choice ignores the T0 core-in-subject condition (task text); with it, some oracle-correct entries could turn wrong. Not measured.
  (2) "Contains gold" is by substring in a word; entries whose words merely contain the gold as part of a longer fragment count.
  (3) The oracle's first position depends on the displayed order, which is a label (code point order of word sets), not a ranking; gold entries are a large share of most lists, so a blind user can hardly tell them apart by position.
  (4) The shown sentence is the tier's unit string, not the original sentence; mapping sid to the original text is not done.
  (5) `none` writes no memory record; whether a rejection should be recorded (T11) is not decided.

- T7 (tier combination, S300 90 questions): (a) Stabilities of different tiers are compared as exact fractions (I-16 "一番安定なもの"), but they are not on one scale: the CHAR tier's stability is the highest in 47 of 87 questions with a result and CHAR holds the gold in 0 of 60 answerable questions, so the most-stable-tier rule shows a gold candidate for 21 of 60 where RUN alone shows 29 (the pool of all tiers' entries holds one for 30). Whether the stability is comparable across tiers, or the user should be shown every tier's entries (as a labelled list) so as not to lose RUN's candidates, is the owner's decision; the behaviour follows I-16 literally. (b) No tie between tiers occurred in S300 (stabilities are fractions with large denominators), so the tie-to-list path (I-14) is tested but not exercised by data. (c) Tied tiers give one list with each tier's entries kept apart; the same words in two tiers stay two entries (I-16: no summing); whether the list should mark such cross-tier agreements to help the user is not decided (agreement is only in `thought`).
