(d) The tier's stability = the best entry's stability; the alternative "mean over entries" or "stability of the adopted states" is not decided. (e) Time: a question with all three tiers takes a median of 109 s and up to 617 s (9 parallel workers), CHAR is the slowest tier; an interactive user needs the N-18 amount-of-inference control (M-2) or the tier subset (`--tiers`), which is not decided as a default. (f) `line3 ask` rebuilds the space and (without `--cache`) the placements per call; there is no resident process / state version (T10) yet.

## T7: tiers combined and the entrance (verantyx/line3/ask.py, `line3` command of verantyx/cli.py)

| id | decision | location |
|---|---|---|
| L-210 | Defaults of the entrance: placement level `mid` (the level of every S300 measurement T5..T6ab) and query budget `QueryBudget(64, 8)` (the T6z / T6ab runs). Every tier runs `cycle.ask_tier` and `readout.read_out_result` with their own defaults (V1/V2/V3, merge sections, same-word-set entries, on-demand budget raise, path words with sources, centre as reference); nothing is overridden per tier. | ask.py (DEFAULT_LEVEL, DEFAULT_BUDGET) |
| L-211 | Placements: `Index.precompute` builds the cross of every unit of a tier (one cross per process task, so the result does not depend on the worker count) and writes `placements_<sha256(data)[:12]>_<TIER>_<level>.pkl` (pickle: our own cache, never read from elsewhere). Loading refuses a file for other data, another tier or level, or a unit set that is not exactly the tier's. A tier without a cache file builds each cross on demand (Placer, L-07) and gives the same answer as a cached one (tested). | ask.py |
| L-212 | Combination (I-16, I-25, I-14): all requested tiers are run in the fixed order RUN, WORD, CHAR. A tier's stability = the best stability among its listed entries (exact Fraction). The tiers with the highest stability are SHOWN: one tier -> its own result (a single entry = ANSWER, several = CHOICE); several tied tiers -> ONE list = every entry of every tied tier, each labelled with its tier, nothing merged across tiers (the same word set in two tiers is two entries), always CHOICE. A tier with adopted states but no section path has no entry and does not take part. No tier with an entry -> UNKNOWN_NO_STATE (no state adopted anywhere) or UNKNOWN_NO_PATH (states, nothing readable). | ask.py combine |
| L-213 | Cross-tier agreement is REPORTED only: for each pair of tiers with entries, the words and centres they share literally (`thought.agreement`, `used_for_selection: false`). It is never counted, summed or used to choose. | ask.py Combined.agreement |
| L-214 | Output (N-08, N-10): `answer` = {verdict, tiers shown, tie_between_tiers, listed, answer (ANSWER only: tier, path_words, source_sids, reference_centres), entries (tier, words, arrangements, centres, stability, source_sids), sources (sid, ORIGINAL sentence text, source)}; `thought` = {question, tiers run, level, query budget, ranking, shown tiers, agreement, per tier: verdict, stability, states adopted, listed, the cycle's thought object, the read-out's thought and answer objects}. Wall time is not in the bytes (`Combined.ms`, `TierOutcome.ms`, shown by `--show-thought` text). Sources show the original sentence (the T6ab open point (4) is closed for this entrance). | ask.py |
| L-215 | Command: `vera line3 build --data D --cache DIR [--level L] [--tiers T,..] [--workers N]` and `vera line3 ask --data D --question Q [--cache DIR] [--tiers ..] [--show-thought] [--format text|json]`; exit 2 for a missing option, unreadable data or an unknown tier. Only `cmd_line3` and its parser entry were added to cli.py; the 20 recorded legacy / round5 invocations (experiments/line3/t7/record_cli.py, cli_baseline.json; the only normalised field is the wall-clock `*_ms`) are byte-identical after the change. | cli.py, experiments/line3/t7 |
| L-216 | No extra check is added to the product path (I-17): the trace check of T6 is an acceptance tool run by experiments, not by `ask`. | ask.py |

## T7b: all tiers' candidates, and the user's choice of the amount of inference (ask.py, cycle.py options, `line3` command)
Owner (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md, 「T7 の測定後の決定」): show every tier's candidates labelled with the tier (votes not added; the user chooses); the amount of inference is chosen by the user per question (fast answer or a slow, thorough one). Principle: candidates exist for real users to grade; the 90-question score is not the goal.

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-220 | View. `combine(view="all")` is the user-facing default: the list = every entry of every tier that has entries, in the order RUN, WORD, CHAR (inside a tier its own entry order), each entry labelled with its tier and `tier_is_most_stable` (a label from the I-16 ranking; it selects, sums and re-orders nothing). One entry in total = ANSWER, otherwise CHOICE; a same word set in two tiers is two entries. `view="stable"` is the T7 / I-16 behaviour (most stable tier(s) only), unchanged. `answer` gains `view`, `most_stable_tiers`, `per_tier_listed`, `read`; `thought` gains `view`, `effort`, `node_budget`, `read`, and its `rule` names the view. | ask.py combine, Combined.answer_obj / thought_obj |
| L-221 | Node budget (M-2(a), N-18). The unit of the amount is the number of CROSSES READ PER TIER (`cycle._ask_tier_once(read_cap=N)`, `plan_read(read_cap=N)`). The crosses the default read (V1: crosses holding a question unit) would read are ordered as M-2(a): crosses whose seed is a question unit, then the other crosses holding one; inside a group by E_Q(seed) = n + sum n(q, seed) descending; equal values are read together or not at all (a tied group that does not fit is not split, so a budget smaller than the first tied group reads nothing: `node_budget.tied_group_not_split` in thought). The read is a prefix of this order (more budget only adds crosses; tested). The result never depends on the order the crosses are then processed in (canonical, as before). `read_cap=None` is byte-identical to the previous behaviour (`ReadPlan` gains cap fields; the thought `read` object gains `node_budget` only when a cap is set). | cycle.py |
| L-222 | Partial marking (design 4.2: "読んだ十字の数・読まなかった十字の数を出力に必ず出し、部分読みと印を付ける"). `answer.read` = {effort, node_budget, rebuild_levels, partial, per_tier: {tier: {crosses_read, left_unread, would_read_in_full}}, rebuild_skipped}; `partial` = some tier left crosses unread. Text output prints 「【部分読み】」 with the counts and says that an unread cross may hold the answer. A budget that covers everything is not partial and reads exactly like the whole read (tested). Rebuilding a cross at a higher placement level (T6y on-demand raise) costs 10-270 s per cross (far more than reading one), so it is part of the effort: a preset that does not rebuild reports, per tier, how many budget-limited crosses it read without a state and did not rebuild (`rebuild_skipped`). | ask.py |
| L-223 | Presets (`ask.EFFORTS` = (node budget per tier, rebuild levels)), fixed by measurement on the 90 S300 questions (experiments/line3/t7b/results/summary.md, serial single-process re-check of 7 questions in results/serial_*.jsonl: same entries, times within 5 % of the sharded run): `fast` = 4 crosses per tier, no rebuild (median 3.2 s, p90 14.8 s, max 68 s; 71 of 90 questions <= 10 s); `standard` = 10 crosses per tier, no rebuild (median 16.6 s, p90 50 s, max 110 s; 84 of 90 <= 60 s); `full` = the whole read with the T6y rebuild (as T7: median 109 s, p90 404 s, max 617 s). A "standard" that kept the rebuild at level `high` was measured on 36 questions and dropped: 18 of them rebuilt crosses and 9 of the 36 took > 60 s (up to 272 s), while the whole difference between standard without rebuild and full is one more answerable question with a gold candidate (29 vs 30) (results/_probe/standard_with_high_rebuild_partial.jsonl). Only `full` rebuilds. Other budgets measured without rebuild: 3, 6, 8, 12, 24 (results/_probe/probe_summary.md). | ask.py EFFORTS |
| L-224 | The user chooses; there is NO product default preset. API: `ask(index, q, effort="fast"|"standard"|"full")` or `ask(..., nodes=N)` (an explicit node budget, named effort "nodes", never rebuilds); with neither the library keeps the T7 whole read (not a preset: the old behaviour). CLI `vera line3 ask`: `--effort fast|standard|full` or `--nodes N`; with neither, a terminal is asked (「速い答え / ふつう / 時間をかけた答え / 十字の数」) and a non-terminal run is refused with exit 2 (a script must say it). `--view all|stable` (default all). Wall time stays out of the output bytes. | ask.py resolve_effort, cli.py cmd_line3 |
| L-225 | Intake with tier labels. `Combined.memory_record(i)` = the user's choice of entry i of the list the user saw (all tiers): the chosen tier's `readout.choose_item` record (kind `memory_answer`, carries `tier`, words, centres, arrangements, stability, origins, `offered` = the whole labelled list's size, `choice_index` = i, `base_changed: false`), plus `view`, `tier_entry_index` (position inside that tier's entries), `tiers_offered`, `effort`, `node_budget`, `partial_read` and the tier's `crosses` counts (so a choice made from a partial read can be told apart in T11). `memory_record()` without an index adopts the single entry automatically (source `auto`); a list is never auto-adopted. CLI: `--choose I [--record FILE]` prints the record (stderr) and appends it as one JSON line; `--record` alone is the automatic adoption of a single answer. | ask.py, cli.py |

### T7b open points
  (1) A budget smaller than the first tied group of the M-2(a) order reads nothing in that tier (the "together or not at all" rule); with nodes=1 the toy space reads 0 crosses in every tier. Whether a too-small budget should read the group partly (and so split a tie) is not decided.
  (2) The budget counts crosses, but the time is dominated by what is inside a cross (members, states): at the same budget one question takes 0.6 s and another 70 s (fast). A budget in settled states / accepted moves would bound time better; M-2(a) says crosses.
  (3) The budget is per tier. The three tiers' crosses cost very differently (CHAR 4 crosses can take longer than RUN 10); a total budget across tiers, or per-tier budgets, is not decided.
  (4) Candidate quality at the presets (answerable 60, gold in some candidate): fast 28, standard 29, full 30; but fast shows a single ANSWER for 4 questions (small lists look confident) and full for 0; unanswerable questions: no candidate 4 / 3 / 1, rejectable list 22 / 25 / 26, single wrong answer 4 / 2 / 3. Full lists are much longer (median 22, max 201 vs 6 / 7 and 51 / 69): the gold survives a small read because the list is small, which is the trade the user makes. Which preset the user is offered first, and whether to prompt the user with the partial-read counts, is a product question.
  (5) The pool of all three tiers is shown; CHAR holds no gold in any preset (0 of 60) and contributes the longest lists (up to 193 entries). A per-tier switch (`--tiers`) exists; whether CHAR should be shown by default is not decided.
  (6) `fast` and `standard` do not rebuild crosses (L-222/L-223): a tier that read only budget-limited crosses and adopted no state reports `rebuild_skipped`; the three questions with no candidate under standard (a-questions: 3 of 60, full 2) are partly this.
  (7) The times are one process per question on this machine (10 cores), 6 to 9 worker processes ran at once for the sweeps; a serial check of 7 questions showed times within 5 % of those.

## T8: layers (matryoshka) and the coarse -> fine descent (verantyx/line3/matryoshka.py; options in placement.py, cycle.py, cli.py)
Binding: decision 9, I-18, I-19, I-20, N-05..N-08, N-11, N-12, N-19, M-1(c), M-2, M-5, and the owner's nesting answer of 2026-10-07 (the upper cross PACKS (compresses) the unstable set of lower crosses and is the next minimal unit; going up is a SEARCH SHORTCUT: search the packed upper crosses first, then only the lower crosses under the elements of the selected path). Principle: candidates are for real users to grade; the 90-question score is not the goal.

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-230 | Switch. `A.ask` (layer 0, T7b) is untouched (byte-identical, tested). `matryoshka.ask_layered(index, q, ..., options=LayerOptions)` = layer 0 + the layers; CLI `line3 ask --layers on|off` (default **off** for now: see OPEN POINT 1), `--query-pass both|A|B`, `--layer-granularity same|compress`, `--layer-feedback none|down`. | matryoshka.py, cli.py |
| L-231 | `placement.build_cross(..., pool_groups=None)`: only the first N share-groups (M-1(b), whole groups, a tie is never split) enter the pool; stop becomes "max_groups" when groups were left out and the budget did not stop it first. Default None = byte-identical (tested). It is the node budget of an upper cross. | placement.py |
| L-232 | `cycle._ask_tier_once(plan_override=ReadPlan)` (the caller names the crosses to read: an upper layer reads the crosses of its query bundles, not "every cross holding a query unit", which would build every upper cross), `observe=False` (no L-20 energy log at an upper layer: it cost 17 of 214 s and the log is for the base), `lazy_members=True` + `members_of(lazy=True)` (with a cap and twins, the first `cap` DISTINCT arrangements in the generation order of the twin permutations are read, listed canonically; the old path expanded a class of millions before slicing). Defaults unchanged (tested). | cycle.py |
| L-233 | Trigger (N-08, N-11), checked WHEN A QUESTION IS ASKED: a layer is stacked for a tier iff (a) a cross the question read is FULL = its placement stopped by the budget (the only way the code loses stability; the state that was stable just before is the restored one, N-05: `Placement.members` of a budget stop), or (b) a class member had no fixed point under the question (query-time collapse). The stored placement's stop is used (a cross that the T6y on-demand raise rebuilt still counts by its stored state). Nothing stacks otherwise: layer 0 is returned alone and `thought.layers` says so. | matryoshka.find_triggers |
| L-234 | Upper layer = the PACK of the lower stable states (owner 2026-10-07; M-1(c)). Layer 1 unit = bundle `⟦1:seed⟧` = the units of the stable state of that seed (representative + twins); its quantity = the UNION of the sentences of its words (`postings_union`); its word order in a sentence = the earliest position of any of its words (BundleTier, a TierSpace subclass so that placement/cycle/readout run on it unchanged: the SAME stability rule, the I-04 key over bundles, verified independently by `verify_class` in tests). p(x,y) is a STRICT comparison of earliest positions (a tie counts for neither side, so no order label biases a key). An upper cross is `build_cross` over the bundle space seeded at a bundle (pool cut by `pool_groups`, placement budget level by effort). Its seats hold bundles, never base words or lower arms (tested). `same` = bundle every stable state of the tier (cached per index), `compress` = only the states this question touched (read crosses, question units, answer units). | matryoshka.py |
| L-235 | Query passing (I-20, M-5(c)). A = the initial query (all its units, the inner crosses 7+ included) then the lower answer; B = the lower answer only (inner units NOT added). Lower answer units = every word of every listed layer-0 entry (path words + centre), in entry order. Units map to their bundles; only the first 6 attach to sections (I-07), all of them carry energy (`scope="whole"`); question bundles come first, the answer's by E_Q descending with ties together or not at all and a pass cap by effort. Crosses read = the query bundles (by E_Q, tied groups whole, node cap by effort). States are adopted by the V3 rule (sentences shared with the query). Entries are read out by the unchanged `read_answer`, then expanded (N-19) to the BASE words under the bundles of the path and centre; entries with the same expanded word set are one (the owner's "same word set" similarity of T6z); labels (tier, layer, variant) are never merged. B with nothing to pass is typed `UNKNOWN_NOTHING_TO_PASS`. | matryoshka._variant_chain/_read_layer |
| L-236 | Layer k+1 (「十字が宇宙」): when an upper cross that was read is itself full or a member lost its fixed point, the upper crosses READ at that layer (the stable states this question needed, hence deterministic regardless of cache) are bundled again: `⟦k+1:⟦k:..⟧⟧`, same rule, same passing. Up to `max_layers` of the effort; a further need is reported (`layer_limit`), not built. | matryoshka.py |
| L-237 | Trace through the layers: each upper run is checked at bundle level with `trace_check.trace_answer` on the bundle space and every base word of every entry must descend through the bundles of its entry and the lower units of every layer to a unit placed in the stable state of a base seed that shares a sentence with it. Fraction exact; 100 % required (416/416 runs at fast). | matryoshka.trace_run |
| L-238 | Effort bounds the layer work (T7b rule): fast = 1 layer, 4 upper crosses, pass 8, pool 3 groups, 8 class members, level low, 2 rounds; standard = 2 layers, 10, 16, 6, 24, mid-low, 4; full = 3 layers, 24, 32, 12, 64, mid, 16. Even full is bounded (an upper class had 2016 members = 3.5 min per cross, another 2.5 million); every cut is marked: `partial`, `left_unread`, `members_read/members_total`, `layer_limit`. Chosen from timing on toy and S300, not from candidate quality. | LAYER_EFFORTS |
| L-239 | Re-read (N-12). `feedback="none"` (default): feed-forward only; a second sweep reads the same inputs, so the fixed point is immediate (reported as such, not recomputed). `feedback="down"`: the upper layers' words are added to the question units and layer 0 is read again, until no layer's entries change (reached) or `rounds` is exhausted (typed `UNKNOWN_NO_FIXED_POINT_LAYERS`, with the budget). The downward flow is my reading: see OPEN POINT 4. | matryoshka.run_layers |
| L-240 | The descent (owner's shortcut): `descend_tier` reads the question on the layer-1 crosses of the question's units first (coarse), takes the lower crosses packed under the bundles on the selected paths (path words + centre, via their seeds), reads ONLY those at layer 0 (fine; node cap by effort, marked), and reports upper + lower crosses read against the crosses the whole flat read would read. The full flat read stays available (`A.ask`). | matryoshka.descend_tier |
| L-241 | Output: `LayeredCombined` = layer-0 entries then upper-layer entries, each labelled (tier, layer, variant); `thought.layers` shows triggers, the compress/same choice with BOTH sizes, fixed point, every run (bundles, read counts, partial marks, trace). `memory_record(i)` carries layer/variant/bundles; the user's choice never changes the base. | matryoshka.LayeredCombined |
| L-242 | The invariant tests of earlier tickets that checked `git status` for placement.py now exclude it (it carries L-231; its default is proved byte-identical in test_matryoshka.py). | tests |

### T8 measurement (S300, 90 questions: 60 answerable, 30 unanswerable; experiments/line3/t8/results/summary_fast.md, summary_standard.md, summary_descent.md; raw .jsonl git-ignored)
Candidate quality first. Gold in some candidate (answerable; layers off / on-same / on-compress, query A, B, A+B): fast 28 / 30, 28, 30 / 36, 29, 36; standard 29 / 32, 30, 32 / 38, 31, 39. A is what gains (compress A: +8 at fast, +9 at standard); B alone adds 0-2. Gold-bearing upper entries are small (median 9 words compress, 16-19 same) but the upper entries as a whole are large bags (median 55-154 words vs 5 at layer 0; N-19 expands a bundle to every word of the lower state). Unanswerable: single wrong answer 4 -> 0-3 (fast), 2 -> 0-1 (standard); "no candidate" falls (4 -> 1-3); lists stay rejectable (22 -> 26-27). Lists grow (standard median 7 -> 15-26). Every question stacks (44 RUN / 74 WORD / 90 CHAR of 90 at both presets): most crosses are budget-stopped and the default query budget (64 states, 8 ends) leaves members without a fixed point, so "stability lost" is the normal case, not the exception. Time added: fast +0.4 s median same / +0.1 s compress (layer 0 3.4 s); standard +39 s same / +21 s compress (layer 0 39 s; 8 worker processes were running at once, so all times are inflated). Trace 100 % in every run (fast 416/416, standard 761 same + 658 compress). Rule-B scores (reference only): off 4/11 right/wrong at fast, layers on A 0/7 (same) 2/5 (compress), B 4/1.
Descent (coarse -> fine, same granularity): reads far fewer crosses than the whole flat read (sum over 90 questions: 1241 / 2257 / 2345 crosses at fast / standard / full bounds vs 13971) but candidate quality is below the flat read of the same preset where the flat read is small: gold in a candidate fast 2 (flat fast 28), standard 19 (29), full-bounds 21 (flat full 30); unanswerable single-wrong 10 / 4 / 4. Time: median 12 s / 35 s / 76 s vs flat 3 / 17 / 109 s: cheaper than the whole flat read only at the full bounds (76 vs 109 s median, p90 173 vs 404 s). The shortcut as built (one upper cross per question unit, the path's seeds below) loses the answer in most questions; whether another way of choosing the path (OPEN POINT 6) keeps it is not known.

### T8 open points (meaning-level; behaviour not decided by the code)
  (1) Default. Decisions 9 / I-18 / N-08 / N-11 imply the check at every question, i.e. layers on. They are on in `ask_layered` and `--layers on`; the CLI default is still `off` because the upper entries are word bags 20-30x the size of layer-0 entries, because every question stacks, and because of points 2-6. Flipping the default is one word in cli.py.
  (2) "compress" vs "same granularity" (M-2) is built as: compress = bundle only the stable states the question touched; same = bundle every stable state (N-06). The owner's text says compress is faster and same is more precise; measured, compress holds the gold in MORE questions at both presets (38 vs 32 at standard) and costs half the time. Whether this is what "compress the lower-structure result" means (it could instead mean a lossy form of each bundle) is not decided.
  (3) What a bundle contributes to a candidate (N-19: "the read-out of the elements along the path"): built as all the base words of the lower state (large bags). The alternative, reading the lower cross out under the question (its path words, as layer 0 does), is not built; it would make candidates short but needs a per-bundle read-out and a rule for combining them.
  (4) Re-read (N-12): the owner says the flow is from a layer to the next; a fixed point needs some feedback. Default `none` is feed-forward only (the fixed point is trivial). `down` (upper words are added to the question of the re-read of the lower layer) is my invention and is NOT measured on S300.
  (5) Query passing: which lower answer units go up when layer 0 gives a list (built: every word of every listed entry, capped by energy with ties together); whether the inner query crosses (7+) are added in variant B (built: no; M-5 says they are added when passing, I-20 says B is the answer only).
  (6) Descent: the path is taken from the layer-1 read of the question's own units; the owner did not say how the coarse path is chosen, how many upper crosses are searched, or what to do when the coarse layer has no path (built: nothing is read below, the question has no candidate). The result above is for this reading only.
  (7) Layer 2+ is bundled from the upper crosses this question read (so that the result does not depend on the cache); the owner's picture bundles any unstable GROUP of lower crosses at any time. Whether a layer should persist across questions (state versions, T10) is open.
  (8) The upper cross's pool is cut to `pool_groups` share-groups and its class members to `members` by the effort preset (marked partial); the true upper layer would not be cut. The owner's "no fixed count; the number is decided by stability" is respected only inside the cut.
  (9) Layer-0 entries stay in the list next to the upper entries (labelled). N-11 says the upper layer answers "in place"; I did not drop layer 0 because the user is to grade candidates, not be shown a single winner.

## T8b: layers on by default, and upper-layer candidates = the path words read under the question (verantyx/line3/matryoshka.py, ask.py untouched, cli.py cmd_line3)
Binding: the owner's decisions of 2026-10-07 after T8 (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md, 「T8 の測定後の決定」): (1) layers ON by default with compress and query passing A (B and same granularity stay options); (2) an upper-layer candidate shows ONLY the path words read under the question (N-19), not all the words of a bundle.

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-250 | Defaults. `LayerOptions()` = variants `("A",)`, granularity `compress`, candidate `path`; CLI `line3 ask`: `--layers on` (default), `--query-pass A`, `--layer-granularity compress`, `--layer-candidate path`. The old behaviour is reached by explicit flags and is byte-identical to the T8 tree (bbf85e3): `--layers off` = `A.ask` (layer 0, untouched, hash in tests); `--query-pass both --layer-granularity same --layer-candidate bag` (library: `LayerOptions(candidate="bag", variants=VARIANTS, granularity="same")`) reproduces T8's bytes for `to_bytes()` incl. `feedback="down"` (4 hashes from the T8 tree in tests; also 10 S300 questions at fast compared tree-to-tree: `experiments/line3/t8b/oldbytes.py`). The JSON of `candidate="bag"` carries none of the new keys (the options JSON shows `candidate`/`down_query` only for `path`). The T7 command tests now pass `--layers off`. | matryoshka.LayerOptions, cli.py |
| L-251 | Candidate = path words (`candidate="path"`). After the upper cross is read (unchanged, so which bundles are on the paths is unchanged), each bundle of an upper entry (its path bundles and its centre bundle) is read DOWN: the lower cross packed in it is read under the question exactly as layer 0 reads a cross (`cycle._ask_tier_once` with `plan_override` = that one cross, scope `whole`, members capped by effort, `ro.read_out_result`). At layer 1 that cross is the base cross of the bundle's seed and the words are base path words; at layer k >= 2 the layer k-1 cross of the seed bundle is read, and every bundle on ITS paths is read down again, to base words (N-19 "read out the elements along the path"). The question units of a lower read = the units the upper layer was asked, mapped one layer down (A: initial query then lower answer; B: lower answer; capped as the pass cap). The candidate's words = the union of the path words (section-path words and centre, as a layer-0 entry has) of those reads; entries with the same word SET are one (as L-235). `word_sources[w]` = the base sentences of the steps into/out of w (`entry_word_sources`, as layer 0); `source_sids` = their union; `path_from` = which lower crosses were read (bundle, layer, seed, units, listed, members read/total, words). The sentence texts are served from the space, so the answer's `sources` carry them. An upper entry whose lower crosses read NO path under the question gives no candidate (counted `entries_without_path_words`; its bundles still go up to the next layer so the layer logic is unchanged). Reads are cached (pure). | matryoshka.down_read, _read_layer |
| L-252 | Option `down_query="seed+question"` (CLI `--layer-down-query`): the lower cross is read under the bundle's own seed first, then the question (so the seed attaches to a section). Default `question`. Added after the measurement showed that over half of the upper entries gave no path (see below); it changes nothing measurable (fast: 98 vs 99 dropped, 28 vs 28 gold). | matryoshka.down_read |
| L-253 | Exact tracing, 100 %: `trace_run` additionally runs `trace_check.trace_answer` on EVERY lower read an entry's words came from (seat, edge sentence, centre) at that layer's space, checks that the entry's words are exactly the words of its `word_sources`, that every word has a source sentence and that every source sentence holds the word, and keeps the T8 check that each word lies in a bundle of the entry that descends through every layer to a stable base state. Measured: every upper run traced (fast 208/208 per config, standard 345/345 A and 313/313 B). Tests assert candidate words are a subset of the path words of an independent re-read of the lower cross, layer-2 candidates are read down through layer 1 to base words, hash seeds 0/1/12345 give equal bytes, no float. | matryoshka.trace_run, tests |

### T8b measurement (S300, 90 questions: 60 answerable, 30 unanswerable; experiments/line3/t8b/results/summary_fast.md, summary_standard.md; raw .jsonl git-ignored)
Candidate quality first. Gold in some candidate (answerable 60): fast: layers off 28, T8 bag compress A 36, T8b path compress A (default) **28**, path B 28, path same A 28; standard: off 29, T8 bag compress A 38, path compress A **30**, path B 30. So the path-word candidate keeps NONE of T8's gain at fast (+0) and +1 at standard (a37 "トチローはどの作品に登場しますか" -> 松本零士), against +8/+9 for the bags: the gold T8 found was in words of the bundled lower state that are not on a path read under the question. Candidate size (words per upper candidate, answerable): T8 bag compress A median 93.5 / max 165 (fast), 118.5 / 191 (standard); path 18 / 35 (fast), 24 / 58 (standard); layer 0 5 / 21. Upper candidates per question (median / max): bag 1 / 7 and 6 / 37; path 1 / 3 and 4 / 9. List sizes (all layers, lists >= 2, median / max): fast off 6 (51), bag 7 (53), path 7 (51); standard off 7 (69), bag 15 (76), path 12 (72). Unanswerable (30): no candidate / rejectable list / single wrong: fast off 4/22/4, bag 3/26/1, path 4/24/2; standard off 3/25/2, bag 1/28/1, path 3/26/1. So path words keep part of the lowering of single wrong answers (fast 4 -> 2, standard 2 -> 1) but not the gold gain. Why: 99 of 170 upper entries at fast (512 of 833 at standard) had NO path under the question: the bundles on an upper path are exactly the lower crosses that lost their stability under the question (the layer is stacked BECAUSE of that), and re-reading such a cross under the question gives `UNKNOWN_NO_FIXED_POINT` / section or ratio disagreement (checked on a02 and others; with the seed attached too). Time (median per question, other measurements ran at the same time, load average 15-35, so inflated): fast layer 0 6.5 s, path compress A +10.3 s (B +5.8, same A +27.0) against T8's +0.1 s for the bag; standard layer 0 29.4 s, path compress A +50.3 s (B +43.6) against T8's +21 s; the cost is the lower reads (a base cross read with 8 units costs 0.1-4 s each, 5 to 30 crosses per entry). Rule-B (reference only, right/wrong): fast off 4/11, bag A 2/5, path A 4/6; standard off 1/5, bag A 0/1, path A 1/2.

### T8b open points (meaning-level; behaviour not decided by the code)
  (1) "Under the question": built as the upper layer's own question units (and the lower answer passed up, in A), mapped one layer down; the lower cross is read as layer 0 reads a cross. The path of the bundle in the upper layer exists because the cross was unstable at this question, so this read often fails (58 % of upper entries). Whether the lower read should instead use a different question (the bundle's seed alone; the upper path's previous bundle as the query), or read the cross WITHOUT the question's energy (its own stable state's paths), is not decided; each would give candidates where this gives none.
  (2) An upper entry whose lower crosses read no path is dropped (no candidate). The alternative (fall back to the T8 bag, or show the bundle's seed only) is not built; the bag would restore the gold gain at fast (+8) and standard (+9) at 5-10x the words.
  (3) How the lower reads of one entry combine: built as the UNION of every listed entry's path words of every bundle on the entry (one candidate per upper entry, equal word sets one). A lower read that itself lists several entries is therefore also unioned (the candidate can be larger than any single path). A candidate per lower entry (a list) is not built.
  (4) Whether the centre (reference) of a lower read counts as a "path word": built as at layer 0 (path words plus centre, N-10/L-160 entry words).
  (5) Query passing for the lower read (the question units of a lower read in variant B = the lower answer only) and the pass cap apply as in the upper read; the inner query crosses (units from the 7th) are in the energy but only the first 6 attach, so a lower cross that holds none of the first 6 gives no path.
  (6) Layers stay unstable by the same rule (the trigger still fires on every question, L-233), so every question now costs the lower reads; whether a question whose layer-0 read already contains a stable answer should skip them is the owner's decision (N-08 says the check is at every question).
  (7) T8 open points 2 (compress meaning), 4 (feedback `down`, not measured with path words), 6-9 are unchanged.

## C1: local space and order ledger (verantyx/line3/carry.py, tests/line3/test_carry_space.py)
Binding: docs/LINE3_CARRY_DESIGN.md 3.5, 3.7, 6.2 (I-1, I-2), 9 (C1); owner's answers OP-1 (a), OP-2 (b), OP-3 (a). C1 builds types, the canonical ledger and the local space only; acceptance/closing (C2) and the tower (C3) are not here. Design-numbered choices realised as designed: L-301 (ids are labels), L-302 (occurrence = first position per sentence, `occurrences_of`), L-311 (strict comparison at equal positions), L-319 (canonical JSONL).

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-322 | An element is (id, kind, vocab, origin). A word is the element with vocab = {itself}; a pack is an element with a vocab of words. One rule for both: an element is in a sentence iff a scope occurrence of it has a unit in the vocab; its position there is the smallest such position. | carry.py `Element`, `LocalSpace` |
| L-323 | Local sentences = the sids that have a scope occurrence, numbered in the order of their first occurrence in the scope stream (the recorded stream order); `sids` maps back to the global sid. | `LocalSpace.sids` |
| L-324 | An occurrence (sid, pos, unit) whose (sid, unit) is already in the scope raises ValueError (the caller applies L-302; no silent skip). sid, pos are ints >= 0. | `_check_inputs`, `Occ` |
| L-325 | Element ids are unique, vocabs non-empty, and a pack id may not equal a word surface in the scope or in a vocab (ValueError). | `_check_inputs` |
| L-326 | An element touched by no scope occurrence is allowed (n = 0). | `LocalSpace` |
| L-327 | Empty scope (N_U = 0): r0 and E_Q are 0 (no evidence; like L-53). | `r0`, `energy` |
| L-328 | Inside a local sentence the element listing is ordered by (position, id); a label only, no count uses it. | `sentence_elements` |
| L-329 | `LocalSpace.to_bytes()` = canonical JSON of scope (stream order), elements (sorted by id, a label), N, n, r0 as "a/b", and n(x,y), p(x,y) of every ordered pair. Incremental and from-scratch must give identical bytes (I-1). | `to_bytes` |
| L-330 | `with_occurrences` / `with_elements` return a NEW immutable LocalSpace; the earlier object never changes (like L-41). | `LocalSpace` |
| L-331 | Ledger events are numbered seq = 1, 2, ... without gaps; kinds = the design's seven plus `split` (design 4.2) and `activation_deferred` (design 4.3 L-308); unknown kind or field raises. | `Ledger` |
| L-332 | `Ledger.from_bytes` accepts only exactly the bytes `to_bytes` would write (canonical); reordered, pretty-printed or edited files are rejected, not normalised. | `Ledger.from_bytes` |
| L-333 | `order_sha256(sids)` = sha256 of the canonical JSON list of the sids in stream order. | `order_sha256` |
| L-334 | `LocalSpace.to_tier()` gives a `TierSpace` subclass (`LocalTier`, strict first-position p_pair) over local sentence indices so placement's `Weights` (the I-04 key) runs on a local space unchanged; its r0 is 0 when N = 0 (L-327, same as `LocalSpace.r0`). placement.py is not modified. C2's settling will call placement on it. | `LocalTier` |
| L-335 | Ledger values are dict/list/str/int/bool/None only; floats and Fractions raise (exact values are written as strings). | `canonical_json` |
| L-336 | `pack()` has no default origin: the caller states `new` (a child pack in its parent unit) or `inherited` (a woken carry). Added in review: the earlier default `inherited` would have mis-marked every child pack at level >= 1. | `pack` |

### C1 open points (meaning-level; not decided by the code)
  (1) Position of a pack inside a unit (design 3.5: "its minimum"): built as the smallest position of any scope occurrence whose unit is in the vocab. When several elements share that position, p counts neither side (L-311); the design does not say whether such a tie should instead be broken by anything else (it is not).
  (2) The question side of E_Q (n_U(q,x)) is counted as sentences of the scope where x is present and the word q occurs; a pack in the query position (q a pack) is not defined by the design and is not built.
  (3) Ledger seq (L-331) numbers EVERY event 1, 2, ... (admit, close, pack, open, ...). Design 4.5 numbers only the admitted occurrences (close / carry rows have "—", rolled-back admissions keep their seq and the replay gets new ones), and 3.2 `admitted_upto` is "the ledger seq" of the restore point. Whether seq is per event or per admitted occurrence, and 0- or 1-based, is for the owner / C2-C3; C1 builds per event, 1-based.
  (4) Design 6.2 I-2 ("rewrite the past, e.g. add question words; every count of U stays byte-identical") holds only while the inherited pack's vocab does not change. In a tower the vocab IS the past scope's words (3.3), and 3.5 places a pack in a scope sentence through its vocab, so adding a word to the past that also occurs in U's scope legitimately changes n, p, r0, E_Q of the pack in U (measured in review: n 2 -> 3 on a small CHAR case). The C1 test keeps the vocab fixed and adds an absolute check that a past-only query word is never evidence. Whether I-2 should be stated as "past rewritten with the vocab fixed" (C4 check) or the membership rule should change is the owner's decision.

## C2: black acceptance and closing (verantyx/line3/carry.py, tests/line3/test_carry_unit.py)
Binding: docs/LINE3_CARRY_DESIGN.md 3.6 items 1-4, 4.1, 4.2, 9 (C2); owner's OP-2 (b), OP-3 (a), T8b answer (backup the stable boundary, restore from it, close from it), "C1 audit" answers (seq on every event, 1-based; a pack's vocab fixed at copy time). Level 0 only; the tower, carry copy and waking are C3 (hooks: `BlackStream.wake`, `BlackStream._after_close`, `Black.admit(occs, elements)`). placement.py untouched (adapter uses its private `_settle`, `_insert_group`, `_Work`, `canon`, `extend`, `min_L`).

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-350 | `Black` is an immutable value (local space, tied class, L, budget); `admit` returns a new Black or raises `Collapse`, so a failed admission changes nothing and the restore point is the earlier value (N-05). The driver `BlackStream` holds the mutable parts. | `Black`, `BlackStream` |
| L-351 | A black that holds exactly one element after an admission is placed without search and is never charged to the budget (one-element cross = trivial fixed point). This is the progress guarantee L-308: every black accepts its first element, even at max_states = 0. | `Black._settle_into` |
| L-352 | One `_Work` per admission, shared by the re-settle (L-71) and the insertion (L-64) (L-306); its (states, moves) is written to the ledger. | `Black.admit` |
| L-353 | An occurrence of a word already seated is also an admission: scope grows, weights change, class is re-settled (design 3.6 step 2). | `BlackStream._run` |
| L-354 | New kind `backup` (written at the start of every sentence = the stable boundary) and new field `to`; every collapse writes one `rollback` whose `to` is the seq of the restore point (the sentence's `backup`, or for a split the last `admit`), and `close` repeats `to`. seq numbers every event. | `EVENT_KINDS`, `EVENT_FIELDS` |
| L-355 | Collapse in a black that held earlier sentences: restore the boundary, close, pack, open, replay the whole sentence (OP-2 b). Collapse in a black empty at sentence start: `split` event, restore last stable state, close from it, continue from the word that did not fit. | `feed_sentence` |
| L-356 | Pack vocab = union of vocabs of the elements NEW in the black at close time, frozen then; inherited elements add no words. | `_close`, `Pack` |
| L-357 | First group of 2+ elements in an empty black (carry hook): each element in turn is the centre (L-304), the rest is inserted, results pooled, settled. If that collapses and part of the group is an activation: `activation_deferred` and the occurrence is admitted alone (L-308). | `_settle_into`, `_run` |
| L-358 | An admission refused by an empty black cannot happen (L-351); if it did: RuntimeError, never a silent skip. A sentence with no unit writes no event. A duplicate (sid, unit) raises (L-324). | `feed_sentence` |
| L-359 | The time of every successful admit (caller's clock; c2.py: CPU time) is kept in `BlackStream.event_secs` outside the ledger (ledger stays byte-identical). A collapsed attempt has no admit seq and is not in it. | `BlackStream` |
| L-360 | `stream_header(...)`: admission = OP-2 (b), instability = OP-3 (a); custom budget written as "max_class=..,max_states=..,max_moves=..". | `stream_header` |

Results (S300, V2 default space, carry empty; experiments/line3/carry/c2/{c2.py,compare.py,results/,logs/}):
- Every closed black and the open one: `verify_class` on its local space is a stable class (all 3 tiers at low, RUN and WORD at mid; 0 failures, 422/676/1078/319/524 blacks).
- low (RUN, WORD, CHAR): identical to C0 black by black (blacks, units, sentences, L, class counts, split sentences).
- mid: RUN 319 blacks (C0 316), units/black med 9 max 12 (C0 9/13), 1-sentence blacks 247 (242), split sentences 88 = the same 88 (96 split events: some sentences split twice); WORD 524 blacks (520), 9 / 15 (9 / 15), 492 (485), split sentences 203 (202, C0's 202 + 1).
- Time per admit at mid: mean 0.13 s, p90 0.47-0.49 s, max 1.95-1.96 s (RUN, WORD; CPU time, two jobs while load ~10-11). Per sentence mean 2.35 s (RUN) / 4.15 s (WORD), max 11.0 / 9.6 s. Stream total 705 s / 1244 s (C0: 926 / 1514 s). low: 10-13 s per tier. (Review: the per-admit numbers cover successful admits only; their sum is 422 s of 705 s (RUN) and 647 s of 1244 s (WORD), so about 40-48 % of the stream time is spent in attempts that collapsed and were rolled back.)
- Why mid differs from C0 (first difference at black 93 RUN, 166 WORD): C0's `tier_of(sids)` gives the black the WHOLE text of every sentence it touches, so (a) the head of a sentence split over two blacks, seated in the EARLIER black, still counts as evidence in the later one, (b) words of the current sentence not fed yet count. In RUN black 93 (s92's tail + s93) C0 counts "上" (in s92's head and in s93) as sharing s92 with the tail words; C2's scope has no such occurrence (condition 2 / design 3.5). Measured step by step: C0 48 classes at the 10th word, C2 30; C2 then collapses at the 14th word under max_class, C0 does not. C0 also skipped the re-settle for repeated words (L-353). At low the budget is too small for either difference to change a closing point.
- Review re-run (C2 review, scratch copy of c0.py, RUN mid, all 300 sentences): C0 unchanged reproduces its 316 blacks exactly; C0 with the black's tier built from the FED occurrences only (C2's scope) matches C2 up to black 108 and differs from black 109 on (318 blacks); C0 with fed-only counts AND a re-settle on repeated words equals C2 black by black (319 blacks: units, sentences, L, class size) and in the split sentences (88). Re-settle alone (first 100 sentences) changes nothing against C0. So both causes named above are real: the scope leak gives the first difference (black 93), the skipped re-settle the later ones. C2's behaviour is the one design 3.5 / 3.6 step 2 asks for (count only the unit's own fed occurrences; every admission re-settles).

### C2 open points (meaning-level; not decided by the code)
  (1) The restore point of a split is the state after the last admit of the black; the owner's T8b "boundary" is implemented as: sentence-start `backup` for an OP-2 (b) close, last `admit` for a split. Whether a split black should instead go back to the last sentence boundary inside the black (none exists when the black began at this sentence) is not asked of the owner by the design; as built, the black keeps the words of the sentence admitted before the one that did not fit.
  (2) A black can close holding a sentence head only (split) and the rest of that sentence's sharing with the head is visible one level up only (design 10, consequence 2); measured: 88 RUN / 203 WORD sentences of 300 at mid.
  (3) Re-settling on a repeated word (L-353) can itself collapse the black; this is counted like any other admission.
  (4) The budget rule makes the first element of every black free; with a carry (C3) the first group may include woken packs, and L-308 then drops the activation (`activation_deferred`). How often that happens is a C3/C8 measurement.

## T8c: upper-layer candidates from the LAST STABLE STATE of each lower cross (verantyx/line3/matryoshka.py `candidate="stable"`, CLI `--layer-candidate stable`; default unchanged = `path`)
Binding: the owner's decision after T8b (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md, 「T8b の測定後の決定」, verbatim): 「安定状態と不安定な状態の境目を記録しておいて不安定状態になった場合に安定状態までバックアップ的なものから復元してそこから束ねて安定状態のまま束ねるということを行うことで安定状態で上の層が作られるようになる」. Everything below that is not that sentence is the most literal reading I could build, numbered, and listed as an open point.

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-340 | New mode `LayerOptions(candidate="stable")` / `--layer-candidate stable` (default stays `path`; `path` and `bag` are byte-identical to dad327f: three golden hashes in tests, the existing T8/T8b goldens unchanged). The upper cross is read exactly as before (which bundles lie on the upper paths is unchanged); only the lower reads of L-251 change: the lower cross packed in a bundle is read at its LAST STABLE STEP of the question instead of under the whole question at once. The candidate words = the path words (section-path words and centre, as a layer-0 entry has) of that restored read; entries with the same word set are one; an entry whose lower reads give no path word gives no candidate (counted `entries_without_path_words`, as L-251). | matryoshka.down_read_stable, _read_layer |
| L-341 | The question is applied unit by unit. Step j (1..n) = the same read as `down_read` (scope whole, members capped by effort, same Reader, same settle) with the FIRST j units of the lower question `ul[j]` (the units `down_read` applies at once, in the same order: the first 6 attach to sections, every unit carries energy). Step 0 = no unit applied = the stored stable cross itself (stable by construction, N-05). A step is STABLE iff every member read reached a fixed point (no member NOFIX, at least one member). The scan goes up from step 1 and stops at the FIRST unstable step: `last_stable_step` = the step before it, `first_unstable_step`, `unstable_unit` = the unit applied at that step, `unstable_unit_attached` = it is one of the 6 attached units. If no step is unstable, `first_unstable_step` is null and the state read is the full-question read (no restore). Pure and cached per (cross, units, member cap, budget). | matryoshka.stable_boundary |
| L-342 | Backup and restore. The backup = the exact end states (the Flats of every fixed point of every member) of the last stable step; at step 0 the member Flats of the stored cross. It is kept in memory and in the answer as `backup = {kind: fixed_points | stored_cross, members, states, sha256 of the canonical flats}` (labels and ints only). When a step is unstable the backup state is the one READ: its candidates are adopted by the unchanged V3 rule and read out by the unchanged `read_answer` (paths, centre, sources); the unstable state is never read. Per bundle read down, the answer JSON (`thought.layers.per_tier.*.runs[].boundaries[]`, also on each entry's `path_from[].boundary`) records `query_units`, `last_stable_step`, `first_unstable_step`, `unstable_unit`, `unstable_unit_attached`, `restored` (true iff a first unstable step exists), `read_at_step`, `backup`. | matryoshka.stable_boundary, backup_digest, LayerRun.boundaries |
| L-343 | Check that the restored state is a fixed point for ALL its members, independent of `settle`: `check_fixed_points` builds a fresh Reader and requires, for every end state of every member read, that no single move improves it (I-05) and that no member is NOFIX. `trace_run` runs it on every restored read an entry came from and fails the trace otherwise; the trace dict of a stable run carries `restored_states_checked`, `fixed_point_end_states_checked`. | matryoshka.check_fixed_points, trace_run |
| L-344 | Provenance (extends L-253): every candidate word keeps its source sentences (`word_sources`), `source_sids`, and `path_from` (which lower cross was read, at which step), and `trace_answer` runs on every restored read at its own layer's space; layers >= 2 read down through layer 1 to base words as in L-251. Measured 100 % (every upper run traced). | matryoshka.down_read_stable, trace_run |
| L-345 | What "bundle from the restored state" means in the code: the units that go up to the next layer are still the units of the stored stable state of each cross read (`placed_units`), which IS the step-0 stable state (the question never changes which units a cross holds: a move permutes seats). So the bundles themselves are stable states already and are not rebuilt; what T8c changes is what is READ from them for the candidate (see open point 1). The 90-question sweeps also read the upper layer's up-bundles unchanged. | matryoshka._variant_chain (unchanged) |

### How this differs from T8's restore-last-stable (L-233 / N-05)
T8's restore is at BUILD time and does not involve the question: `placement.build_cross` never keeps the growth step that broke (`stop == "budget"`), so `Placement.members` is the state that was stable just before growth overflowed; a query-time collapse (a member without a fixed point) just stacks that same pre-question member. Nothing records at which unit of the question the cross lost its fixed point, and the candidate of T8b was read from the collapsed full-question read (so 58 % had no path). T8c adds the query-time boundary (L-341), keeps the last stable step's end states as the backup (L-342) and reads the candidate from it. When the boundary is at step 0 the backup coincides with T8's restored state, but a state without any query unit has no working section, so no path can be read from it.

### T8c measurement (S300, 90 questions: 60 answerable, 30 unanswerable; experiments/line3/t8c/results/summary_fast.md, summary_standard.md; raw .jsonl git-ignored; harness experiments/line3/t8c/{measure.py,summarize.py}, 6 worker processes, load 5-11)
Gold in some candidate (answerable 60), layers off / T8 bag A / T8b path A / T8c stable A / T8c stable B: fast 28 / 36 / 28 / **28** / **28**; standard 29 / 38 / 30 / **29** / **30**. So the stable-state read does NOT bring back T8's gain (+0 at fast, +0 (A) / +1 (B) at standard; T8b +0 / +1). Unanswerable (no candidate / rejectable list / single wrong): fast off 4/22/4, bag 3/26/1, path 4/24/2, stable A 4/23/3, stable B 4/23/3; standard off 3/25/2, bag 1/28/1, path 3/26/1, stable A 3/26/1, stable B 3/26/1. Upper candidate size (words, answerable, median / max): bag 93.5 / 165 (fast), 118.5 / 191 (standard); path 18 / 35, 24 / 58; stable A 16 / 25, 20 / 50; stable B 18 / 27, 18 / 38. Upper entries / entries dropped for lack of path words (all questions): fast path 71 / 99, stable A 56 / 117, stable B 60 / 75; standard path 321 / 512, stable A 273 / 748, stable B 312 / 593. The boundary: of 3533 lower reads (standard, stable A) 1143 stay stable through the whole question, 2390 are restored, and 1838 of those restore to STEP 0, i.e. the FIRST question unit already makes the cross lose its fixed point (fast: 398 of 491); only 552 restore at step >= 2 (327 of those give path words) (histogram of the last stable step in the summaries). Time per question added (median, inflated by load): fast path 6.7 s, stable A 13.0 s, stable B 11.0 s; standard path 37.7 s, stable A 61.1 s, stable B 45.8 s (stable reads once per prefix, n = ~8-10 units). Trace 100 % in every run (fast 208/208 per config, standard 345/345 A, 313/313 B); restored states verified as fixed points in all of them. (The standard sweep was run in two parts: 30 questions with 3 workers, then the other 60 with 6 workers; identical code.)

### T8c open points (meaning-level; not decided by the code)
  (1) A restored state at step 0 (the stored cross before any question unit) has no working section, hence no path words: it gives no candidate. This is the most literal reading of 「安定状態までバックアップから復元」 and it is exactly what happens to 77 % of the restored reads (the first unit breaks the cross). Which state should then be read: (a) the stored cross's own arrangement paths read WITHOUT any query (candidate = its stable state's words, not tied to the question), (b) the step before the first ATTACHED unit that breaks, skipping energy-only units, (c) a different order of applying the units (the order is the question's order; the owner did not say), or (d) nothing (built).
  (2) "Stable" is built as: every member read reaches a fixed point within the query budget (64 states / 8 ends). A budget-limited member counts as unstable (NOFIX), as in L-233(b). Whether stability should also require a unique end / agreed answer (AMBIG, NONE_ members are stable here) is not decided.
  (3) The scan stops at the FIRST unstable step. A later prefix could be stable again; it is not looked at (stability need not be monotone in the units). Taking the last stable prefix over all steps is the alternative reading of 「境目」 and is not built.
  (4) The backup is the exact end states of the members read (capped by effort, marked), not the whole class when the class is larger than the cap.
  (5) "Bundle from the restored state" (L-345): the bundled unit set does not depend on the restored state, so layers >= 2 are formed as before. If the owner means that the upper layer should hold the RESTORED state's arrangement (its paths) as the bundled quantity, that is a different bundle (quantity = sentences of the words on the restored paths) and is not built.
  (6) The upper cross itself is still read under the whole question (L-251); only the lower reads are restored. The upper cross can itself lose its fixed point under the question (stack_points); T8c records that as before but does not apply the boundary scan to upper reads.
  (7) Cost: the unit-by-unit scan reads the cross once per prefix (about 8-10 reads), so stable A costs about 1.6-2x the path read; no cache is shared across prefixes.
  (8) T8 open points 2, 4, 6-9 and T8b open points 2-7 are unchanged.

## C3: the tower — carry-up, copying, waking (verantyx/line3/carry.py, tests/line3/test_carry_tower.py)
Binding: docs/LINE3_CARRY_DESIGN.md 3.3, 3.4, 3.6, 3.7, 4.2, 4.5, 6 (P-1, O-1..O-3), 9 (C3); owner's OP-1 (a) (copy the frontier of ALL levels; packs sleep and take a seat only when a NEW sentence contains a word of their vocab), OP-2 (b), OP-3 (a), T8b answer (restore the last stable state), "C1 audit" answers (seq on every event; a pack's vocab fixed at copy time). placement.py, cycle.py, readout.py, matryoshka.py, cli.py, ask.py untouched. C2 code changes: `BlackStream.__init__(inject=)`, `Pack` gained default-valued provenance fields, `stream_header(copy=)`; every C2 test is unchanged and green.

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-370 | `CarryTower(BlackStream)` adds levels k >= 1. A level-k unit is a `Black` of level k fed ONE child pack at a time (item = pack); its scope = the own_occ of its new children in arrival order; the restore point is the state after the previous item. Same budget at every level (L-305). | `CarryTower._admit_item` |
| L-371 | Frontier F(k) = the NEW packs of the open units of levels > k (level ascending, arrival order inside a unit). A unit that opens (after the carry-up its own closing caused) gets a copy: a tuple of immutable `Pack`s stored under the unit id, never changed (L-309). # OP-1 (a) | `frontier`, `_new_carry` |
| L-372 | Dormant = carry minus the ids seated in the black: DERIVED from the black's elements, no mutable state, so a rollback needs no repair. An item wakes a carried pack iff one unit of the item's scope (an occurrence's unit at level 0; the units of the child pack's own_occ at level k) is in the carried pack's FROZEN vocab; nothing else wakes. # OP-1 (a), condition 2 | `_woken`, `_wake` |
| L-373 | Event order: close, pack, open (new unit), [carry-up of the pack, recursively], carry (group = copied pack ids, maybe empty; exactly one per `open`, before the unit's first admit), then the admit of the item. A level-k admit has item = child pack id, group = [pack] + woken ids, activated = woken ids; rollback / activation_deferred likewise. No separate `activate` event (the woken ids are in `admit.activated`; the C2 ledger already did so). | `_close_upper`, `_after_close` |
| L-374 | The first unit of a new level is created when the level below closes a unit; its copy is empty. | `_carry_up` |
| L-375 | `Pack` gains `children` (ids of its new child packs; () at level 1), `inherited` (woken carried packs seated when it closed), `carry` (ids copied when its unit opened). vocab = union of the vocab of the NEW children = the units of own_occ, at every level (L-356). | `Pack` |
| L-376 | Test-only injection `inject(level, unit, item) -> reason | None`: a reason forces a collapse of a NON-EMPTY unit before the real admission (an empty unit always accepts, L-351); the reason is the ledger's budget_reason and must start with "injected" (ValueError otherwise; added in review), so an injected ledger is never taken for a budget one. Used to fix the design 4.5 collapse points. Default none. | `BlackStream._run`, `_admit_item` |
| L-377 | `replay_ledger(ledger)` rebuilds every unit (scope, new elements, woken, carry, status, pack) from the ledger alone: a rollback removes the unit's admits whose seq is greater than its `to`. `ledger_mismatches(tower)` compares (O-2, net of rollbacks); since the review it also compares each pack's vocab with its `pack` event and with the units of its unit's replayed scope. | `replay_ledger` |
| L-378 | Cache key (L-320): `check_cache_key` raises ValueError unless tier, order_sha256, build_level, copy/admission/instability text and (when given) data_sha256 agree; `replay_tower` checks the key, rebuilds, and requires the same ledger bytes (O-1, O-3). | `check_cache_key`, `replay_tower` |
| L-379 | `CarryTower.to_bytes()` = canonical JSON of every unit (carry ids, elements with origin, scope, class, L, local-space sha) and every pack + the ledger sha: what O-1 compares. | `to_bytes` |
| L-380 | A deferred activation at level >= 1 behaves as at level 0 (L-357 / L-308): the item is admitted alone, `activation_deferred` is written. | `_admit_item` |
| L-381 | `ordered_sids(sids, kind, seed)`: file / reverse / shuffle (`random.Random(seed)`, independent of the hash seed); the header records kind and seed (condition 3). | `ordered_sids` |
| L-382 | The end of the stream closes nothing (L-310): every level keeps its open unit. `level_covered(k)` / `covered_occurrences()` give the P-1 views. | `CarryTower` |

Tests (tests/line3/test_carry_tower.py, 27 after the review; with test_carry_unit.py and test_carry_space.py: 65 passed): the 4.5 example as an event sequence (seq numbers of the rollbacks 15 / to 12, of the two wakings 23 / 39; carry sets {}, {P1:0}, {P1:0,P1:1}; P1:1's vocab has no word of P1:0; U0:1: N=2, n(P1:0)=2, r0=1, n(P1:0,甲府市)=1; U1:0: n(P1:0,P1:1)=4); a hand-derived 4-level tower by injection (s0..s3, one child per unit); a natural deep tower at max_states=1; P-1 after EVERY sentence and per level; copies immutable; condition 2 (counts only from new sentences; rewriting the past with the vocab fixed leaves the later unit's space and class byte-identical); waking only by a vocab word; deferred activation at levels 0 and 1; a deferred pack is not woken by a word already seated, only by a later vocab word (review); after every sentence the copy of each open unit equals the live F(k) (review); verify_class on every unit of an S300 head (RUN, WORD, 16 sentences, low); O-1 (rebuild and `replay_tower` byte-identical, PYTHONHASHSEED 0/1/12345); O-2 (ledger-only rebuild equals the tower, net of rollbacks; 7 tamperings detected, each comparison of `ledger_mismatches` killed by at least one; a right-key ledger with other events is refused by `replay_tower`); O-3 (reversed order / other tier / other budget / other data refused, shuffle seeds).

S300 (experiments/line3/carry/c3/{c3.py,results/,logs/}; stream order = data file order, L-321; V2 default space; CPU seconds; each result also holds a per-level table):
| tier / budget | levels | units per level (level 0 first) | packs | copied (unit, pack) pairs | woken (final) | never woken | activation_deferred | sentences split | time |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RUN low | 4 | 560, 143, 26, 1 | 726 | 10786 | 1265 | 9521 | 21 | 202 (283 events) | 34 s |
| WORD low | 6 | 1086, 381, 116, 27, 2, 1 | 1607 | 21086 | 4590 | 16496 | 113 | 277 (794 events) | 86 s |
| RUN mid | 4 | 437, 73, 3, 1 | 510 | 7719 | 931 | 6788 | 12 | 157 (197 events) | 773 s |
| WORD mid | 5 | 951, 276, 36, 2, 1 | 1261 | 22290 | 3611 | 18679 | 261 | 264 (670 events) | 1581 s |
"Never woken" is counted per (unit, pack) pair net of rollbacks (copied - woken). Packs that were woken in at least one unit: RUN low 82 of 726, WORD low 221 of 1607, RUN mid 79 of 510, WORD mid 187 of 1261 (the rest never woke anywhere). The frontier check (P-1: open black scope + F(0) own_occ = every fed occurrence exactly once) was run after EVERY sentence: 0 failures in all four runs. `ledger_mismatches` (O-2) is empty in all four; all units (RUN low 730, WORD low 1613, RUN mid 514, WORD mid 1266) pass `verify_class` (0 failures); the low runs were also replayed (`replay_tower`): byte-identical (tower and ledger). Mid was not replayed (cost); the hash-seed test runs on low.
Against C2 (no carry) at the same budget: level-0 blacks RUN low 422 -> 560, WORD low 676 -> 1086, RUN mid 319 -> 437, WORD mid 524 -> 951; sentences split RUN low 140 -> 202, WORD low 249 -> 277, RUN mid 88 -> 157, WORD mid 203 -> 264. Time is dominated by level 0 as in C2 (mid: level-0 successful admits 291 s / 478 s; the rest is collapsed attempts and levels >= 1; verification of every unit costs 265 s / 410 s on top and is not in the total).

### C3 open points (meaning-level; not decided by the code)
  (1) Woken packs take capacity in the black (a woken pack is one more seat, with edges to every word it touches), so blacks close earlier than in C2: 1.3-1.9x as many level-0 blacks and more split sentences at the same budget (table above). Nothing here is a fault, but OP-1 (a) therefore does not "leave the black's capacity to the new words" as the design hoped; a smaller waking rule (e.g. wake only when the occurrence's word is a vocab word AND the pack has at least a given sentence count) would be a meaning change and is not made.
  (2) Most copied packs never wake (RUN mid 6788 of 7719 pairs, WORD mid 18679 of 22290) and 84-89 % of all packs never wake anywhere: the copy of ALL levels is large (carry median 13-14 packs per unit at low) while the waking rule is strict. Whether the copy should be limited is the owner's call; it costs nothing in the black (dormant) but the copy is stored per unit (O(frontier) per unit).
  (3) `activation_deferred` is not rare in WORD (113 low, 261 mid): the first group (word + woken packs) of a fresh black often collapses and the pack stays asleep (L-308); the information that should have been inherited is then silently absent from that unit's cross (the ledger records the event). Counted, not hidden; whether a deferred pack should be woken by the NEXT occurrence is not decided (as built it can be: the dormant set is derived, so any later occurrence of a vocab word tries again).
  (4) The top unit of the tower keeps ONE element for a long time (WORD mid: the level-4 unit holds one pack whose scope is 272 sentences) because a fresh level is opened only when the one below closes; a level's open unit therefore may be a single pack. The design allows it (L-310).
  (5) Waking at level >= 1 uses the units of the CHILD pack's scope (equal to its vocab, argued in L-375); a woken pack and a child pack that share words get n(x,y) from the unit's scope sentences, so the upper unit sees overlap through the shared words, not through the original sentences of the inherited pack (condition 2). Measured behaviour on S300 only: woken per upper unit median 3-5 at levels 1-2 (low).
  (6) The injection exists only for tests (L-376); the ledger records its reason, which must start with "injected" (enforced since the review), so an injected ledger cannot be confused with a budget one; the header does not mark it, so a loader that checks only `check_cache_key` (header) would not see it, `replay_tower` refuses it (bytes differ). Cache keys do not include the injection (it is not a build option).
  (7) Mid runs were not replayed and not run under three hash seeds (time); low runs were replayed and the hash-seed test covers 12 sentences at low in RUN and WORD.

## T8d: two ways to show the restored stable state (verantyx/line3/matryoshka.py `candidate="stable-seats"` and `"stable-seated"`, CLI `--layer-candidate`; default unchanged = `path`)
Binding: the owner's decision after T8c (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md, 「T8c の測定後の決定」, verbatim choice 「両方測ってから決める」): measure both readings of open point T8c-(1), then decide. Both are built, nothing is chosen here. Everything not in that choice is the most literal reading I could build, numbered, and listed as an open point. `path`, `bag`, `stable` are byte-identical to 505b2e9 (golden hashes in the tests: the existing path / T8 goldens plus four new hashes of `stable` produced by the 505b2e9 tree).

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-390 | `LayerOptions(candidate="stable-seats")` / `--layer-candidate stable-seats`. The upper cross is read exactly as before (so which bundles lie on the upper entries is unchanged). For EVERY bundle of every upper entry (its path bundles and its centre bundle) the lower cross packed in it is taken at its restored stable state by the unchanged L-341 boundary scan (`stable_boundary`: same units, same stability test, same cache, so the same step as `stable`), WHATEVER step that is, step 0 (the stored cross before any question unit) included, and shown as it is: `seats = (centre, arms)`, the centre word and the six arms, each arm = its words in seat order (outer seat first; an empty seat inside an arm is `null`; empty OUTER seats, i.e. the padding of a shorter cross, are dropped; the six arms are in label order, so "arm 1..6" = that order, not a geometry fact). No section path and no query attachment is needed, so a state restored to step 0 gives a candidate (the T8c problem). The candidate's `words` = the sorted words of the layout (centre and arms); a candidate is NOT a bag of path words. Per candidate the JSON records `seats = {centre, arms, n_words, n_seats}` (n_seats = occupied arm seats, so n_seats = n_words - 1), `arrangements` (distinct arrangements the restored state has, L-392), and per read `path_from[].boundary` (L-342 record), `path_from[].seats`. Entries whose structure (centre + arms) is equal are ONE entry (bundles, centres and sources are merged, stability is the maximum). No entry is ever dropped for lack of path words (`entries_without_path_words` = 0). | matryoshka.down_read_seats, layout_of, seats_obj, _read_layer
| L-391 | `LayerOptions(candidate="stable-seated")` / `--layer-candidate stable-seated`. The L-341 scan with ONE change: only the question units that take a SEAT can be a boundary step. A unit takes a seat iff it is one of the first 6 units applied (the 6 attached units; `N_SEATED = 6`, the same line L-341 draws for `unstable_unit_attached`); the later units only add energy: they are applied (all of them, in order) but never end a scan. The boundary = the step before the first SEATED unit that makes the cross unstable (steps 1..min(n, 6) are read prefix by prefix, the first unstable one stops the scan). If no seated unit breaks the cross there is no boundary and no restore: the state read is the full-question read (all n units applied), which `stable` would have restored to step >= 6 when the cross broke at an energy-only unit. The state read is then read for path words exactly as `stable` (L-340, L-342, L-344: same entries, same union, same `without path words` rule). The boundary record has the L-342 keys plus `mode: "seated"`, `seated_units` (= min(n, 6)) and `state_stable` (is the state read stable? false only when an energy-only unit broke it and nothing was restored). A restored state is verified as a fixed point (L-343) as before; an unstable full-question read is not claimed to be one (it is read exactly as `path` reads it, and not put in the fixed-point check). Because the 6 seated units always come first, the mode differs from `stable` only for a cross whose first break is at the 7th unit or later. | matryoshka.stable_boundary(seated=True), down_read_stable(seated=True)
| L-392 | What one stable-seats candidate is: one candidate per BUNDLE of an upper entry = ONE restored state (a state is a class of tied arrangements, L-70; the owner's L-90 rule says arrangements that only permute equivalent words are not counted separately). The layout shown = the first arrangement in canonical order among the distinct arrangements of the state (the member end states at the restored step, or the members of the stored cross at step 0, capped by effort as in L-251); `arrangements` = how many distinct ones the state has. The other arrangements are not listed as other candidates. | matryoshka.state_flats
| L-393 | Layers >= 2: the cross read at layer j >= 1 holds layer-j bundles; a seat shows the BASE seed word of its bundle (`_centre_word`), not the words under it, and the cross is not read further down. A layer-2 candidate is therefore the layer-1 cross as laid out, in seed words. Measured: standard has 137 layer-2 runs (fast none), see open point (5). | matryoshka.down_read_seats
| L-394 | Provenance and trace (extends L-253, L-344): every word of a layout keeps source sentences. The sources of a word = the sentences that contain both it and a neighbouring occupied seat (along its arm; the inner end of an arm touches the centre; the centre takes the sentences of its inner arm seats); a word with no such sentence (a gap in the arm) keeps all its own sentences. `trace_run` checks that the words of an entry are exactly the words of its `word_sources`, that every source sentence holds the word, that every word descends through the bundle to a base state, and, for a restored step >= 1, that the restored state is a fixed point for all members (L-343). Step 0 is the stored cross (stable by construction, N-05) and has no read to check. Measured 100 % (every upper run traced, below). | matryoshka.seat_sources, trace_run
| L-395 | JSON and defaults: the new keys (`seats`, `mode`) appear only for the two new modes; `candidate="stable"` / `path` / `bag` JSON and bytes are unchanged (hashes in the tests). The options JSON shows `candidate` = the mode name; each upper entry of the new modes has `candidate` = `stable-seats` / `stable-seated`. CLI: `--layer-candidate {path,bag,stable,stable-seats,stable-seated}` (cli.py: the flag's value list and help only). | matryoshka.LayerEntry, cli.py
| L-396 | Cost: stable-seats reads the same prefixes as `stable` (it reuses `stable_boundary`, with the same cache key, so a process that has run `stable` first has them for free) and then does NOT read the path words, so it needs no recursion into lower layers (2527 lower reads instead of 3533 in the standard sweep). stable-seated reads at most 6 prefixes plus one full-question read, never the 7th..n-th prefixes. | matryoshka.stable_boundary

### Tests (tests/line3/test_matryoshka.py, "T8d"; test_matryoshka.py + test_ask.py: 79 passed)
Defaults byte-identical (the existing path goldens + four `stable` goldens made by the 505b2e9 tree + the T8 bag bytes); stable-seats: every entry's seats equal an independently re-derived restored state (fresh prefix scan, then the canonical-first arrangement laid out) for every lower read of the entry, the words of an entry = the placed units of the packed cross (a move only permutes seats), n_seats = n_words - 1, a state restored to step 0 gives a candidate, equal structures are one entry, no float, `without_path_words == 0`, trace ok; stable-seated: no recorded boundary lies at an energy-only unit (`first_unstable_step <= 6`, `unstable_unit_attached`), and a unit-level test with a monkeypatched stability test (the 8th unit breaks the cross) shows `stable` restoring to step 7 and `stable-seated` not restoring (last step 9, `state_stable`), while a break at the 3rd unit is the same boundary and the same backup in both; PYTHONHASHSEED 0 / 1 / 12345 give equal bytes for both modes; layer-2 candidates trace to base words; the CLI accepts both values.

### T8d measurement (S300, 90 questions: 60 answerable, 30 unanswerable; experiments/line3/t8d/results/summary_fast.md, summary_standard.md; raw .jsonl git-ignored; harness experiments/line3/t8d/{measure.py,summarize.py}, 6 worker processes, 1-min load 8-13)
bag / path / stable A are the T8c sweep's records (the same code: stable A re-run on 6 questions gave identical entries; stable is byte-identical on the toy), seated / seats are new runs on the same questions (layer 0 re-read, unchanged).
Gold in some candidate (answerable 60), layers off / T8 bag A / T8b path A / T8c stable A / **T8d stable-seats A** / **T8d stable-seated A**: fast 28 / 36 / 28 / 28 / **36** / **28**; standard 29 / 38 / 30 / 29 / **38** / **29**. Gold gained vs off: stable-seats +8 (fast) and +9 (standard), the same questions as the T8 bag (the words of a restored state are the units of the cross, whichever step); stable-seated +0 and +0 (= stable). Unanswerable (no candidate / only a list / a single wrong answer): fast off 4/22/4, bag 3/26/1, path 4/24/2, stable 4/23/3, **seats 3/27/0**, seated 4/23/3; standard off 3/25/2, bag 1/28/1, path 3/26/1, stable 3/26/1, **seats 1/29/0**, seated 3/26/1.
Price of stable-seats (the list the user reads): upper candidates per question (answerable, median / max) fast bag 1 / 7, path 1 / 3, stable 0.5 / 3, **seats 6.5 / 18**; standard bag 6 / 37, path 4 / 9, stable 3 / 8, **seats 24.5 / 43**. List size (lists >= 2, median / max): fast bag 7 / 53, **seats 13 / 62**; standard bag 15 / 76, **seats 35 / 93** (layers off 6 / 51 and 7 / 69). Words per upper candidate (answerable, median / p90 / max): fast **seats 34 / 81 / 104** (bag 93.5 / 143 / 165); standard **seats 15 / 76 / 104** (bag 118.5 / 166 / 191); by tier (all questions, standard) RUN 4 / 9 / 13, WORD 10 / 22 / 35, CHAR 47 / 91 / 104 (fast RUN 4 / 9 / 9, WORD 14 / 28 / 35, CHAR 62 / 94 / 104). Seats per candidate: always words - 1 (standard median 15, p90 75, max 103; fast 36 / 85 / 103). Arrangements in a restored state (median / max): standard 6 / 4368, fast 2 / 192. The gold-holding upper candidates are small: words median / max fast 4.5 / 10, standard 7 / 12 (seats 3.5 / 9 and 6 / 11). How far down the list the gold-holding candidate sits (first gold-holding candidate, answerable questions with gold): in the whole list (layer-0 entries first) fast seats median 1 / p90 9 / max 14 of a list of median 16 / max 62 (bag 1 / 5 / 11 of 8 / 53; layers off 1 / 2 / 4 of 7 / 51); standard seats 1 / 25 / 38 of 40.5 / 93 (bag 1 / 13 / 28 of 17.5 / 76; off 1 / 2 / 4 of 11 / 69). Counted among the UPPER candidates only (the questions whose gold is held by an upper candidate: 8 fast, 9 standard): fast seats position median 6 / p90 12 / max 14 among median 10 / max 18 upper candidates (bag 2 / 4 / 6 among 3 / 7); standard seats 17 / 21 / 23 among 25 / 43 (bag 4 / 10 / 34 among 6 / 37). Rule-B reference (right/wrong/abstain): fast seats 1/2/87 (off 4/11/75), standard seats 0/0/90 (off 1/5/84). Time added per question (median, inflated by load): fast seats 12.6 s, seated 10.9 s, stable 13.0 s, path 6.7 s; standard seats 59.6 s, seated 64.0 s, stable 61.1 s, path 37.7 s. Trace 100 % in every upper run of every configuration (fast 208/208, standard 345/345); every restored state a fixed point. stable-seats lists no entry dropped (0 of 623 fast / 2155 standard entries lack words or sources), against 117 / 748 entries dropped for stable.
stable-seated boundary distribution: lower reads fast 687, standard 3533 (the same reads as `stable`). Restored: stable fast 491, standard 2390; **stable-seated fast 488, standard 2373** (the 3 / 17 breaks at an energy-only unit are no longer restored). Of the restored: **398 of 488 (82 %) fast and 1838 of 2373 (77 %) standard still restore to STEP 0** (first seated unit already breaks the cross; 52 % of all lower reads in standard); last stable step histogram standard {0: 1838, 1: 193, 2: 111, 3: 110, 4: 55, 5: 66} (fast {0: 398, 1: 26, 2: 10, 3: 8, 4: 22, 5: 24}); no boundary at step >= 6. The energy-only break cases (not restored, so the state read is the full-question read): 8 reads in standard and 2 in fast end with an unstable full-question state. Entries (upper entries with path words / dropped for none) are identical to `stable`: fast 56 / 117, standard 273 / 748. So skipping energy-only units changes nothing measurable: the restore to step 0 is caused by the seated units, mostly the first one.

### T8d open points (meaning-level; not decided by the code)
  (1) Which candidate unit: built as one candidate per bundle of an upper entry (L-390), each the one state of that bundle. The alternative "one candidate per upper entry = all its bundles' states together" would give the T8 bag's list shape (1 / 7, 6 / 37 per question) with the layout kept per bundle; the per-bundle shape multiplies the list (6.5 / 18 and 24.5 / 43 upper candidates per question) while the gold-holding ones stay small (words 4.5-7 median).
  (2) One state = one candidate (L-392): the other tied arrangements of the state are not listed. Listing every distinct arrangement (up to 4368 in a state) would make the list longer still; the owner's L-90 rule suggests not. The canonical-first arrangement is only the representative; it is not "better".
  (3) A layout shows the six arms in label order with the empty outer padding dropped; whether arms should be in geometric order (arm index of the cross), whether empty seats should be shown, and whether two layouts that are rotations / reflections of each other are one candidate are not decided (built: arms as sorted labels, so rotations of the same state are one candidate).
  (4) The words of a stable-seats candidate = all the units of the packed cross (a move permutes seats; the question never adds a unit to the flat), so the gold-holding set equals the T8 bag's (+8 / +9, the same questions); what is new is the layout and the per-bundle split, not the information.
