(6) In build.py the stop build of a cross runs just before its skip build in the same worker and warms the shared `Weights` cache (a pure cache), so the skip CPU is if anything understated; percentiles in summary.md are index int(p*n) of the sorted list (a nearest-rank variant). The doc claims of F1 about "CPU 2-7x" refer to F1's machine; the F1c ratios are same-machine Air timings and are not comparable to the Pro totals in summary.md (a "stop total (Pro)" column is shown for orientation only).

## G2-f (2026-10-09): the grammar layer's particle records, kinds, question slot and pattern, grammar cross, alignment and read order (verantyx/line3/grammar.py; tests/line3/test_grammar.py; docs/LINE3_G2_AXES_AND_GRAMMAR_LAYER.md 4, docs/LINE3_G3_SLIDING_PACKS.md 4)
Owner (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md): 「助詞などを文法層という別の層を作ってそこから読むのと読む際に十字の種類で読む順番などを調整する」; 「文法層も粒度を変えたものと因果関係を持たせて全体をパックするソブリンの中で回るようにします」; 「粒度のパターンからの未知への対応を含めたもの」; 「軸では腕のラベルとして、文法層では十字ごとの参照として」; 「文法層の十字の回転を「手」にして、助詞の腕をデータの腕に合わせる」; 「梯子の順に x、y、z へ。重みは辺の流れと配置の結合と読む順に」. One new module and its test; nothing is hooked into ask / cycle / placement / matryoshka / cli (a later ticket), so every existing output is untouched. Gamma is not evidence (I-G2-2): it holds no space, no n / N / r0, and answers nothing; it only orders what is read.

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-540 | Heads and the foundation. A record is made for every occurrence of a CONTENT unit (not `funcwords.is_function_unit`) of the RUN and WORD tiers; the particle must be in P7 = は の に を が で と (G1, hand-written, not UniDic tags); a P7 particle is itself a function unit, so it is never a head. Heads fulllead: WORD 7892, RUN 5027. This is the definition that gives the doc's 2314 (a head that is a function word, e.g. 「は」 followed by 「の」, has no record). | grammar.P7, build_records |
| L-541 | CHAR excluded from the records: adjacency is defined on the WORD cut (L-542), and the next WORD unit after a character is mostly inside the same word. CHAR (and WORD) are used on the question side only, as the parts of the granularity pattern (L-550). | grammar.RECORD_TIERS |
| L-542 | Adjacency (L-G2-2): for a unit with span [a, b) of the attribution-stripped sentence (L-44), in either tier, the follower is the first WORD unit that starts at or after b (`granularity._raw_spans(WORD)`: letter-bearing tokens, punctuation skipped, function words kept); it is recorded iff it is in P7. A WORD unit covering b (the unit ends inside a WORD: a RUN stem such as 食 of 食べる) is the label `straddle`: nothing is decided and it is COUNTED (`Records.follow[tier]`: P7 particles / other / end / straddle add up to the heads, tested). fulllead: WORD 2314 attachments (の 623, に 456, は 357, で 292, を 214, と 209, が 163), straddle 0; RUN 2134 attachments, straddle 353 of 5027 (G1 counted 1491 on RUN's own cut; the WORD cut gives more, as G1 2.4(3) predicted). | grammar.follower, _build |
| L-543 | Kind: the particle with the largest attachment count over the unit's records (counts of occurrences, not of sentences); two or more at the maximum = `tied`, a labelled group (`Kind("tied", particles, count)`), never broken by order (the particles are listed in P7 order, which is a label, not a winner; tested with reversed insertion and reversed sentence order); no P7 follower = `none`. fulllead WORD types: 1118 particle (の 297, に 205, は 195, で 130, を 114, と 101, が 76) / 178 tied / 2261 none (= 3557). RUN: 1605 / 109 / 1939. This is the kind of a UNIT (= of a layer-0 cross with that centre, G2 4.1), over all of P7 including は; the kind of a WINDOW is over the six arms only (L-549). For the owner only (not used): the unit kind over the six arms would be 965 particle / 150 tied / 2442 none. | grammar.kind_of_counts, Records.kind |
| L-544 | Provenance and bytes. `Attachment(tier, sid, start, end, unit, particle, p_start, p_end)`; `Records.attachments` is sorted by that tuple (canonical order, never a ranking); counts per (tier, unit, particle) and per (tier, unit) (`counts`, `n_head`, `n_attached`) are derived from the attachments and the head table. `trace_check(records, sentence_text)` re-reads every attachment against its sentence (surfaces, nothing letter-like between unit and particle, head not a function unit, counts add up): [] on all 2314 + 2134. `to_bytes()` of every object = canonical JSON (sort_keys, compact, ensure_ascii False, UTF-8), Fractions as "n/d" strings, tuples as lists, a float raises; format `line3.grammar.v1`. | grammar.Attachment, Records, trace_check, _canon |
| L-545 | The interrogatives are a hand-written list (INTERROG, copied from experiments/line3/g2/probe_particles.py: 何 なに なん どこ 誰 だれ いつ どの どれ いくつ どう どんな いくら なぜ 何故 どちら). Slot = the first interrogative WORD extended over the content WORD units glued to it (no character between, so 何+年 = 何年; a punctuation mark ends the phrase), and the P7 particle right after the phrase (L-542); otherwise `particle=None` with `after` saying what follows (other:です, other:位, end). fulllead: 91 of 94 questions have an interrogative; intra2 20 of 69 have a P7 slot (の 6, と 6, に 5, を 3), unans 5 of 25 (all に). `QuestionReading.frames` = the particle after every content WORD unit of the question (は 81, の 68, で 30, に 29, と 16, を 15, が 13). | grammar.slot_of, read_question |
| L-546 | The gold side (used by the test and by G2-g): `answer_particle(sentence, alternatives)` = the first alternative found by `str.find` in the stripped evidence sentence, and the P7 particle after its end by L-542 (label particle / other / end / straddle / notfound; the probe's MIDTOKEN = straddle). intra2: the slot particle equals the gold's particle in 11 of the 20 slotted questions (の 5/6, に 3/5, を 2/3, と 1/6); the chance expectation from the marginals (sum over P7 of slot count x gold count / 69) is exactly 139/69 (the doc: 2.0; tested as the Fraction). | grammar.answer_particle |
| L-547 | `records_of_space(space)` = the records of the BASE sentences of a Space (memory sentences are not corpus text), sids kept as the space's; `build_records(texts)` numbers the sentences by position (L-33). Equal bytes for a base-only space. | grammar.records_of_space |
| L-548 | The predicate slot 「XのYは何ですか」 (OP-G2-7 (c)) is built as a SEPARATE field `QuestionReading.predicate` (y, x, spans) and is never merged into `slot`, so the slot counts of L-545 do not move. Rule: no P7 slot, and the interrogative is right after は; Y = the contiguous content WORD units ending at the unit before は (plus at most one function unit at the end: 長+さ, 高+さ), X = the same kind of run before the の that precedes Y (the leading function unit of 「この山」 is dropped); a cleft 「…のは何」 (the unit before は is の) gives None. 50 of the 94 fulllead questions have one. The read order does NOT use it: a question with only a predicate slot has no `match` group (OP-G2-7 (c) is not the owner's choice yet; OP-G2-7 (a) is the default). | grammar.predicate_of, read_order |
| L-549 | The grammar cross of a window placeholder (`grammar_cross(records, window_id, sids, tiers=("WORD",))`): `window` is an opaque id (slide.py does not exist yet), `sids` its sentences; the cross is a `geometry.Cross` with L=1 (centre 「は」 233/377; arms in geometry.AXES order +x -x +y -y +z -z = の に で と を が, the owner's 「梯子の順に x、y、z へ」, OP-G3-6 (a)), carried BY REFERENCE: `foundation` = the sha256 of `foundation_obj()` (centre, arms, exact weights); the weights are never copied into a ratio. `attachments` = the window's records, `seats(p)` = the distinct (tier, unit) of the arm p in sentence-position order, `counts` per P7 particle (including は, the centre: its records are kept), `kind` = kind_of_counts of the counts of the SIX ARM particles only (G3 4.2: kind(W_N) = {p: |腕 p|}, the six arms = F's six particles, the centre は is a reference with no arm; the owner's 「助詞の腕をデータの腕に合わせる」 and 「梯子の順に x、y、z へ」 put the six on arms and は on none). Changed in review from the G2 4.1 kind over P7, which stays for a unit's kind (L-543). Consequence: a slot は never matches a window's kind (a unit's kind can still be は). `seats(p)` is in attachment order (tier, sid, start), i.e. sentence position order within a tier. Default tier WORD: summing RUN and WORD would count one sentence's adjacency twice. | grammar.GrammarCross, grammar_cross |
| L-550 | The question's granularity pattern and stand-ins (G1 3.2, G3 4.4): for each content RUN unit u of the question (span [a, b)): `word_parts` = the content WORD units and `char_parts` = the content CHAR units of u cut AS A STRING (G1: 「u を WORD の区切りで切った」; the cut in the sentence's context differs for 3 units and does not reproduce G1's 92), each with `known` = has a posting in the space; type T1 u is in the space; T2 unknown, >= 1 WORD part and all known; T3 unknown, some known; T4 no WORD part known, some CHAR part known; T5 nothing known. Stand-ins (`standins_of`): the units v of the RUN tier (in the space) whose span in some sentence contains the span of an occurrence of a known part, via WORD parts for T2 / T3 (G1 L_RW) and via CHAR parts for T4 (G1 L_RC); T5 has none; every occurrence is listed as `Standin(tier, unit, sid, span, part_tier, part, part_span)` sorted by (unit, sid, span), no cap (N-20), `granularity-derived`. Chance count: read as `n_standins` (distinct units) with `n_sentences` and `pool` (RUN units in the space), so the chance that a random unit is a stand-in is n_standins/pool, an exact fraction for the reader; the G1 偶然の当たり control (other questions' golds hit) is G2-g's. Reproduced on fulllead: 139 unknown RUN units of 94 questions; >= 1 WORD part known 92, all 74, >= 1 CHAR part known 118, no stand-in via WORD 47 (all as G1 2.4(5)); types T2 74 / T3 18 / T4 32 / T5 15 (G3's estimate T4 26 / T5 21 is replaced by the count). Stand-ins per unknown word via WORD (zero for T4 / T5, as G1), over all 139: median 2, p90 119, max 136 (p90 = sorted[(9 n) // 10], the index G1's numbers use; the G3-a/b convention sorted[(9 (n - 1)) // 10] gives 86). G1 2.4(5) / 3.2 wrote median 3, p90 132, max 151: the review found the cause, they are STRING containment (the RUN units of the default-filtered space whose string contains a known WORD part: 3 / 132 / 151 / zero 47 exactly; tested), e.g. 年代記 counted for the part 年 although the WORD cut there is 年代+記 and no WORD 年 lies inside. G1 3.2's own definition is span containment (「F2 の SpanIndex、L-480 の span の包含。文字列の部分一致ではない」), so 2 / 119 / 136 is the right number and G1's are a measurement slip; every span stand-in is also a string stand-in (tested). Also: string containment on the `unit_filter=None` space gives max 170, so G1's numbers came from the filtered space although G1 2.4 says it used `unit_filter=None`. | grammar._pattern, standins_of |
| L-551 | The 24 rotations and the alignment (decision G3 5: the grammar cross's rotation is a move that aligns the particle arms with the data arms). The moves are exactly `geometry.G24` (24 proper rotations, tested against an independent signed-permutation enumeration; indices are labels, L-16). The data side is abstract: six `{particle: n}` counters in world-direction order (AXES), built by `data_arm_counts(records, arms, sids)` = the exact number of attachments of the units seated on each data arm (optionally only those in the window's sentences). Score of a rotation r = sum over the six grammar arms i of counts[r(i)][particle_i] (grammar arm i, labelled with its particle, faces data arm r(i); the centre は faces the data centre in every rotation and adds one constant, so it is left out); an integer, the weights do not enter. Only the 24 are scored; the maximum is returned with EVERY rotation that reaches it (`best_indices`), never one by index. | grammar.align, align_score, data_arm_counts |
| L-552 | Read order (G2 4.2 step 2, G3 4.2 steps 2-3): `read_order(question | slot particle | None, [(id, Kind)])` returns groups, in this order: (1) `match`: the single-particle kind equal to the question's slot (only when the slot is a P7 particle); (2) the other single-particle kinds, one group per particle by the ladder weight (は 233, の 144, に 89, で 55, と 34, を 21, が 13; は ranks first although it is the centre); (3) `tied` kinds, one group per distinct tied set, the sets ordered by their descending ladder weights compared in turn; (4) `none`. A tied kind never matches a slot (L-G2-9); candidates with the same kind are one group, whatever their input order, and the kind's own count is not a tie-break; ids inside a group are sorted for byte identity only. The set of candidates is unchanged (only an order). Tied before none, and tied after every single-particle kind, is this ticket's choice (a tied kind is the weaker evidence). | grammar.read_order |
| L-553 | Scope and determinism: the module imports `granularity._raw_spans` / `SpanIndex`, `funcwords`, `space`, `geometry` and nothing of ask / cycle / placement / matryoshka / energy / carry / cli (tested); pure functions of their arguments; no float, no division, no set / dict-order dependence (tested by AST and by the output bytes under PYTHONHASHSEED 0 / 1 / 12345: records, every question reading with its pattern, a grammar cross, an alignment, a read order). Importing grammar loads none of the question path (subprocess `sys.modules` check), and no question-path module imports grammar (AST, tested). 38 tests, 2 s; the suite passes under PYTHONHASHSEED 0 / 1 / 12345. | tests/line3/test_grammar.py |

### G2-f open points (owner; meaning level)
(1) RUN particles are read on the WORD cut (L-542): 353 of the 5027 RUN heads end inside a WORD (a verb stem such as 食 of 食べる) and get no record; a RUN unit followed by a conjugation ending then has no 助詞 to carry a kind. Is that the intended meaning of 「直後の助詞」 for the RUN tier, or should a stem take the particle after the whole WORD (食べる → を)? (2) The predicate slot of 「XのYは何ですか」 (50 of 94 questions) is extracted but unused in the read order (OP-G2-7 (c) is not decided; the data side of such a question is not a particle: gold followers in intra2 です-questions are 文末 8 / が 7 / と 4 / で 4 / content 4 ...). (3) Where tied and none sit in the read order, and whether は (the centre) as a kind ranks first, are this ticket's choices (L-552); the owner's 「読む順に」 weights only say the ladder is used. (4) The alignment (L-551) reads 「データの腕のラベルに一致する付着」 as: the units seated on a data arm whose follower particle equals the particle of the grammar arm that faces it. The data arms' own labels (G2-b / G3-c) are not defined yet, so the data side is an abstract six-counter input; when axes exist, say whether the data arm's label should be the ladder label of its axis (then the score is the number of attachments agreeing with the arm's fixed label, and the rotation chooses which grammar arm is read against it). (5) Resolved in review (L-550): G1 2.4(5)'s 3 / 132 / 151 are string containment, G1 3.2's definition (span containment) gives 2 / 119 / 136; G1's numbers should be read as a slip of its probe (and its `unit_filter=None` note is not what produced them). Which p90 index the line-3 docs use (G1 / G2-f sorted[(9 n) // 10] vs G3-a/b sorted[(9 (n - 1)) // 10]; 119 vs 86 here) is not fixed anywhere. (8) A window's kind is over the six arms (L-549, G3 4.2); G3 4.2 step 2 says 「kind(W_N) が slot を含む窓」 and kind(W_N) is written as the multiset {p: |腕 p|}: "含む" can be read as (i) the single most frequent arm is the slot (built here, L-G2-9), (ii) the slot is among the most frequent (a tied set containing it matches), or (iii) the slot's arm is not empty. (9) The alignment score (L-551) is a plain count; the ladder weights do not enter it (the owner's 「重みは辺の流れと配置の結合と読む順に」 does not name the alignment). (6) Kind counts occurrences (records), not distinct sentences: a unit repeated in one sentence counts twice (L-543). (7) CHAR has no records (L-541); a character-level kind is a later question.

## G3-a/b (2026-10-09): the sliding windows and the exact counts of their three axes (verantyx/line3/slide.py; tests/line3/test_slide.py; experiments/line3/g3/reach_windows.py, reach_windows.txt)
Owner (G3 answers 1-4 and 5-6, ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md): 「文ごとの 3 層パック。この文と次の文。1 文ずつ重ねて進む」; 「同じ記事の隣り合う 2 文」; 「x＝語順、y＝粒度、z＝スライド」; 「梯子の順に x、y、z へ。重みは辺の流れと配置の結合と読む順に」. Nothing here is hooked into ask / placement / cycle (G3-c/d); the Space is only read (its sha is asserted unchanged); no existing output, cache name or test changes. Design: docs/LINE3_G3_SLIDING_PACKS.md 2, 3.2, 3.5, 7.2.

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-520 | Article rule (makes L-G3-9 strict). An article = the consecutive rows with the same title in `source` ("title#i"), and the number must be previous + 1. ValueError (no silent merge / split / skip) for: a source that is not "title#i"; a row whose `title` field disagrees with its source; a title that comes back after another article; a number that skips or repeats. fulllead is clean (300 articles, 592 sentences, 0 gaps, 0 split blocks, titles = source titles). | slide.articles |
| L-521 | Window lists. `pairs()` (alias `windows()`) = the adjacent sentence pairs (N, N+1) of one article in data-file order, W_N with N = the sid of the first sentence (fulllead 292; the last sentence of an article forms no pair; no pair crosses an article end). The one-sentence windows are RECORDED, not decided: `singletons()` (one-sentence articles, 125), `lasts()` (the last sentence of every article, 300, contains the singletons), `lone()` = what the spec's rule asks for (`WindowRule.lone` in none / last / singleton; default "last" = L-G3-10, so `sequence()` = pairs + lone in W_N order = 592 on fulllead, the design's "窓 592・2 文の窓 292"). A one-sentence window has an empty z (its units are all "this"). Measured: the 69 intra2 reach is 45 (RUN 44) with the pairs alone, with the singletons and with every last sentence (the golds are all in multi-sentence articles), so bank2 cannot decide it (open point 1). | slide.Slide.pairs / singletons / lasts / lone / sequence |
| L-522 | Pack of a window = every occurrence (tier, sid, k, unit, start, end) of RUN, WORD and CHAR of both sentences, ordered (sid, tier ladder RUN > WORD > CHAR, k); repeats keep all their places; spans from granularity.SpanIndex (L-480). Provenance is checked: text[start:end] == unit and sentence_units[k] == unit for every occurrence (fulllead included). | slide.Slide.pack, Occ |
| L-523 | x (word order). Same tier, same sentence, every ordered pair (o, i) of distinct units of the pack; the place of a unit is its FIRST occurrence (L-G3-3, the p_pair rule). key (tier, o, i) -> (before, after): `before` = sources where o's first occurrence precedes i's (= p(o,i), the +x reading, outer first), `after` = where i precedes o (= p(i,o)); n_x = len(before) + len(after); (i, o) is the mirror. A source is (sid, o_start, o_end, i_start, i_end). In corpus scope n_x equals Space n_pair and `before` equals p_pair (asserted for every key). | slide.Slide._x |
| L-524 | y (granularity). o of a coarser tier, i of a finer tier (pairs RUN>WORD, RUN>CHAR, WORD>CHAR; the spec lists them; RUN>CHAR is the transitive pair the probe did not count, it is in the key set but not in any reach number). A sentence counts once for (o, i) if ANY occurrence of o has a span that contains ANY occurrence of i (not only the first ones: the probe's 17 of 69 needs this, first occurrences only give 13 of 69; the 6 of 10 is 6 either way (review, 2026-10-09); e.g. "京は東京である" has 京 at (0,1) outside 東京 (2,4) and again at (3,4) inside). Containment is non-strict (equal spans count: RUN 東京 and WORD 東京 cut the same string), the source is the first containing pair in (k_o, k_i) order: (sid, o_start, o_end, i_start, i_end). `Slide.contain(...)` is the one function; reach_windows uses it. Units of one tier have no y edge. | slide.Slide._y / contain |
| L-525 | z (slide). For a pair window (N, N+1): u in sentence N and v in sentence N+1, any two tiers (9 combinations; the spec parameter z.tiers = "all", "same" = 3), first occurrence of each as the source (sid_N, sid_N+1, u_start, u_end, v_start, v_end). +z(o=u, i=v), -z(o=v, i=u) is the same key read the other way. n_z in corpus scope = the number of article pairs (N, N+1) over the whole corpus with u in N and v in N+1 (never N+2, never across an article end: asserted over every source of every toy window and by verify_counts). A unit in both sentences makes an edge with itself (u = v, e.g. 表 -> 表). `z_side[(tier, unit)]` = ("this",), ("next",) or ("this", "next"). | slide.Slide._z / _z_side |
| L-526 | Two scopes. "window": the evidence inside this window only (x, y: its two sentences; z: its own pair, once). "corpus" (L-G3-1): seats are the pack's units, the counts are taken over the whole corpus. A key with no source is absent (count 0); every count is the length of its source tuple (int). | slide.Slide.counts |
| L-527 | The spec (`SlideSpec`): corpus sha (Space.sha256()), window rule (span 2, same-article, lone rule, id and order in words), the three axis definitions (text + the parameters the counting reads: x occurrence=first tiers=same; y containment=non-strict occurrence=any pairs; z occurrence=first tiers=all|same; a value that is not built is a ValueError) and the foundation. sha256 = sha of the canonical bytes; `short()` = the 12 hex digits for a cache key (`_slide-<sha12>`, L-G3-8). A Slide refuses a spec made for another corpus. Changing any axis text or parameter, the window rule, the corpus, a label, a ladder position, a weight or the foundation name changes the sha (tested one by one). Not in the spec (review, 2026-10-09): the scope (window / corpus, L-526) is an argument of `counts()` and is written in each counts document, not in the spec; L-G3-1 fixes corpus for the cross, so G3-c must carry the scope it reads in the spec or the cache key. | slide.SlideSpec, default_spec |
| L-528 | Serialisation = canonical JSON as Space.to_bytes (sorted keys, compact, ensure_ascii False, UTF-8); Fractions as "p/q" strings; a float anywhere raises TypeError (checked by type name so the module never names the type). `SlideSpec.to_bytes`, `Slide.to_bytes(pack=True)` (spec, spec sha, articles, pair windows with their packs, lone windows, singletons), `WindowCounts.to_bytes` (keys in tier order, then code point; sources ascending). No set or dict is emitted without sorting. Identical under PYTHONHASHSEED 0 / 1 / 12345 (a subprocess digest of spec + slide + counts of all 6 toy windows in both scopes). | slide.canonical |
| L-529 | Foundation P7 (OP-G3-6, 「梯子の順に x、y、z へ。重みは辺の流れと配置の結合と読む順に」). Ladder order は の に で と を が (the order of the share of fulllead WORD sentences holding the particle: 446 399 341 260 193 162 143, strictly decreasing, asserted on the old space where particles are units), weights F(14-k)/F(14) = 233 144 89 55 34 21 13 over 377 as exact Fractions (13/377 is held as 1/29: the doc therefore also writes `per` = 377 and the numerators). は is the centre side (on no arm); the next six go on +x -x +y -y +z -z in ladder order: の 144, に 89, で 55, と 34, を 21, が 13; axis weights 233 : 89 : 34 (/377). Labels sit on arms, never as seats; the doc records weights_enter = edge_flow, binding, read_order and weights_not_in = n, N, r0, E_Q, key (I-G3-2). Ties between weights, a float weight, a label twice, arms out of order are refused. Only the spec carries the foundation; no count reads it. | slide.Foundation, p7 |
| L-530 | `verify_counts(slide, counts)` (I-G3-1): every source is re-read against the sentence surface (the span shows the unit), x: the cited spans are the first occurrences and their claimed order holds (first-occurrence check added at review), z: the cited spans are the first occurrences, y: the outer span contains the inner span, z: (N, N+1) of one article; window scope: every source lies in the window; corpus scope: x totals equal n_pair and p_pair. ValueError at the first failure. Tested to accept 100 % of the toy counts (both scopes, all windows) and fulllead window 0, and to reject a wrong span, a false "before", an x or z source that cites a later occurrence, a non-containing y, a z over two non-neighbours and a z of another window. | slide.verify_counts |
| L-531 | reach_windows.py (G3-a) formalises probe_hops.py, which stays frozen. Same reach definitions and every counted number. Different: (1) question units are cut from bank2.tsv (cycle.split_question + the tier filter + make_context), not read from the T9 audit records; `Reach.t9_check()` repeats the probe's proof that they are equal (207 / 207) when the records are present; (2) the a2 windows and the articles come from slide.py (`Slide.sequence()`, 592 windows, 292 of them pairs), the y link from `Slide.contain`; (3) no float: medians and means are exact Fractions printed as the probe printed them (an even count as x.0 / x.5, a mean with one decimal, half up), the p90 index is (9 * (n - 1)) // 10; (4) nothing from a set without sorted(): the probe printed the "article" column of its per-item lines from a set, so two lines of probe_hops.txt (I2-039, R2-I019) changed with the hash seed (no number affected); at review (2026-10-09) the probe's print was sorted (`sorted(arts)[:2]`, nothing else changed) and probe_hops.txt regenerated, byte-identical under hash seeds 0 / 1 / 12345; (5) nothing cut silently: the probe printed only the first 2 articles (column 3) and first 4 gold sentences (column 5) of a per-item line with no count; reach_windows prints both whole (only I2-039 was cut: 6 and 6). The test compares the whole report with the committed probe_hops.txt: every line byte-equal but I2-039, whose columns 3 / 5 in the probe are the first 2 / 4 entries of ours; reach_windows.txt is the report, diffed byte-identical under hash seeds 0 / 1 / 12345 by running the script (the test compares in process). | experiments/line3/g3/reach_windows.py |

### G3-a/b reproduced numbers (fulllead_sents, bank2 intra2 69 / unans 25; Pro, reach_windows 9 s, tests 33 s for test_slide + test_granularity)
Reach (same-tier RUN or WORD; RUN only; of the 10 two-hop): s1 592 windows 41 (0), RUN 39; a2 (N, N+1 inside the article) 592 windows 45 (4), RUN 44 (4); f2 (file order) 591: 45 (4), 44; a3 (3 sentences) 592: 51 (10), RUN 50; whole article 300: 51 (10), 50; one-hop 41, two-hop 10, tok 18. Chance (other golds reached per question, median / mean of 68): 0 / 0.9, 1 / 1.7, 2 / 2.2, 1 / 1.9, 1 / 2.0. Granularity link (F2 containment): 6 of the 10 two-hop (I2-020 I2-039 I2-043 R2-I002 R2-I007 R2-I012), 17 of 69 over the corpus, 6 new over the 41; union with a2 49 of 69, two-hop covered 8 (R2-I003 R2-I004 left); chance 0 / 0.9. Windows: 592 (292 pairs + 300 last-sentence windows, 125 one-sentence articles), RUN pack median 15 (p90 25, max 42), 3-tier sum 68 (p90 111, max 168), one sentence 37.5; windows read per question s1 5 (p90 16, max 23), a2 8 (24, 41), unans 3 (max 18 / 26); RUN units a question faces: 45 -> 71 (intra2), 30 -> 49 (unans).
Not part of the design's numbers, measured here: 7541 RUN>WORD containment links in fulllead, 2381 (32 %) with equal spans; WORD>CHAR 15992, 2055 (13 %). Counting only strictly larger spans the y numbers stay 17 of 69 / 6 new / 6 of 10.

### G3-a/b tests (tests/line3/test_slide.py: 41 passed; with tests/line3/test_granularity.py 67 passed; the Pro, hash seeds 0 / 1 / 12345)
Toy (3 articles: 3, 2 and 1 sentence): article list, pair list, ids, numbers inside the article, no pair across an article end, lone windows (none / last / singleton) and W_N order; the article rule's refusals; pack occurrence counts, repeats, surfaces; x, y, z counted by hand on named sentences (first occurrence of 京 vs the second, 表 twice, 京は東京である, ともいう contains いう, 東京 -> 東京 counted twice in the corpus and 日本 not reaching N+2); a one-sentence window has no z; an independent brute-force reference written in the test (no slide code but the spans) equals every window in both scopes; every count is an int = len(sources); verify_counts accepts everything and rejects seven tampered counts (two added at review: an x and a z source citing a later occurrence); the Space sha is unchanged; the spec (ladder fractions, arms, axis weights; every perturbation of the sha; refusals); bytes identical under three hash seeds; no float (AST of both modules, JSON parse with a float hook); fulllead: windows 592 / 292 / 125, RUN median 15, every number above, the whole report against probe_hops.txt, the a2 windows equal the probe's own definition, one-sentence windows do not change the 45, the y link is `Slide.contain`, T9 records 207 / 207, ladder = share order.

### G3-a/b open points (owner; meaning only)
(1) Do the one-sentence articles (125 of 300) and the last sentence of each article form a one-sentence window (z arms empty)? Default "last" = the design's L-G3-10; without them those sentences are in no window and can never be answered by one; bank2 cannot tell (reach 45 either way). (2) Does a unit that stands in both sentences of a window (東京 ... 東京) link the two seats of itself (counted now as n_z(u, u))? It is the most direct evidence that two sentences speak of the same thing, and also the case where a window repeats what a one-sentence cross already has. (3) Does a y link need a STRICTLY larger span? Counted non-strict now (a third of RUN>WORD links are the same string cut twice); the bank2 reach is the same either way, the edge counts are not. (4) Do z edges join units of different tiers (RUN in sentence N to CHAR in N+1)? Counted for all 9 tier pairs now (spec z.tiers = "all"; "same" is a spec switch); the first measurement (S1, RUN only) uses the same-tier part. (5) (review, 2026-10-09) x = 語順 counts a pair by the FIRST occurrence of each unit (L-G3-3, = the existing p_pair, L-302); when a unit repeats, a sentence can hold both orders and first occurrence picks one. Sentence-level unit pairs with both orders present in fulllead: RUN 808 of 23423 (3 %), WORD 4946 of 47504 (10 %), CHAR 27768 of 155726 (18 %). Alternatives: count every occurrence pair, or a sentence counts for both directions. Note the asymmetry: y counts ANY occurrence pair (L-524), x the first only; z counts presence (its first occurrence only picks the cited span).

## G3-c (2026-10-09): placement of a WINDOW cross with the per-axis key (verantyx/line3/slide_place.py; tests/line3/test_slide_place.py; experiments/line3/g3/place_windows.py, experiments/line3/g3/place/)
Owner (G3 answers 1-6 and G3-a/b answers, ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md): 「文ごとの 3 層パック。この文と次の文。1 文ずつ重ねて進む」; 「x＝語順、y＝粒度、z＝スライド」; 「軸ごとに判定し、一致した軸がそれぞれラベル付きの答えを出す」; 「梯子の順に x、y、z へ。重みは辺の流れと配置の結合と読む順に」; G2 4 「基準のラベルは腕と一緒に動き、回転は全て「手」のまま」; F1 「文の語順で 1 つずつ入れ、崩れたら直前で止める」; 「そこで止めるを既定にする」; 「ちょうど良い数に後ろに仮の文を足してキリの良いところをつけて閉じるようにする」; 「数える（今の実装）」 (z self-link); 「含める（同じ範囲も接続）」; 「同じ層どうしだけ（まず S1 で測る）」. One new module and its test; placement.py, slide.py and every other module are unchanged (checked against `git show HEAD:`), nothing imports slide_place (checked in a subprocess), no existing output, cache name or test changes. File names follow the ticket text (`slide_place.py`, `test_slide_place.py`), not the design's §7.3 (`placement.py` / `slide.py`, `test_slide_placement.py`). The record holds ints and exact Fractions only; the experiment log place/*.jsonl also holds wall-clock seconds as floats (timing only, not part of any record, spec or sha).

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-560 | Arms are the axis labels. The six arms are geometry.AXES (+x -x +y -y +z -z), arm index = label; the foundation particles of the slide spec (の に で と を が, weights 144 89 55 34 21 13 over 377 as exact Fractions) are recorded on them in ladder order (OP-G3-6 (a)) and are NOT in the key (I-G3-2: `weights_in_key` false in every record). A rotation (the 23 non-identity cube rotations are moves) changes the orientation only: the contents stay on their labelled arm, the label "+x" points to a new world direction, and the key, with its per-axis split, never changes. The verifier checks all 23 rotations (key unchanged, every arm keeps its cells, `world_arms()[r(g(a))]` is arm a). A label tied to a world direction (key changes under rotation) is the reading G2 4 refused. | slide_place.ARM_NAMES, verify_fixed_point_slide |
| L-561 | placement's `_settle` / `_scan` / `_insert_group` / `Weights` are NOT importable as they stand: they call `canon` (legs sorted: the quotient over arm assignment, L-61) and the weight has no arm argument, but with a per-axis key the arm IS part of the key. The L-71 algorithm is therefore copied (climb by all best improving swaps, terminals of the best key, closure under equal-key swaps, climb again from improving escapes; `_Over` on max_class / max_states / max_moves) with two changes: weights are `w(arm, outer, inner) -> (n, omega)` and a state is the flat tuple of the labelled arms (no leg sorting). Reused from placement: Budget, LEVELS, budget_level, level_name, `_Layout` (edges; the arm of an edge = (outer index - 1) // L), `_Work`, `_Over`, to_cross; from geometry: Cross, rotate, swap, edges, seats, moves_rotate, moves_swap; from slide: canonical, Slide, WindowCounts. carry's LocalTier was not used (it feeds placement.Weights, the wrong interface). Consequence: arrangements that differ by exchanging two legs of different arms are different states here (the quotient of L-61 does not exist), so classes are larger than in placement.py for the same budget level. (review, 2026-10-09) A third difference: the twin quotient of L-90 / L-91 (`find_twins`, `QWeights`, placement's default `quotient=True`) is not applied; every unit is its own label. Measured with the per-arm automorphism test (w(a, u, z) = w(a, v, z), w(a, z, u) = w(a, z, v) for the four seated arms and every z of the window, w(a, u, v) = w(a, v, u)): 6 twin pairs in 6 of the 592 windows, so the effect on class sizes is negligible in S1. Inherited unchanged from placement._settle: the terminals of the best key form the class before any max_class check (only closure additions are checked), so a class can be larger than max_class (292 pairs: 2 windows, class 1440 and 2160 against max_class 1000; 5 stable steps). L-73 defines max_class as the most arrangements in one class; fixing it here alone would make this search differ from placement's, so both are left as they are and the count is printed by place_windows.py. | slide_place._scan, _settle, _insert_one |
| L-562 | The y arms exist and carry their labels but have no seats in S1 (L-G3-5; the units of a cross are of ONE tier, y needs two tiers, so n_y = 0 for every pair). They are not an insertion target and not a swap target (same shape as L-77), and a record lists them in `seatless_arms`. So the capacity of a cross of arm length L is 4 L + 1, and L is the smallest with 4 L + 1 >= units (`min_L(count, 4)`), not 6 L + 1. Spec switch `y_seats=True` seats them (zero gain; it exists to test the verifier and for S2). | slide_place.arms_ok, min_L |
| L-563 | The key (G3 3.4, OP-G3-3/4). key = (sum over the cross edges of n_a, sum of omega_a), lexicographic, a = the axis of the ARM the edge lies on; an edge is (outer, inner), the inner end of an arm's last seat is the centre. +x (n_x(o,i), p(o,i)), -x (n_x(o,i), p(i,o)), +y/-y (n_y, 0) [no edge in S1], +z (n_z(o,i), n_z(o,i)), -z (n_z(i,o), n_z(i,o)), empty seat (0, 0). The numbers are read from `slide.counts(window, scope)` (G3-b, scope corpus by default); in corpus scope the +x weight equals today's (n_pair, p_pair) and -x equals (n_pair, p_pair(i, o)) for every pair of a window's units (tested). The record stores the key and its split by axis ((x, n, omega), (y, ...), (z, ...)) of the representative, and `axis_splits` = the number of different splits inside the class. This is the design's SUM over the axes (3.4); the per-axis judgement of OP-G3-4 is for G3-d. (review, 2026-10-09) The key form is the design's own recommendation (3.4 「推奨・G2 OP-G2-3 (a) の形」), not an owner choice: the owner's answer to OP-G2-3 (「エネルギー比を軸ごとに計算する」) was free text about the three ratios and took none of its options (a) key / (b) third key / (c) E_Q, and OP-G3-4 (「軸ごとに判定し…」) is the agreement of the ratios (G3-d). The owner's key is I-04 「隣り合う語の共有＋語順」; the sum key extends it (key 1 also counts n_z, key 2 also counts omega_z = n_z, which is not word order). Neither the sum nor a per-axis (lexicographic or separate) key follows from the owner's words: open point 3. `axis_split_set` = every distinct split of the class with its member count, so the members a per-axis reading would prefer INSIDE the sum-class can be read from the record; a real per-axis key changes the climbing itself and needs a new search. Note the asymmetry written as L-G3-4: x's n is the same for both directions of a pair and the direction sits in omega; z's n is directed (n_z(o,i) vs n_z(i,o)) and omega = n. | slide_place.ArmWeights, score_flat, axis_key_flat |
| L-564 | Growth (F1 stop, G3 3.4). Insertion order: the distinct units of sentence N in word order (first occurrence), then those of sentence N+1; a unit in both has its seat when N is read (`side` = this / next / both is recorded in `order_log`). The first unit sits at the centre alone (L-77); the centre then moves by search (I-02). Each next unit is inserted into EVERY member of the class (best-gain empty seatable seats, ties branch), the results are settled (L-561), the best-key class is kept; the unit that breaks the budget (a fresh `_Work` per unit, as F1 open point 6) is restored away and growth STOPS (default, L-463; skip is not built here), `left` = the units not inserted (the breaking one included). A window with no unit is recorded with stop "empty". | slide_place.grow, insertion_order |
| L-565 | Independent verifier (I-05 / L-74 for the per-axis key): `cross_key` reads the edges from geometry.edges and the arm of the outer seat, with weights read through the WindowCounts accessors (`n_x`, `before_x`, `after_x`, `n_z`) or a hand table, never through ArmWeights / the search's flat layout / score_flat (tested by AST: the three verifier functions call none of them). `verify_fixed_point_slide` (23 rotations leave the key unchanged and the labels follow; no swap of two seatable seats improves the key; the centre is never emptied) and `verify_class_slide` (one key, every member a fixed point, closure under equal-key swaps, centres non-empty, no unit on a seatless arm, labels follow). It rejects a reversed arrangement (key (4, 0) against (4, 4)) and a unit on a y arm (tested). Not checked by the verifier (design 7.4 「窓の外の単位が席に入っていないか」): it holds by construction (only `insertion_order` units are inserted, and the search only swaps seats); the records do not store members, the centres of all 292 pair records are window units. | slide_place.verify_* |
| L-566 | The spec of a placement (`PlaceSpec`): the slide spec sha (which carries the corpus sha, window rule, axis definitions, foundation) + corpus sha + scope + tier + padding + y_seats + mode + budget + the key and growth rules in words; sha256 of the canonical bytes; `short()` = the 12 hex digits for a cache key `_slideplace-<sha12>` (not built into any cache: nothing is hooked). This answers the note of L-527: the scope is in the spec of the placement, slide.py is not modified. Every perturbation of scope, tier, padding, y_seats, mode, budget, slide spec changes the sha (tested one by one); a spec made for another slide spec or corpus is refused. | slide_place.PlaceSpec |
| L-567 | Padding (owner: add constructed sentences after an article's last sentence so that every real sentence has a next-sentence window, closed at a round number). Built: the simplest, behind the spec switch `padding`: "none" (default; the windows are slide.sequence(), the slide spec's own lone rule) or "one" (the pairs and, for the last sentence of every article, a window (N, pad) flagged `constructed`, pad name `pad:<title>#<next number>`). The pad sentence has no text and no unit: it adds no seat, no edge, no count and is never evidence; the placement of a padded window is byte-for-byte the placement of the same lone window except the `constructed` / `pad` fields and the spec sha (tested; the first 40 windows of fulllead too: 40 of 40 class sha256 equal, 20 constructed). In S1 padding is therefore bookkeeping: what it changes is the label and that every one of the 592 sentences is the first sentence of a window. Which "round number" is still open (open point 1). `padding_table(slide)` prints the counts of each reading. | slide_place.place_windows, padding_table |
| L-568 | The record (`SlidePlacement`): axis labels and exact weights, seatless arms, window (n, title, sids, idx, constructed, pad), spec sha and slide spec sha and scope, tier, mode, L, size, centres, key and per-axis key, `axis_splits`, `axis_split_set` (review: each distinct split with its member count), class size and `class_sha256` (members optional in `doc(members=False)`), `order_log` (unit, side) in insertion order, every step (unit, side, status, states explored, size, class size, reason), stop, `broke_on`, `left`, budget and level, `z_self`, `next_seat`. `to_bytes()` = slide.canonical (sorted keys, compact UTF-8, Fractions as "p/q", a float raises); identical under PYTHONHASHSEED 0 / 1 / 12345 (subprocess digest of specs and every record in both paddings, both scopes, both modes). | slide_place.SlidePlacement |
| L-569 | "Did N+1 get a seat" (G3 3.4, 6.4, 7.3) in two readings, both recorded in `next_seat`, defined only for two-sentence windows: STRICT = a seated unit that is in N+1 and not in N; LOOSE = a seated unit that is in N+1 (a unit shared with N included, which is seated while N is read). A third number is added: "z edge realised" = the representative's z part of the key is > 0 (a real z link on the cross), because a strict seat can also be an overflow unit on a z arm (open point 2; measured: a strict seat without a z edge never occurred, and z realised = LOOSE in every two-sentence window, 292 of 292). | slide_place.NextSeat |
| L-570 | z self-link (owner: counted): `slide.counts` counts n_z(u, u); a unit has ONE seat, so the self-link can never be an edge of the cross. It is recorded for the seated units (`z_self`, fulllead first 40: 6 windows, 12 units) and enters nothing else. | slide_place.SlidePlacement.z_self |
| L-571 | The diagnostic line (L-G3-6, mode "line", no search, never a default): centre = the lower middle unit of sentence N (units in word order), +x = the units before it outward in reverse, -x = the units after it, -z = the units only in N+1 in word order, the first next to the centre. Its key and fixed-point status are read by the same independent verifier; it is a measurement, not a promise (numbers below). | slide_place._line_arrangement |
| L-572 | What is NOT decided or built here: S1 z edges between tiers (the cross has one tier), the per-axis energy ratios and agreement (G3-d), skip on collapse, a cache or a hook into ask / cycle, the build of all 592 windows (next ticket; the 292 pair windows were placed at review, below). The search is local (swaps only): a class is a fixed point, not the global optimum (see the toy table below). | - |

### Padding: what is a "round number"? The readings, in numbers (padding_table, fulllead: 592 real sentences, 300 articles; every reading first gives each article's last sentence a next sentence)
| reading | constructed sentences | total |
|---|---|---|
| (a) one padding sentence after each article's last sentence (BUILT, `padding="one"`) | 300 | 892 |
| (b) per article, round up to an even count | 412 | 1004 |
| (c) per article, round up to a multiple of 4 | 704 | 1296 |
| (d) corpus total a multiple of 100, after (a) | 308 | 900 |
| (e) corpus total a multiple of 100 alone (not enough: does not give every last sentence a next sentence) | 8 | 600 |

### Tests (tests/line3/test_slide_place.py: 29 passed; with tests/line3/test_slide.py 70 passed; the Pro, PYTHONHASHSEED 0 / 1 / 12345, 20 s each)
(review, 2026-10-09: the record test also checks `axis_split_set`: one row per distinct split, member counts add up to the class, every row at the total key.)
Hand tables (3-6 units): the x DIRECTION decides (A before C, B after C: C at the centre, A on +x, B on -x; the mirror table swaps the arms; the same counts n placed the wrong way score (4, 0) against (4, 4)); the centre is decided by z edges alone (no x evidence: M in both sentences at the centre, U on +z, V on -z, key (2, 2); mirrored counts swap the arms; brute force agrees); every class of three five-unit hand tables is a fixed point by the independent verifier; the verifier rejects a worse arrangement and a unit on a y arm; labels follow all 23 rotations; the y arms hold nothing, capacity 4 L + 1 (min_L 1 5 6 9 10 13 -> 1 1 2 2 3 3); a local fixed point below the brute-force optimum (insertion order U, M, V sticks at (1, 1) where M, U, V reaches (2, 2): the order matters, F1 open point 2); a table where sentence N fills the budget (max_class 3): N+1 has no seat, the kept class equals growth of the inserted units alone. Toy corpus with the real slide counts (3 articles, and a separate 2-sentence article D): window (0, 1) by hand: 東京 at the centre, 日本 on -x (2, 2), 首都 on +z (1, 1), 都市 on -z (1, 1), key (4, 4), per-axis ((x 2 2) (y 0 0) (z 2 2)), brute force best (4, 4); every toy window in both scopes: stable class by the verifier, search key <= brute force best, equal to the verifier's key member by member, and strictly below it in at least one window (the lone window (1,): (3, 2) against (3, 3)); corpus-scope +x / -x weights equal pl.Weights; insertion order and sides; z self-link 猫 counted and one seat; padding windows constructed, pad names, the same class as the lone windows, no pad in any unit / edge / count, every sentence starts a window only with padding; article D (山 川 海 空 森 | 星 月 光): class sizes 1 1 1 8 20 1 26 138, with max_class 8 growth breaks on 森 inside N, left 森 星 月 光, N+1 strict False loose False, and the kept class equals the first four units grown alone; max_class 30 breaks inside N+1; foundation labels and Fractions recorded, another ladder changes the slide spec sha but not the arrangement or key; the line version on named windows; spec sha perturbations; placement.py equals `git show HEAD:`, no ask / cycle / matryoshka / carry / grammar module loaded by importing slide_place, none of them mentions slide_place, `serialize_all` of toy ordered crosses identical with and without importing slide_place; bytes under three hash seeds; AST: no float constant / name / true division, JSON re-parsed with a float hook; fulllead first six windows (size, L, class, stop, key) as measured.

### G3-c measurement (fulllead_sents, tier RUN, scope corpus, level mid {1000, 20000, 400000}, padding none, stop on collapse; the Pro, load 2-3; experiments/line3/g3/place/*.md, *.jsonl)
Two small runs at the build: the first 40 windows of slide.sequence() (20 two-sentence, 20 one-sentence) and the first 40 two-sentence windows; at review (2026-10-09) all 292 two-sentence windows (`place_windows.py 292 mid none 1`). The search takes 8.4 s (mean 0.21 s, median 0.17 s, max 1.08 s), 10.8 s (mean 0.27 s, median 0.21 s, max 1.10 s) and 91.8 s for the 292 (mean 0.31 s, median 0.19 s, max 4.00 s); with the independent verifier and the line version 21.7 s / 25.3 s / 226.6 s. The review reran every run after adding `axis_split_set` and the extra counts: every record is byte-equal to the build's except the new fields and the wall-clock seconds; padding "one" gives the same records as "none" (40 of 40 class sha256).

| number | first 40 windows (20 two-sentence) | first 40 two-sentence windows | all 292 two-sentence windows (review) |
|---|---|---|---|
| N+1 got a seat, STRICT (L-569) | 8/20 = 40.0 % | 20/40 = 50.0 % | **178/292 = 60.9 %** |
| N+1 got a seat, LOOSE | 12/20 = 60.0 % | 28/40 = 70.0 % | **209/292 = 71.5 %** |
| z edge realised (z part of the key > 0; the same for every member of every class) | 12/20 = 60.0 % | 28/40 = 70.0 % | 209/292 = 71.5 % (= LOOSE in every window) |
| strict seats without a z edge | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| stops of two-sentence windows | budget inside N 11, budget inside N+1 6, exhausted 3 (all max_class) | budget inside N 19, inside N+1 10, exhausted 11 (all max_class) | budget inside N 113 (max_class 111, max_moves 2; 4 of them on a unit of both sentences), inside N+1 105 (max_class 104, max_moves 1), exhausted 74; N+1 has no strict seat in any of the 113 |
| stops of one-sentence windows | budget 9, exhausted 11 | - | - |
| units in the window / seated (median) / L (median) | 13 / 7 / 2 (286 of 572 units seated) | 14 / 8 / 2 (339 of 644) | 15 / 9 / 2 (2512 of 4621) |
| class size median / max / windows with class size 1 | 216 / 960 / 5 | 165 / 960 / 0 | 174 / 2160 / 2 |
| classes larger than max_class (L-561, inherited) | 0 | 0 | 2 windows (1440, 2160), 5 stable steps |
| key split of the representatives, sum n_x / n_y / n_z (the representative is a label: over the members the sums range n_x 189-191 / 208-216 / 1472-1532, n_z 38-40 / 65-73 / 452-512) | 189 / 0 / 40 | 211 / 0 / 70 | 1476 / 0 / 508 |
| windows whose class holds 2 different per-axis splits at one total key | 2 (window 4: class 600; window 30: class 124) | 8 | 57 (in 53 of them a split preferred by x first, then z, is not the representative's) |
| units on z arms / no z evidence on the inner edge / on neither edge | 96 / 69 / 58 (two-sentence windows only: 66 / 39 / 28; the 30 in one-sentence windows have none) | 127 / 72 / 51 | 1000 / 547 / 338 |
| independent verifier: stable class | 40/40 | 40/40 | 292/292 |
| diagnostic line: fixed point of single swaps / key below the search's | 18/40 / 4/40 | 14/40 / 14/40 | 135/292 / 101/292 |
| the same, only where the line and the search seat the same units (search exhausted): line below / equal / above | 14: 3 / 5 / 6 | 11: 9 / 2 / 0 | 74: 50 / 17 / 7 |
| z self-links on seated units | 6 windows, 12 units | 13 windows, 20 units | 81 windows, 124 units |

The design's stop rule (G3 6.4, 7.3: if fewer than half of the two-sentence windows seat a unit of N+1, report and raise as an OP, change nothing silently) on the full set of 292 two-sentence windows: STRICT 178/292 = 60.9 %, LOOSE 209/292 = 71.5 %, both above half, so the rule does not fire. The first-40 sample (STRICT 40 %) was low. What the full set still shows is the danger the rule guards (3.4, T-G3-5): in 113 of 292 windows (38.7 %) growth stops on the budget while sentence N is read and N+1 gets no strict seat; 338 of the 1000 units on z arms have no z evidence on either edge (open point 2). Nothing was changed in the placement.
Note on the line rows: the line seats every unit and the search only those before its stop, so "key below the search's" over all windows compares different unit sets; where both seat the same units the line is below the search in 50 of 74, and ABOVE it in 7 (the search is local, open point 5).

### G3-c open points (owner; meaning level)
(1) What is the "round number" of the padding (list above)? Built: (a) one padding sentence per article (300, 892 sentences). Either reading changes only the window labels in S1 (a pad adds no unit); (b) / (c) / (d) would add several constructed sentences in a row after some articles, which a window of two sentences (N, N+1) would turn into windows (pad, pad) with no unit at all: whether those empty windows exist, or the rounding is on the count only, is also yours. (2) The sentence-N/N+1 balance (the pre-registered danger, G3 3.4): in 11 of 20 (first 40) and 19 of 40 (pairs) two-sentence windows growth stops on max_class while sentence N is still being read, and 69 of the 96 (39 of 66 in the two-sentence windows) / 72 of the 127 units on the z arms are fillers with no z evidence: the cross has room for only about 2 L units on the two x arms, the rest of sentence N goes to whatever empty seat ties at zero gain (z arms included), the classes explode (median 216, max 960 of 1000) and sentence N+1 finds the z arms taken. (review) On all 292 pair windows: 113 stop inside N (N+1 never strictly seated there), 547 of 1000 z-arm units have no z evidence on their inner edge and 338 on neither edge. This is the literal result of the rules (L-64: the best-gain empty seats, ties branch over EVERY seatable seat; L-G3-5 removes only the y seats; F1 order N then N+1), not a slip of the code. Structural note: on a z arm the edges are (outer, inner) and +z wants outer in N, inner in N+1, so two consecutive edges of one z arm can both carry evidence only through a unit of both sentences; with units of one sentence only, at most every other edge of a z arm can be a real link. Readings to choose from (none built): units of N that no x edge can use wait (are not seated) until N+1 is read; the z arms are reserved for N+1 (no seat for N's units there); N and N+1 are inserted interleaved by their links; a larger budget level for the window crosses. (3) The key is the SUM over the axes (G3 3.4, built); inside one class the x part and the z part of the total can differ (8 of 40 pair windows hold two different splits). Under the per-axis judgement (OP-G3-4) those members give different ratios. Should the key rather be lexicographic over the axes in ladder order (x, then y, then z), or per axis separately? (review) Not derivable: the owner's words about axes (「エネルギー比を軸ごとに計算する」, 「軸ごとに判定し…」) are about the three ratios and their agreement; the sum key is the design's recommendation (L-563). Full set: 57 of 292 classes hold two splits, and in 53 of them x first then z would prefer a member that is not the representative; `axis_split_set` records every split with its member count. A per-axis key also changes the search (climbing, closure), so its classes need a new run. (4) A one-sentence window (the last sentence of an article, 300 of 592) still seats units on its z arms (15 of 20 lone windows in the first 40 do) although n_z = 0 everywhere there (the window has no N+1, so its counts have no z key at all): by the same reasoning as L-G3-5 (no seat where no edge can exist) its z arms should have no seats either; L-G3-10 only says "z の腕は空" (the arm has no edge). (review) Read literally, 「空」 is "holds nothing", which with the L-G3-5 rule shape (no seat where no edge can exist) gives no z seats in a one-sentence window; the build reads it as "no edge". A design-level reading (L-G3-10 is the design's local choice), not an owner word; changing it changes the 300 lone windows' classes and L, not the 292 pair windows. (5) The search is local (swaps only, greedy insertion): a class is a fixed point, not the best arrangement (toy: window (1,) (3, 2) against a brute-force (3, 3); the hand table with insertion order U, M, V). The result depends on the insertion order (F1 open point 2). Is a fixed point enough as the definition of "placed", or should the search also test two-seat exchanges / restarts? (6) Classes are counted WITHOUT the quotient over arm assignment (the arms are different, L-561): a class of 960 here is not comparable to a class of 960 in placement.py. Should arms whose edges are all zero in a window (the y arms, and the z arms of a one-sentence window) still be distinguished? (7) The line (L-G3-6) is a fixed point in 18 of 40 (first 40) / 14 of 40 (pairs) and its key is below the search's in 4 / 14: as an initial placement it is worse than the search in a third of the pair windows, so it is not offered as a default. (review) Those counts compare different unit sets (the line seats every unit); where both seat the same units (292 pairs: 74 exhausted windows) the line is below the search in 50, equal in 17 and above it in 7. (8) y: the cross has no y edge in S1 by construction (one tier). A y link needs the next ticket's two-tier pack (G3-h); the y arms hold their label and the weight 55 / 34 over 377 but nothing else. (9) z self-links (n_z(u, u)) are counted but cannot be an edge (one seat per unit): should a unit that lies in both sentences be allowed two seats (one "this", one "next") so that the self-link is a real z edge? It is the most direct evidence that two sentences speak of the same thing (G3-a/b open point 2).

## G3-d (2026-10-09): the three ratios per axis of a window cross and the labelled per-axis answer (verantyx/line3/slide_ratios.py; tests/line3/test_slide_ratios.py; docs/LINE3_G3_SLIDING_PACKS.md 3.3 - 3.5, 7.3)
Owner (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md): decision 7 「断面から中心の経路、…辺を伝った時、配置自体で空間のエネルギー比率の３つで一致した時に安定したものでその状態から言葉を順に読み出して…」 (and the audit reading of (3)(7): (1) inside the section, (2) along the edges, (3) the placement itself, three energy ratios, stable when the three agree); G2 3 「エネルギー比を軸ごとに計算する」; G3 4 「軸ごとに判定し、一致した軸がそれぞれラベル付きの答えを出す」; G3 6 「梯子の順に x、y、z へ。重みは辺の流れと配置の結合と読む順に」; I-06 + N-01 (query energy E_Q), I-07 (question attachment), I-09 (sections = arm windows), I-10 (three separate quantities), I-11 (one unit), G3-c answers (「文ごとに席を分ける」, 「軸ごとの鍵の比べ方」). Reused unchanged: energy.energy / unique_argmax / _n / _n_pair (E_Q, L-52, L-53, L-58), cycle.make_context (I-07, L-109), geometry.edges / visible_arms / G24, slide.WindowCounts / canonical. No hook into ask / cycle / matryoshka / cli (G3-e); slide_place.py is not imported (the record is read by duck typing).

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-600 | Scope and inputs. ONE window cross of ONE tier (S1: RUN). Seats are keyed (unit, sid) from the start (`SeatRec(unit, sid, arm, position, sources)`; `position` 0 = the outermost seat of the arm, the centre has arm "center"; "centre" is accepted); a cross is a `WindowCross` (L, seats, tier, sids, orientation, the record's own meta) and the geometry used is geometry.edges / visible_arms / Seat only (a unit on two seats cannot live in a geometry.Cross by its sid, so the reader works on the seat map; `to_cross()` gives the same cells as a Cross for energy.py and the merged check). `window_cross(record, member)` reads: member 0 of a record with `seats` (G3-c2: sources {occ, edge} are turned into rows ("occ", tier, sid, k, unit, start, end) and ("edge", axis, arm, unit, sid, n, omega)); another member through `items` (token -> (unit, sid)); the G3-c record (`members`, `order_log`): one seat per unit, sid = sids[0] for "this" and "both", sids[1] for "next" (a unit of both sentences gets the sid of N), sources from `pack` if given. A seat out of range, two units on one seat, or one (unit, sid) on two seats is refused. (review, 2026-10-09) The record's spec shas are read under the object's names (`spec_sha`, `slide_spec_sha`) and, fixed at review, under the doc's (`spec_sha256`, `slide_spec_sha256`): a jsonl line of place2 now reads to the same bytes as the object (before, the dict form dropped the two shas from `cross.record`; tested). | slide_ratios.SeatRec, WindowCross, window_cross, window_crosses |
| L-601 | The axis of an edge is the axis of the ARM it lies on: the arm of its OUTER seat; the inner end of an arm's last seat is the centre. Every edge lies on exactly one arm, so x, y, z PARTITION the edges and a seat has edges only on its own arm's axis (the centre has the inner edge of all six arms, i.e. of all three axes). | slide_ratios._Reader.edges_of |
| L-602 | Evidence n_a of an edge (outer o, inner i), through `Evidence(arm, o, i) -> (n, sources)`: +x and -x n_x(o,i) (symmetric); +y n_y(o,i), -y n_y(i,o); +z n_z(o,i), -z n_z(i,o). Both ends of an edge read the SAME number (the arm's, with o the outer seat), never n_a with the roles swapped (tested with the asymmetric z edge: 都市 on -z has n_z = 1, on +z 0). `counts_evidence(counts, tier)` reads slide.counts and returns the counts' own source rows; `table_evidence` is a hand table (synthetic rows); `label_blind_evidence` is n_pair on every arm (today's cycle). The tier space (E_Q, n(u)) is the corpus', whatever the scope of the counts. | slide_ratios.counts_evidence, table_evidence, label_blind_evidence |
| L-603 | R1_a, the section walk of axis a: for each world section s (I-09: geometry.visible_arms(orientation, s, window)) only the visible arms OF AXIS a are walked, outer end -> centre, with energy.py's L-55 (a step needs the edge evidenced, here n_a(edge) > 0, and "the energy does not drop") and L-59 (E_q of the query unit attached to the section by I-07, else E_Q); a section points to the strict maximum of its termini (L-54, L-58); a section that sees no arm of axis a is not a section of a (not listed); one that sees an arm of a but points nowhere is listed and not working (L-57); R1_a = the one unit all working sections of a point to; different units = section_disagreement. Sections see the arm by the labelled arm, so a rotation (orientation) changes only which sections see which axis (x in sections 0 1 2 5, y 1 2 3 4, z 3 4 5 0 for the identity). NO weight in R1. | slide_ratios._Reader.walk, section |
| L-604 | R2_a and R3_a, per axis a over the a-edges e = (u, v): F_a(v) = sum w(arm e) * E_Q(u) * n_a(e) / n(u); B_a(v) = sum w(arm e) * n_a(e) / n(v) (the formulas of energy.edge_flow / placement_binding with the edge's own count). Per seat, summed per unit (energy.py's L-56): a unit on two seats gets the sum; a pair of seats of ONE unit adds nothing (energy.py), EXCEPT on a z edge when `z_self_edges=True` (off by default; the owner counted the self-link in the key, whether the ratios count it is an open point). n(u) = 0 terms are dropped (the guard of edge_flow); every placed unit has an entry (0 if no flow); the argmax is unique_argmax (a tie or all-zero points nowhere). | slide_ratios._Reader.flows |
| L-605 | WHERE THE WEIGHTS ENTER (G3 6, 3.5): w(arm) = the Fibonacci weight of the foundation label on that arm (+x の 144/377, -x に 89/377, +y で 55/377, -y と 34/377, +z を 21/377, -z が 13/377, exact Fractions from the slide spec's foundation, or an explicit `weights` mapping). (a) the whole edge term of F_a is multiplied by w(arm of the edge), once per edge, at both ends alike (`F[v] += w * E_Q(u) * n/n(u)`); (b) the whole edge term of B_a likewise (`B[v] += w * n/n(v)`); (c) the read order of the answers: axes by w(+a) + w(-a) descending (x, y, z for the ladder; equal weights keep x, y, z). Nowhere else: not in R1, E_Q, n, N, r0, the counts, the key (I-G3-2). The decision of R2 and R3 is made on the WEIGHTED values; the unweighted sums are computed beside them (`edge_flow_plain`, `binding_plain`) and recorded. Consequence (tested): changing a weight changes only F_a / B_a of the arm's axis and, through them, possibly the agreement; the section walk, the plain sums and the other axes are bit-identical; a common factor on all arms changes no decision. | slide_ratios._Reader.flows, read_order |
| L-606 | Agreement per axis (I-11 per axis, G3 4): AGREE iff R1_a = R2_a = R3_a = one unit. Otherwise a typed abstention per axis: points_nowhere (with which of section / edge / binding point nowhere), section_disagreement (the working sections point to different units), ratio_disagreement (three units, not all equal), and no_edges (the axis has no edge with both ends seated: y in S1, one tier; structure, not evidence, so that a measurement can tell it from an evidential tie). The result lists an answer per agreeing axis and an abstention for each other axis; the axes never vote together. | slide_ratios.AxisRatios.status, Abstention |
| L-607 | N-03 (an answer needs a working section with an in-space query unit attached) is NOT applied here: `grounded` (L-104: working sections of the axis whose attached unit is in the space) is recorded on the axis and on the answer, and an answer with grounded 0 is still an answer (tested with a question absent from the space). The gate belongs to G3-e. | AxisRatios.grounded |
| L-608 | The labelled answer `AxisAnswer`: axis, label "slide:x" / "slide:y" / "slide:z", unit, the three ratio values as exact Fractions (section = the largest energy at which the working sections' walks ended on the unit (E_q for an attached section); edge_flow = F_a(unit) weighted; binding = B_a(unit) weighted) plus the plain F and B, every seat of the unit keyed (unit, sid), the edge terms (n, the counts' source rows, weight, n(this), n(other), E_Q(other)) and the walk steps with their sources, `grounded`, and `sources` = the rows of the terms and steps prefixed by the axis, deduplicated, in order. Answers are in read order (L-605 (c)). | slide_ratios.AxisAnswer |
| L-609 | The merged check of 7.3 (`merged_check`): with `label_blind_evidence` (n_a = n_pair on EVERY arm) and unit weights, sum over x, y, z of the plain F_a (resp. B_a) per unit equals energy.edge_flow (resp. placement_binding) of today's cycle on the same cross, as Fractions, dict for dict (every placed unit has an entry). It holds because the edges are partitioned by arm (L-601) and each term is counted once; it does NOT hold on the real counts for z (n_z is not n_pair: 日本 -> 東京 has n_z 1, n_pair 2; tested explicitly) and does not hold for the weighted sums (all w < 1, strictly smaller; tested), so the equality is stated, and tested, for the unweighted variant on label-blind evidence; on the real counts an x-only cross equals today's sums (n_x = n_pair on the corpus scope). Tested on 6 toy crosses (L = 1, 2, twin seats, no query) in all 24 orientations and on real fulllead window crosses. | slide_ratios.merged_check |
| L-610 | Trace (I-G3-1): `check_trace(result)` (ValueError at the first violation): every answer unit has a seat keyed (unit, sid); every edge term and walk step has n > 0, exactly n source rows and both ends among the seats; the terms re-add to the answer's F and B (sum w * E_Q(other) * n / n(other), sum w * n / n(this)); the answer has sources. The x sources of an answer are exactly the counts' rows (tested against slide.counts). | slide_ratios.check_trace |
| L-611 | Bytes: `SlideRatios.to_bytes()` = slide.canonical of the doc (format "line3.slide_ratios.v1": the cross with its seats and record meta, the query set and attachment, the six weights, the weights-enter sentence, per axis every section / walk / step and all four sums per unit, answers, abstentions, read order); Fractions as "n/d", a float is refused by canonical, the module has no float and no division operator (AST test). Identical under PYTHONHASHSEED 0 / 1 / 12345 (subprocess test). | slide_ratios.SlideRatios |
| L-612 | Question: `question` is the unit sequence of the cross's tier (cycle.split_question cuts a question) or a cycle.QueryContext; I-07 is cycle.make_context's (the first layer's units, unit i attached to world section i; N-21 beyond six is the next layer, L-109 default), E_Q reads the context's energy_units (L-51: a set). The import of cycle has no side effect (subprocess test: nothing written, no ask / matryoshka / carry / readout / grammar / slide_place loaded). A query unit outside the space is neither error nor evidence (L-52). | slide_ratios.read_axes |
| L-613 | Members of a class: the G3-c record is a CLASS of equal-key arrangements and the ratios depend on the arrangement. `window_crosses(record)` gives every member as a WindowCross and `read_members` runs read_axes on each and lists, per axis, the distinct units its agreeing members agree on and how many agree. Nothing is chosen between members (the member choice by stability is G3-e, L-107). | slide_ratios.read_members |
| L-614 | NOT built here: the N-03 gate, the member choice, any hook into ask / cycle / cli, a cache, y edges between tiers (a cross has one tier; the y arms run through the same edge machinery and read counts.y, and are exercised by hand tables on y arms in the exhaustive single-edge test and by the merged check, but no real y evidence exists in S1), the Fibonacci weights in the key (never), the diagnostic or skip variants of the placement. | - |

### G3-d tests (tests/line3/test_slide_ratios.py; Pro, PYTHONHASHSEED 0 / 1 / 12345 identical)
45 tests. Hand toy windows (test_slide_place's corpus, window (0, 1)): T1 (東京 centre, 日本 -x, 首都 +z, 都市 -z; question 首都 東京): axis x ties (F_x = B_x = 89/377 for 東京 and 日本) and abstains with nowhere = edge, binding; axis z has F_z(東京) 89/1885, F_z(首都) 21/754, B_z(東京) 17/377, B_z(首都) 21/377 (plain B_z is a three-way tie, so the weight alone makes 首都 the binding's unit) = ratio_disagreement; axis y no_edges. T2 (首都 +x, 日本 -x): x AGREE on 東京 with F_x 877/1885, B_x 161/377, sources 1 + 2 rows. T3 (日本 +z, 都市 -z, 首都 -x; question 東京): z AGREE on 東京 (F_z 68/1885, B_z 17/377) while x abstains, i.e. the axes are judged separately. An asymmetric z edge from both ends; the independent route of the test (from geometry.neighbours, not edges) equals the reader on 5 crosses; the weight tests (only the edge terms move, sections / plain sums / other axes bit-identical, a common factor changes no decision, read order flips with the axis weights); the merged equality; 24 orientations (F, B invariant, the sections of an axis equal the window table); two seats keyed (unit, sid) with and without the z self edge; the real slide_place record in the G3-c configuration (= T1) and in the module default (two seats), all members; the question attachment; trace; bytes; no float; nothing hooked. Exhaustive: an axis with ONE evidenced edge never agrees (all ordered pairs of units x 6 arms x n 1..3 x 12 questions of 0..2 units, 10 000+ cases).
Fulllead, first pair windows (informational, not a measurement of the model: the "question" is the window's own first one or two units, no bank question; at most 4 members per window): 60 windows, G3-c configuration, 704 member reads: slide:x answers in 30 reads (10 per question form), no slide:z answer, y always no_edges; the invariants (merged equality, trace, determinism) hold on every real cross read. Time: placement 0.29 s per window (0.60 s in the two-seat default), reading + merged check 3 ms per member.

### G3-d open points (owner; meaning level)
(1) The weight is PER ARM (+x の 144 and -x に 89 weigh the two sides of one axis differently), as G3 3.5 (1) reads it ("腕 a の辺の項に r_F(p_a) を掛ける"). Inside an axis this is a bias toward the +side (T1: the plain z binding is a tie, the weighted one picks 首都). If the weight were per AXIS (w(+a) + w(-a) on every edge of a) it would be a pure scale inside an axis and could change no decision, only the read order. The owner's words (「重みは辺の流れと配置の結合と読む順に」) do not say which. (2) Structure: an axis with ONE evidenced edge can never agree (exhaustively tested; algebra: R1 on the centre needs E(C) >= E(O), R2 needs E(O)/n(O) > E(C)/n(C), R3 needs n(C) < n(O)), so agreement needs at least two evidenced edges on the axis, and for z it needs a unit on each z arm with z evidence (the G3-c record, 338 of 1000 z-arm units have none). Together with the weights this is why agreements are rare in the first measurement (x in about 4 % of member reads, z none). Is that the intended strictness, or should the three ratios of a single edge be allowed to agree (e.g. R1 not required to stop on the same side)? (review, 2026-10-09) "Never" is too strong: the algebra above reads ONE energy E for R1 and R2, but R1 reads E_q of the section's attached query unit (L-59) and R2 reads E_Q. It holds when some section that sees the edge's arm reads E_Q, and for every question of at most 3 units (window 3: the arm's three sections then carry the whole query, and the sum of their three walk conditions gives E_Q(C) > E_Q(O), against R2); the exhaustive test used questions of 0-2 units only. With 4+ units it fails: C in 4 sentences, O in 5 (one shared), a b c only with C, d only with O, question a b c d, O on -x: the three -x sections carry a b c and walk O -> C, d pulls E_Q to O (9/40 > 7/32), n(C) < n(O): one evidenced edge, AGREE on C (test_one_evidenced_edge_can_agree_when_every_section_of_its_arm_reads_an_attached_unit). Rare, not impossible (0 of the single-edge axes in the 20 bank2 windows of the review). The background line of the owner's decision after G3-d (「決して一致しない（代数と全数試験）」) rests on the narrower statement. (3) The self z edge of a unit with two seats (the owner counted it in the key): should it count in F_z and B_z (option `z_self_edges`, default off = energy.py's L-56)? With it on, the unit with two seats is the only unit with z flow and its axis agrees by itself. (4) Is N-03 per axis (an agreeing axis answers only if one of ITS working sections has an in-space query unit attached), or global as in the cycle? Recorded (`grounded`), not gated. (5) A class of equal-key arrangements gives different per-axis ratios (G3-c open point 3): which member answers, the most stable one per axis (as L-107) or one for all axes? `read_members` lists the agreeing members per axis and breaks no tie. (6) "The section ratio value" of an answer: built as the largest energy at which the working sections' walks ended on the unit (E_q for attached sections); the owner's three "ratio values" are energies/flows, a walk has no ratio of its own. Another reading (a count of working sections, or the energy under the whole query) changes only what is printed, not the agreement. (7) y is structurally empty (no_edges) until the two-tier pack (G3-h); the y machinery is the same code and reads counts.y when a cross has two tiers (the unit would then need a tier on its seat).

### G3-d addendum (2026-10-09): owner decisions on the G3-d open points (verantyx/line3/slide_ratios.py; tests/line3/test_slide_ratios.py)
Owner (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md, 「G3-d の実装後の決定」): both single-edge rules are to be measured; weights stay per arm; the z self edge counts by default. L-615 `read_axes(..., agreement="three" | "two_if_single_edge")`, default "three" (= L-606 unchanged). "two_if_single_edge": an axis with exactly ONE evidenced edge (both ends seated, n_a > 0, not a same-unit pair that is skipped) is judged on R2 and R3 only (AGREE iff both point to the same unit, else points_nowhere / ratio_disagreement); its section walk is skipped and recorded as not applicable (`section_applicable` false, no sections, `section_unit` None, the answer's `section` value None, no walk steps, `grounded` 0, "section" never listed in `nowhere`); axes with 0 or 2+ evidenced edges are read exactly as under "three" (tested for equality); the rule is in the bytes (`agreement`). L-616 `z_self_edges` default is ON (L-604 changes only its default; `merged_check` fixes it to False because energy.py's L-56 skips same-unit pairs, so the merged equality of 7.3 is unchanged); the weights stay per arm (L-605 unchanged). Tests: 51 (6 new: the switch on T1 and T3, a single-edge axis that answers under the new rule only (centre 東京, 首都 on +x, question 都市: F_x 216/1885 vs 144/1885, B_x 144/377 vs 144/754), an exhaustive single-edge sweep (no answer under "three"; under the new rule an answer exactly when R2 = R3, other axes unchanged), two-edge axes alike under both rules, the z default); the two-seat test now compares the default (on) with an explicit False. (review, 2026-10-09: 52 tests; +1 the single-edge counter-example with a 4-unit question; the exhaustive single-edge test is renamed to what it shows (questions of 0-2 units); the two-seat adapter test also reads the record's jsonl form and compares the bytes under both agreement rules.) Open (meaning): under "two_if_single_edge" a single-edge answer rests on two ratios that by construction pull in opposite directions in many cases (R3 prefers the unit of small n, R2 the unit of high E per n); the measurement of both rules should report how often each axis answers and how the answer agrees with the evidence sentence.

## G3-c2 (2026-10-09): the owner's decisions after G3-c as spec switches of the window placement (verantyx/line3/slide_place.py; tests/line3/test_slide_place2.py; tests/line3/test_slide_place.py fixture; experiments/line3/g3/place2/)
Owner (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md, 「G3-c の実装後の決定」 and 「G3-c の監査後の決定」, verbatim choices): 「記事ごとに仮の文を 1 つ足す（合計 892）」; 「軸ごとに別々に判定し、各軸がそれぞれ安定を持つ」 (合計の鍵ではなく、各軸で改善が無いときに安定); 「両方を比較する」 (証拠の無い軸の腕に座らせる版と座らせない版を S1 で並べて測る); 「文ごとに席を分ける（同じ語が 2 席、z の辺でつながる）」; 「z の腕は N+1 の語専用にする」 (x の腕は文 N、z の腕は文 N+1). One module is extended (slide_place.py) and one new test file added; placement.py, slide.py, slide_ratios.py (G3-d, written concurrently, reads the record below) and every other module are untouched; nothing imports slide_place from ask / cycle / matryoshka. The G3-c behaviour is ONE named configuration (`SP.LEGACY`) and is byte-identical to the committed G3-c records; the owner's configuration is `SP.DEFAULTS`. Judgement points are numbered from L-580 (L-573..L-579 are unused; G3-d took L-600..).

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-580 | Four spec switches, defaults = the owner's configuration, G3-c = `LEGACY`. `stability` "sum" (G3-c) / "per_axis"; `seat_key` "unit" (G3-c: one seat per unit) / "unit_sid"; `seat_empty_axis` "allow" (G3-c) / "deny"; `growth` "n_then_n1" (G3-c) / "interleave" / "z_reserved". `DEFAULTS` = per_axis, unit_sid, allow, z_reserved (per_axis became the default because LEGACY is reachable byte-identically, tests below; z_reserved is the owner's 「z の腕は N+1 の語専用」; allow is kept because the owner said "両方を比較する" and measured results are below). `make_spec(..., stability=None, seat_key=None, seat_empty_axis=None, growth=None)`: a switch left at None takes the default; `make_spec(slide, **SP.LEGACY)` is the G3-c spec. A fourth switch beyond the ticket's three (`seat_key`) is needed because decision 4 changes the arrangement of every window with a shared unit (81 of the 292 windows seat a unit with n_z(u, u) > 0, L-570) and the committed records would otherwise be unreachable. The spec doc lists the switches (and the rule texts) only when they differ from LEGACY, so the LEGACY spec keeps its G3-c bytes and its sha256 (tested equal to the G3-c module's). The low-level `grow(...)`, `verify_fixed_point_slide`, `verify_class_slide`, `min_L` default to the G3-c behaviour (keyword switches `stability`, `z_reserved`, `arm_names`, `sides`), so the G3-c hand tables read unchanged. The only edit of the G3-c test file: its spec fixture and the 8 `SP.make_spec(` calls go through `mk_spec(slide, **kw) = SP.make_spec(slide, **dict(SP.LEGACY, **kw))` (a file that tests G3-c asks for the G3-c configuration); its 29 tests pass unchanged. | slide_place.LEGACY / DEFAULTS / PlaceSpec / make_spec |
| L-581 | Per-axis stability = PARETO. The key of an arrangement is split by axis, (n_x, omega_x), (n_y, omega_y), (n_z, omega_z), each axis compared lexicographically as in G3-c. A single move is IMPROVING when no axis falls and at least one rises ("an arrangement whose per-axis keys are all >= is better"); it is EQUAL when no axis changes; a move that raises one axis and lowers another is neither (so a move improving x and worsening z is not taken, a move improving both is). A state is a fixed point when it has no improving move. The summed key (sum n_a, sum omega_a) is still computed and recorded (`key`) but is no longer read by the decision. Why not the most literal reading ("no move improves key_a on ANY axis", so a trade-off move also makes a state unstable): a trade-off x up / z down and its inverse z up / x down would each make the other state unstable, so a search with that rule can cycle and need not have any stable state at all; Pareto is the weakest rule under which every climb terminates (product order on a finite set) and which still makes each axis "have its own stability" (an axis that rises while none falls is never given up for another). The strict reading, lexicographic (x, then y, then z) and weighted readings are open point 1; `tradeoff_counts` measures how often they would differ (below). On windows with only x (one-sentence windows: no z, y never has an edge) the rule is the G3-c rule (tested equal: toy and 12 fulllead lone windows). | slide_place._vflags, _vdom, _maximal, _scan_pa |
