| L-582 | The per-axis search (G3-c's L-561 machinery with a partial order). `_scan_pa`: of the improving swaps keep those whose axis-delta is not dominated by another improving swap's (the Pareto-maximal ones; ties and incomparable ones all branch, as G3-c's "best" ties did); `_settle_pa`: climb, keep the terminals whose per-axis key no other terminal's dominates, close each key's terminals under the no-axis-changes swaps (an escape = an improving swap from a member, as before), climb again from the escapes of a class that has any, and return the union of the closed classes whose key no other closed class dominates; `_insert_one_pa`: a unit goes to the empty allowed seats whose gain 6-vector is not dominated by another seat's (incomparable gains branch). Consequence: a class may hold SEVERAL INCOMPARABLE per-axis keys (record: `axis_splits` > 1 and `axis_split_set` lists every key with its member count; measured: 57 of 292 windows under n_then_n1, 0 under z_reserved); `one_key` need not hold, the verifier checks an antichain instead (L-589). The max_class budget keeps counting closure additions per key class only, exactly as G3-c's `_settle` (the defect noted at L-561 is NOT fixed here, a separate task does: 7 windows end with a class above max_class under per_axis n_then_n1, 10 under interleave, 0 under z_reserved). The G3-c functions are kept as they were (copied algorithm, `sum` path) with one added optional argument, the seating policy, so the sum path is byte-identical. | slide_place._scan_pa, _settle_pa, _insert_one_pa |
| L-583 | `seat_empty_axis="deny"`: an arm whose axis has ZERO evidence in the window has no seats. Evidence of an axis = any count of the axis for pairs of the tier's units in `slide.counts` (x: a shared sentence, y: a containment between the tier's units, z: a unit of N with a unit of N+1); `axes_with_evidence`. In S1 y never has any (one tier: no y edge), so the y arms are seatless as before; in a one-sentence window the z arms have no seats (no N+1: no z count at all); a sentence pair whose sentences hold one unit each has no x. The arms of denied axes are listed in `seatless_arms` (the record also lists `axis_evidence` {x, y, z}); `min_L` uses the reduced number of arms (2 L + 1 for the x arms alone, so a 7-unit one-sentence window gets L = 3 where "allow" gives L = 2); `min_L` is None when no arm has seats and more than one unit comes (the growth then stops with reason "no_seat", L-586). The PER-UNIT variant (a unit may sit on an arm only if it has at least one edge of that axis) is NOT built (open point 3). allow = G3-c (a z arm of a one-sentence window holds fillers at zero gain). | slide_place.axes_with_evidence, arms in place_window |
| L-584 | `seat_key="unit_sid"`: a seat is keyed (unit, sentence). A unit that occurs in both sentences of the window gets two seats, one per sentence: tokens `unit + SEP + sid` (`SEP` = "\x00"; units of one sentence keep the plain unit as token), `Item(token, unit, side, sid)` with side "this" (sentence N) or "next" (N+1) -- "both" no longer occurs. The edge weights stay the unit-level corpus evidence (`ArmWeights` reads a token as its unit, `unit_of`), so the two seats of one unit are linked by the z self-edge n_z(u, u) wherever they sit next to each other on a z arm or through the centre (n_z(u, u) is symmetric: +z and -z weigh the same), and have no x edge (n_x(u, u) is not defined: 0). The z weight does NOT look at the sentence of the seat (it stays "the corpus counts windows with o in N and i in N+1", L-G3-1: seats from the window, evidence from the corpus); gating the weight by the seat's sentence would also change seats="unit" and is open point 6. All provenance is per seat (L-587). `next_seat` (strict / loose) is read on seats: STRICT = a seated unit only in N+1, LOOSE = a seated N+1 seat (a shared unit's N+1 seat included, its N seat no longer counts), so LOOSE is not comparable with G3-c's (71.5 % there). `z_self` stays (seated units with n_z(u, u) > 0, once per unit) and `self_links` is new: per unit with two seats its n_z(u, u), whether both are seated and whether they are the two ends of ONE cross edge (and on which arm). Under "unit" the old rule holds (one seat, side "both", sid = the sentence where it is first read). Classes are larger under "unit_sid" (the two seats of a unit are exchangeable at equal key). | slide_place.Item, seat_items, unit_of, _self_links |
| L-585 | `growth` orders (the first unit of sentence N is always the centre). "n_then_n1" (G3-c): all of N in word order, then all of N+1 (a unit's first occurrence in its sentence; under "unit_sid" a shared unit appears in both lists). "interleave": N's first, N+1's first, N's second, N+1's second, ... (the longer sentence continues alone; under "unit" a unit already read is skipped, so a unit first read from N+1 is seated with sid N+1 and side "both"). `seat_items` builds the order for all three (with seat_key "unit" and n_then_n1 it equals G3-c's `insertion_order`, tested). | slide_place.seat_items |
| L-586 | `growth="z_reserved"` (owner: 「z の腕は N+1 の語専用にする」): the x arms take only sentence-N seats, the z arms only sentence-N+1 seats, y arms unchanged, the centre takes any seat (a seat that is in both, under seat_key "unit", is accepted on both). The reservation binds INSERTION (a seat goes only to an allowed empty seat) and EVERY SWAP (a move that would put a seat on an arm it is not allowed on is not a move; an empty seat takes anything) -- both in the per_axis and the sum search (`Policy`, `_swap_legal`). Capacity: the arm length L is the smallest at which Hall's conditions hold for the seats inserted so far (tokens of N <= 1 + x seats + y seats, tokens of N+1 <= 1 + z seats + y seats, all <= all seats), and then grown until every class member has an empty allowed seat for the new token: the arms GROW (the literal reading; stopping instead, as at a budget break, would conflate the reservation with the F1 stop); a seat for which no L helps (all its arms denied) stops the growth with reason "no_seat" (recorded like a budget break, `left` lists the rest). One consequence is measured (open point 4): the z arms hold only N+1 seats, so a +z arm (whose edge weight reads "outer in N, inner in N+1") holds them in the reversed direction. In a one-sentence window no seat is of N+1: the z arms stay empty with "allow" too (so "allow" and "deny" coincide under z_reserved: 0 of 300 lone windows differ, 0 of 292 pair windows). | slide_place.Policy, grow |
| L-587 | The record for G3-d (`SlidePlacement`, new fields only; the G3-c fields keep their names and bytes). `record.seats`: the representative member's seats as a list of {unit, sid, arm, position, depth, side, token, sources}: arm = "centre" or "+x" "-x" "+y" "-y" "+z" "-z" (geometry.AXES labels); position = the seat index on its arm, 0 = the OUTERMOST seat (the last position, L - 1, is next to the centre; the centre has position 0, depth 0; depth = L - position = steps to the centre); sid = the corpus sentence id of the seat; side "this" / "next" ("both" only under seat_key "unit"); sources = {"occ": [tier, sid, k, unit, start, end] (the unit's FIRST occurrence in that sentence, slide.pack, so the text span is traceable), "edge": the edge from this seat to its inner neighbour (the seat or the centre next to it toward the centre) as {axis, arm, with: {unit, sid}, n, omega, sources: [[...]]} -- the rows are the counts' own source tuples (x: sid and the two spans, z: N, N+1 and the two spans) -- or None when the inner seat is empty}. Every edge of the cross belongs to exactly one seat (its outer end), so the seats' edge sources add up to the key. `record.axis_keys` = {"x": [n, omega], "y": [...], "z": [...]} of the representative (a label: members of a per_axis class may hold several keys, `axis_split_set` lists them all: (n_x, omega_x, n_y, omega_y, n_z, omega_z, members)); the G3-c `axis_key` (same numbers) and the summed `key` stay. Also: `items` (the seats in insertion order, {token, unit, side, sid}: token -> (unit, sid), which lets a reader of the cross cells of ANY member, `members`, name its seats), `seat_order` [[unit, sid]], `switches`, `axis_evidence`, `self_links`, `tradeoffs` (moves of the representative that improve one axis and worsen another, per axis improved; and "pareto_improving", 0 in a fixed point), SStep gets `sid` (None under "unit"). `NEW_RECORD_FIELDS` lists the fields G3-c2 adds; a LEGACY record without them is the G3-c record. G3-d reads `seats` and `items` (checked: `slide_ratios.window_crosses(record)` on the default record reads all members). | slide_place.SlidePlacement, _seat_rows |
| L-588 | Regression (the byte-identity gate, written first and kept green): with `**SP.LEGACY` the spec doc, the spec sha256 and every record minus `NEW_RECORD_FIELDS` equal (1) the G3-c module (`git show 6af87ba9:verantyx/line3/slide_place.py`, executed as a module) on the toy corpus in both paddings, both scopes and both modes (bytes of `to_bytes()` and of the spec), (2) the committed records of the first 40 fulllead windows (`place/fulllead_RUN_mid_none_first40.jsonl`, every field the record has, timing and runner fields excluded), and (3) all 292 committed pair records (`place/fulllead_RUN_mid_none_pairs292.jsonl`: the LEGACY re-run in experiments/line3/g3/place2/ compared field by field, 0 mismatches; a check made when the runs were done, not a test). | tests, place2 runs |
| L-589 | The independent verifier (written on geometry.edges / swap / rotate and the counts' accessors, not on the search's flat layout, `Policy`, `_scan_pa` ...; the AST test lists the forbidden names and covers the new helpers `_pareto_better`, `_legal_seat`, `_move_ok`): `stability="per_axis"` calls a swap improving when every axis of the new per-axis key is >= and one is >, reports trade-off swaps (`swaps_tradeoff`) and the equal-key plateau; `verify_class_slide` requires every checked member a Pareto fixed point, closure under the no-axis-changes swaps, an ANTICHAIN of per-axis keys (no member's key dominated by another's; `keys` = the number of distinct keys), centres non-empty, no seat on an arm without seats and none against the reservation (`arm_names`, `sides`, `z_reserved` are the rule, written once more in the verifier); `sample=k` tests the swaps of k evenly spread members only: keys, antichain, non-empty centres and seat legality are read for EVERY member, but the fixed-point and closure checks cover the checked members only (`members_checked` says how many). The runs below use sample 12 and every checked member passed (592/592 classes in the 'all' runs); the owner's configuration on the first 6 fulllead windows is also checked with no sample (test below). A Pareto-improvable state, a state with a dominated member, a unit on a y arm and a unit on a reserved arm of the other sentence are rejected (tested). | slide_place.verify_* |
| L-590 | NOT built here: the max_class defect (L-561, a separate task), a per-unit evidence rule for seats (open point 3), gating the z weight by the seat's sentence (open point 6), a hook into ask / cycle, a cache, y between tiers, skip on collapse, the choice between members of a class (G3-d L-613). Padding: the owner's answer to G3-c open point 1 is reading (a) (one constructed sentence per article, 892 in all), already built in G3-c as `padding="one"` (L-567); it is NOT made the default here (`place_windows` / `make_spec` keep "none"; padding is bookkeeping in S1 and the 292-pair measurement below is independent of it). | - |

### Tests (tests/line3/test_slide_place2.py: 26 passed; tests/line3/test_slide_place.py 29 passed unchanged; with test_slide.py 96 passed; the Pro, PYTHONHASHSEED 0 / 1 / 12345, 45 s each; with test_slide_ratios.py (G3-d) 106 passed together)
Hand tables: a swap that improves x and worsens z is taken by the summed key and refused by per_axis (and is a "trade-off" in `tradeoff_counts`, x +1); a swap that improves both is taken by both; a swap with a zero summed change but x +1 / z -1 is an equal-key move for sum and not for per_axis (verifier: plateau 1 against 0); incomparable gains branch (U on +x with gain (2,2) and on +z with (1,1) both kept, the dominated -x gain dropped; sum keeps +x alone) and the class of the two has two keys, antichain true; a class with a dominated member is not stable; `_vdom`, `_maximal`, `_vflags`; the new verifier helpers call none of the search functions (AST). Seats: the toy window (犬 猫 | 猫 魚 食) has two 猫 seats (sids 2 and 3) under unit_sid and one under unit; n_z(猫, 猫) = 1 is a real z edge between two tokens of one unit (hand table: the N+1 seat sits next to the N seat on a z arm, z key (3,3)); `self_links.linked` equals the geometric adjacency read from `geo.edges`; every seat's provenance (occ row is the unit's first occurrence in that sentence; the edge n equals `counts.n_z` / `n_x` and the number of its source rows); `axis_keys` sum to `key`. Deny: `min_L(7, 2)` = 3, `min_L(2, 0)` None, a one-sentence window has its z arms empty and `seatless_arms` +z -z under deny, a pair window keeps them, a window with no seatable arm holds its centre and stops with "no_seat". Growth: interleave order on the toy and on 山 川 海 空 森 | 星 月 光 (山 星 川 月 海 光 空 森), seat_key unit with n_then_n1 equals `insertion_order`; z_reserved: an N-only seat that wants a z arm and an N+1-only seat that wants an x arm (free search puts them there) are kept off by the reservation in insertion and swaps, four N seats need L = 2 reserved (L = 1 free), no member of any toy window has an N seat on a z arm or an N+1 seat on an x arm, a one-sentence window has no z seat; `Policy.capacity_ok`. Every toy window x every growth, seat_key and seat_empty_axis value under per_axis ends in a class the verifier calls stable (with the reservation and the denied arms). Regression: the three comparisons of L-588 (toy corpus against the G3-c module, first 40 windows against the committed jsonl) the owner's configuration (the defaults) on the first 6 fulllead windows with the full verifier on every member (sizes 16 9 8 12 13 7, L 7 3 4 4 3 3, classes 260 720 1 204 720 1, axis keys pinned), and per_axis == sum on the one-sentence windows (toy, and 12 fulllead lone windows: members, steps, stop). Spec: LEGACY spec has no `switches` key, defaults, 7 distinct shas over the switch perturbations, bad switch values raise; bytes of every record of all 24 switch combinations identical under three hash seeds; no float in any new record (JSON parsed with a float hook; AST: no float constant / name / division in slide_place.py).

### G3-c2 measurement (fulllead_sents, tier RUN, scope corpus, level mid {1000, 20000, 400000}, padding none, seat_key unit_sid; the 292 pair windows; the Pro, 5-8 processes at once, load 4-7; experiments/line3/g3/place2/*.md, *.jsonl, place2_windows.py)
A run = `place2_windows.py pairs per_axis unit_sid <allow|deny> <growth>`; the record per window is in the jsonl (members left out, seats with sources kept); the md has every count below. The owner's configuration is marked.
| run | N+1 seat STRICT / LOOSE | windows stopping inside N / inside N+1 / never | z-arm units (of the representative): without z evidence on the inner edge / on neither edge, of | class size median / max (windows > max_class) | seats seated (of all seats) | windows with >1 per-axis key | trade-off moves not taken | time |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| G3-c committed (sum, unit, allow, n_then_n1; re-run reproduces the 292 records) | 178 = 60.9 % / 209 = 71.5 % | 113 (4 of them on a shared unit) / 105 / 74 | 547 / 338 of 1000 | 174 / 2160 (2) | 2512 of 4621 (units) | 57 (sum splits) | - | 106 s (91.8 s at the build) |
| 1. per_axis, allow, n_then_n1 | 174 = 59.5 % / 174 = 59.5 % | 114 / 106 / 72 | 570 / 407 of 994 | 240 / 2160 (7) | 2529 of 4785 | 57 | 374 of 12913 | 147 s |
| 2. per_axis, deny, n_then_n1 | same as 1 (292 of 292 classes equal) | same | same | same | same | same | same | 148 s |
| 3. per_axis, allow, interleave | 290 = 99.3 % / 292 = 100 % | 154 / 66 / 72 | 405 / 99 of 1103 | 192 / 1488 (10) | 2734 of 4785 | 86 | 405 of 15880 | 196 s |
| **4. per_axis, allow, z_reserved (the owner's configuration)** | **236 = 80.8 % / 236 = 80.8 %** | **56 / 139 / 97** | 656 / 634 of 953 | 240 / 960 (0); class size 1 in 42 windows | 3150 of 4785 | 0 | 22 of 13830 | 206 s |
| 5. per_axis, deny, z_reserved | same as 4 (292 of 292 classes equal) | same | same | same | same | same | same | 206 s |
Attribution of the G3-c to run 1 change (the two other single changes): sum + unit_sid: STRICT 176 = 60.2 %, LOOSE 177 = 60.6 %, stops inside N 113, never 70, z-arm 534 / 332 of 1010, 53 splits, class 168 / 2160, 110 s; per_axis + unit: STRICT 177 = 60.6 %, LOOSE 209 = 71.5 %, stops inside N 114, never 74, z-arm 575 / 404 of 986, 48 windows with >1 key, class 240 / 2160, 146 s. So per_axis alone moves almost nothing in the stop numbers (it only changes which members the class keeps), and the two seats cost the loose figure its meaning (a shared unit's N seat no longer counts as an N+1 seat) rather than seating fewer units. Seats of sentence N+1 that are seated: run 1 663 of 2363 (28.0 %), run 3 1269 (53.7 %), run 4 953 (40.3 %). Units with two seats: 164 in 102 windows; both seated 39 / 67 / 61 (runs 1 / 3 / 4) and directly linked by one cross edge 13 / 28 / 10. Independent verifier: 292 of 292 classes stable in every run.
The pair windows always hold x and z evidence (one window, [344, 345], has one unit per sentence and so no x), so deny differs from allow only in the one-sentence windows. All 592 windows (`all` runs), the 300 one-sentence windows: n_then_n1 allow / deny: stops inside N 116 / 61, units seated 1765 / 2108 of 2388 (deny exhausts 239 of 300 windows, allow 184), class median 108 / 1, z-arm units 494 / 0, L max 3 / 10; 257 of 300 classes differ. z_reserved allow / deny: identical in all 300 (stops 61, class median 1, no z-arm unit). The 292 pairs of the 'all' runs are the numbers above. Time: 0.50 / 0.51 / 0.67 / 0.71 / 0.71 s per window (runs 1-5, median 0.24 - 0.39 s, max 3.7 - 5.1 s); 5-8 runs were going at once (G3-c: 0.31 s per window alone), so the times are an upper bound.
Reading (numbers, not a decision): z_reserved gives N+1 a seat in 80.8 % of the pairs (G3-c 60.9 %) and halves the windows that die inside N (113 -> 56) but moves the break into N+1 (139 windows): it is the same budget, now hit later; the per-axis classes lose their incomparable keys (0 windows with more than one key) because the reserved arms make the x and the z parts independent. Interleave seats the most N+1 seats (53.7 % of them) and 99.3 % strict, with 154 windows dying inside N. The z-arm units without z evidence rise under z_reserved (656 of 953 = 69 % against 55 %), see open point 4: of the seats on the z arms under run 4 only 15 of 306 on the +z arm and 282 of 647 on the -z arm have z evidence on their inner edge (run 1: 160 of 378 and 264 of 616).

### G3-c2 open points (owner; meaning level)
(1) Per-axis stability is built as PARETO (L-581): no axis falls and one rises. The strict reading (a state is stable when NO move improves ANY axis) cannot be a termination rule (a trade-off and its inverse alternate; stable states need not exist); alternatives: lexicographic in the ladder order x, y, z (decide by x, only then by z), a weighted sum with the foundation weights (233 : 89 : 34), or the strict reading used as a test only (`tradeoff_counts`: how many single moves of the representative improve one axis and worsen another: 374 of 12913 under n_then_n1, 22 of 13830 under z_reserved). Which is the owner's 「各軸がそれぞれ安定を持つ」? (review, 2026-10-09) Pareto is the implementer's constructive choice, not the literal gloss: 「各軸で改善が無いときに安定」 reads as the strict condition (no move improves any axis). The non-termination argument is about a SEARCH rule; as a stability JUDGEMENT the strict condition is a well-defined predicate on a state and is already measured: a Pareto fixed point with no trade-off move (`tradeoffs.improve_one_worsen_another` = 0, verifier `swaps_tradeoff`) is also strictly stable. Representatives that are: 274 of 292 under z_reserved (run 4), 189 of 292 under n_then_n1 (run 1). A third reading (each axis its own arrangement, judged by its own key alone) does not fit one cross per window and is listed only.
(2) A per-axis class can hold several INCOMPARABLE per-axis keys (n_then_n1: 57 of 292 windows, up to 4 keys; interleave 86, up to 5; z_reserved none). Is a class with two keys one answer (keep all members and let G3-d read each) or should a rule pick one key (which needs a preference among axes, point 1)?
(3) `deny` is the axis rule (an arm of an axis with no evidence in the window has no seats). It only matters for the 300 one-sentence windows (z) and, under z_reserved, not at all. The per-unit variant (a unit may sit on a z arm only if it has at least one z edge, on an x arm only with an x edge, ...) is not built: with it a seat could not be a filler on any arm; 570 of 994 z-arm units have no z evidence on the inner edge in run 1 (407 on neither edge). Build it?
(4) z_reserved and the direction of z. The +z arm's edge reads "outer in N, inner in N+1" (G3 3.2), the -z arm "outer in N+1, inner in N"; with the z arms reserved for N+1 seats the centre (a sentence-N unit) is the inner end of the innermost seat, which is the -z reading, and the +z arm holds N+1 seats in the reversed direction: 15 of 306 seats on +z have z evidence on their inner edge, 282 of 647 on -z (and beyond the innermost seat both neighbours are N+1 seats, whose z edge counts only by corpus coincidence). Options: reserve +z for N seats and -z for N+1 seats (then x and +z both hold N), flip the +z weight for N+1 seats, or give the z arms one direction only. Not decided here. (review, 2026-10-09) By depth (run 4, representatives; the centre is a sentence-N seat in 292 of 292): -z depth 1 (inner = centre, the only N -> N+1 edge of the cross) 236 of 236 evidenced (the window's own pair always counts in corpus scope; 7 of them self-edges); +z depth 1 9 of 129 (its weight n_z(o, i) needs the pair in the reversed sentence order elsewhere: 3 self-edges, 6 coincidences); deeper z seats (N+1 -> N+1 edges) -z 46 of 411, +z 6 of 177 (corpus coincidence). So the z axis holds at most one real slide edge per window, and 192 of 292 windows have exactly one evidenced z edge (56 none). The section walk starts at the OUTER end (L-55): in the 20 review windows (owner configuration, bank2 questions) the 120 z-arm walks stop 81 on an empty outer seat and 39 on an unproven N+1 -> N+1 step, 0 reach the centre, so R1_z cannot work. Further options: z arms of depth 1 (one N+1 seat per z arm), deeper z edges weighed by the word order of N+1 (an x-type count), or the centre may also be an N+1 seat.
(5) z_reserved lets the arms GROW until the reserved arms hold their sentence (L up to 8 on the pairs, 10 on a one-sentence window of 21 units since only the two x arms may hold N): arm length is not bounded by the budget. Alternative: stop when the reserved arms are full (like an F1 stop, `left` listing the rest), or bound L. Which is meant by "z の腕は N+1 の語専用"? (review, 2026-10-09) The growth is the literal consequence of the reservation, one arm length for all arms and no stop (not a slip); its only bound is `bound = len(order) + 2` in `grow` (never reached when a seat exists: at L = len(order) every allowed arm has room), an unnumbered cap. Longer arms leave the z arms' outer seats empty, which is where the section walk starts (point 4).
(6) The two seats of one unit have the same unit-level weights except for their mutual z self-edge: their sentence changes where they may sit (z_reserved) and in which order they are inserted, not what an edge weighs. Should an edge's z weight depend on the seats' sentences (a +z edge needs the outer seat in N and the inner in N+1)? It would be a different key (and change seat_key "unit" too).
(7) Interleave: 99.3 % strict and 53.7 % of N+1's seats seated, but 154 windows still die inside N and 10 end with a class above max_class; z_reserved: 80.8 % strict, 56 die inside N. Neither fixes the budget (214 of the 220 stops of run 1 are max_class on classes of tied fillers): a larger level or the quotient over interchangeable seats (the twin quotient of L-90 is not applied, L-561) is the structural cure; the max_class defect (a class can end above max_class) is a separate task.
(8) The search is local (swaps only; a Pareto fixed point is weaker than a summed-key fixed point, which is itself not the optimum, G3-c open point 5). Is a Pareto fixed point enough as "placed"?

## G3-c3 (2026-10-09): the centre in either sentence, the arm length measured both ways, stability judged strictly (verantyx/line3/slide_place.py; tests/line3/test_slide_place3.py; tests/line3/test_slide_place2.py one fixture; experiments/line3/g3/place3/)
Owner (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md, 「G3-c2 の実装後の決定」 and 「G3-c2/G3-d の監査後の決定」, verbatim choices): 「中心も N+1 の語になれる（中心の探索を両文に広げる）」 (the centre is searched over the units of both sentences; with the centre in N+1 the x arms are N+1 and the z arms are N: symmetric); 「両方測ってから決める」 for the arm length ((a) grow until the budget stops, (b) as long as the x arms); 「厳密：どの軸にも改善する手が無いときだけ安定」 (the search stays Pareto, only the judgement is strict; an axis that still has an improving move means unstable = not judgeable). One module is extended (slide_place.py) and one new test file added; placement.py, slide.py, slide_ratios.py, ask.py, cli.py, slide_query.py (G3-e, written concurrently) are untouched. The record only gains fields (`NEW_RECORD_FIELDS_C3`); `record.seats` (unit / sid / arm / position / sources), `axis_keys`, `items`, `self_links`, `tradeoffs` keep their meaning. Judgement points are numbered L-620.. (L-600..L-616 are G3-d).

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-620 | Switch `centre_scope`: "n" (G3-c2: the first unit of N is the centre and the centre moves by search among what the reservation allows) | "both". "both" only has a meaning with growth `z_reserved` (without the reservation the centre already moves freely; the switch is then a recorded no-op, tested). **The centre cannot move between sentences by a swap**: with x arms reserved for one sentence, swapping the centre with an arm seat of the other sentence leaves every other seat on an arm it is not allowed on (that is why run 4 shows the centre in N in 292 of 292). So "search the centre over both sentences" is TWO GROWTHS per two-sentence window: growth N (centre = first unit of N, x arms = N, z arms = N+1, order N then N+1; the G3-c2 growth) and growth N+1 (centre = first unit of N+1, x arms = N+1 in its word order, z arms = N, order N+1 then N). Inside a growth the centre moves by swaps among its own sentence's units (it takes only a unit of the sentence its x arms are reserved for, or a unit of both under seat_key "unit": `Policy.centre_only`); the N growth no longer lets an N+1 unit sit at the centre (G3-c2 allowed it; it never happened). A one-sentence window has one growth. | slide_place.PlaceSpec.centre_scope, place_window, Policy.centre_only |
| L-621 | The reservation is mirrored, not rewritten: `Policy(x_side="this"|"next")`. x arms take the units of `x_side`'s sentence (or "both"), z arms the other (or "both"), y arms any, as before; capacity (Hall's conditions) exchanges the roles (centre's sentence <= centre + x + y seats, other sentence <= z + y seats when the centre is closed to it, all <= all seats). `grow(..., x_side, centre_only, cap_x)`; `seat_items(..., centre="next")` reads N+1 first (all of N+1 in word order, then all of N). `side` of an item stays the corpus fact (this = N, next = N+1), so `record.seats`, `items`, `slide_ratios.window_cross` need no change (checked: `slide_ratios.window_crosses` reads a centre-in-N+1 record, members included). With seat_key "unit" a unit of both sentences is seated when first read, so its `sid` is that of the sentence read first in each growth; `items` (and `order_log`, `seat_order`) of the record are those of the REPRESENTATIVE's growth (review fix: they were the N growth's, so a centre-in-N+1 representative carried N's insertion order beside the N+1 growth's `steps`); under seat_key "unit" a shared unit's `sid` in `items` / `seats` is therefore that of the sentence the representative's growth read first, and the members of the other growth in the same record are read through the same `items` (seat_key "unit" is not the default; under the default unit_sid the two growths have the same tokens and sids, only the order differs). Per-weight nothing changes: with the centre in N+1 and N on the z arms the +z edge (outer in N, inner in N+1) is the right-directed one and -z the reversed one, the exact mirror of run 4. | slide_place.Policy, grow, seat_items |
| L-622 | The merge. The final classes of the two growths are compared member by member on the per-axis key (6-vector of L-581): a member is kept unless a member of the OTHER growth's class dominates it; equal keys keep both; incomparable keys keep both (`choice` "this", "next", "both_equal", "both_incomparable", "single"). Members are ordered growth N first, then growth N+1, each sorted as before, so the representative (`members[0]`) is the N growth's first member whenever the N growth survives; `order_log`, `items`, `seat_order`, `steps`, `stop`, `broke_on`, `left`, `size`, `unseated` are those of the representative's growth. The order rule (growth N first) decides the representative's centre sentence in every window that keeps both growths (106 of 292 in run A); the record shows it (`centre_search.choice` both_*, `member_x_side`) but does not name the rule (open point 1). The comparison is made between FINAL classes, which may have seated different numbers of units (budget stops): a growth that stopped early has fewer edges and usually loses; that is a rule of this ticket, not a measured virtue (open point 1). | slide_place._finish |

Tried first and rejected: settling the shorter growth's class again at the longer length; it broke max_class in 144 of 292 growths (the longer arms multiply the equal-key arrangements of the fillers) and so made the centre choice an artifact of arm length. Padding is the same defect as G3-c2 open point 4 (empty outer seats are where the section walk starts), now also in 89 growths of run A. | slide_place._finish, verify_*(own_L / own_Ls) |

Hand tables: Policy with x_side "next" / centre_only (arms, centre, capacity); a table where the best centre is a unit of N+1 (x arm holds N+1, z arms hold N; the N+1 growth's key dominates the N growth's; the independent verifier accepts it with x_side "next" and rejects it under x_side "this"). Toys: a window whose placement picks the centre in N+1 (`choice` "next", every seat on an x arm is of N+1, on a z arm of N, the centre of N+1, verifier stable with every member checked, `other_seat` strict), equal and incomparable keys keep both growths (members of both orientations, each legal), the shorter growth is padded and stable at its own length (the verifier without `own_Ls` counts more moves), every member of every toy window under every combination of centre_scope x arm_cap x seat_key x growth is legal and verifier-stable, centre_scope "both" without the reservation is the same growth. arm_cap x on a table (z arms stop at the x length, the unseated units are listed, `stop` "cap", `left`, step status; budget grows to L 2; inert without the reservation and when the other sentence fits) and in the record. Strict judgement: a Pareto-stable state with an improving x move is UNSTABLE_AXIS_IMPROVABLE with axes ["x"], the independent count equals the record's, the pareto rule marks nothing, members counted; a reservation removes the improving move; the committed run-4 windows with trade-off moves (6 of the first 40 pair windows, 4 checked + 2 without) are marked with the axes the old `tradeoffs.improved_axis` named and `improving_moves_left == tradeoffs.improved_axis` there; the judgement rule does not change the search. Regression: centre_scope "n" + arm_cap "budget" + pareto (`C2_EQUIV`) equals the committed G3-c2 run-4 records of the first 40 pair windows (every field, timing and runner fields excluded, the G3-c3 fields stripped; spec sha256 equal); the same on all 292 pair windows in the run below (0 mismatches); LEGACY byte-identical to G3-c is still tested in test_slide_place.py / test_slide_place2.py. Spec: the new switches appear only when they differ, 5 distinct shas, bad values raise. Hash seeds 0 / 1 / 12345 give identical bytes over every new switch combination; no float in any record or spec; the verifier's source names none of the search's names.

(A real N<->N+1 edge = a cross edge whose two ends are seats of different sentences, self-edges of a shared unit included; "with a count" is median 1 / max 2 as well. In A the 186 windows with one surviving growth are 112 N + 74 N+1; in 13 of them the winning growth seated fewer units than the loser. 89 growths of A were padded (L-623).)

### G3-c3 open points (owner; meaning level)
(1) Which growth is "the best centre"? The two final classes are compared by their per-axis key although they may have seated different numbers of units (they differ in 186 of 292 windows) and a growth that stopped early has fewer edges. 106 windows keep both (15 equal, 91 incomparable: one growth is higher on x, the other on z) and the representative is then the N growth by the order rule, which is arbitrary: the "centre in N+1" share is 74 of 292 as representatives, 180 if a window counts when any member has the centre in N+1. Compare on equal footing (same seated set), prefer the growth that seated more, or ask the key's axis preference (G3-c2 point 1)?
(2) The symmetric centre answers the DIRECTION of z, not the NUMBER of cross-sentence edges. With the centre in one sentence and the z arms holding the other, the only N<->N+1 edges are the two innermost z edges (centre to the depth-1 seat of +z and of -z): median 1, max 2 in every run, 51 windows none (D: 56), 138 two (D: 129). Depth-1 z edges now carry evidence in 52-74 % (D: +z 7 %, -z 100 %: the centre in N made -z the only right-directed arm); the deeper z edges are N->N or N+1->N+1 pairs and carry evidence only by corpus coincidence (9.5-13 %). So the z axis still holds at most two slide edges. Precisely: the y arms hold no seat, so every edge joins two adjacent seats of one arm or an arm's innermost seat and the centre; the x arms and the centre hold only the centre's sentence and the z arms only the other sentence, so the only edges whose ends are seats of different sentences are (centre, +z depth 1) and (centre, -z depth 1): at most two per member, whichever sentence holds the centre and under either arm_cap (checked on the records of runs A-D: 0 such edges anywhere else). Options: (a) accept (the z axis is structurally a one-or-two-edge axis; G3-d's single-edge agreement rule is then the z norm, not the exception); (b) deeper z edges carry the other sentence's word-order evidence (an x-type count of the other sentence on the z arms); (c) more than one centre-like link between the sentences (e.g. several seats of the centre's sentence that open a z edge, or a z arm that alternates the sentences); (d) z arms of depth 1 only (one seat of the other sentence per z arm); (e) both z arms holding the other sentence in both directions.
(3) arm_cap x vs budget (A vs B, same search otherwise). x skips units of the other sentence that do not fit: 351 units in 52 windows (18 %); it halves the class (median 426 -> 216, windows above max_class 16 -> 7, 438 s -> 353 s), keeps the strict share (82.5 % both), seats fewer other-sentence seats (810 vs 875) and raises the z-evidence share (37.4 % vs 34.8 %); the representative stability is the same (94.1 vs 94.5 %). The cap is hard when the centre's sentence is short: a 2-unit centre sentence gives L = 1 and two z seats, whatever the other sentence has. Which is meant by 「x の腕と同じ長さまで」: the length the centre's sentence needed (built), or the length of the seated x arms after the search (often shorter), or a ratio? Decide before it becomes the default.
(4) Strict stability: 16 of 292 representatives are not strictly stable in A (all on z; D 18), but 15 of those 16 windows have a class member that is: a representative chosen among the strictly stable members would abstain in 1 window instead of 16 (97.4 % of all members pass). Is the unit of judgement the representative (built: `unstable`), the class (all members), or "some member" (`first_stable_strict_member`)? A trade-off move is one the Pareto search refuses on purpose (z down for x up); calling the state unstable for it means abstaining where the arrangement is a legitimate Pareto optimum.
(5) The merged class is the union of two growths' classes: up to 2079 members (16 windows above max_class 1000, D none): the budget counts each growth, not the union (the max_class defect of L-561 is still a separate task).
(6) The shorter growth is padded at the outer end (L-623): its outer seats are empty and a reader that walks from the outer end (R1_z, G2 section walk) starts on an empty seat in 89 growths more than before. Cap x gives shorter arms (L median 4 both) only when it bites. Should a record keep one record per growth instead of one merged L?
(7) Budget stops still decide most outcomes: 175 of the 186 budget stops in A are max_class on classes of tied fillers (D 187 of 195); 135 representatives stop inside the OTHER sentence and 51 inside the centre's. The structural cure is still the quotient over interchangeable seats (L-561, G3-c2 point 7).
(8) seat_key "unit" with centre_scope "both" (shared units seated once) is built and tested but not measured: a shared unit's `sid` is that of the sentence read first, which differs between the two growths.

## G3-e (2026-10-09): the question path over the sliding windows, `structure="slide"` (verantyx/line3/slide_query.py; ask.py / cli.py opt-in hook; tests/line3/test_slide_query.py; experiments/line3/g3/s1/; docs/LINE3_G3_SLIDING_PACKS.md 5, 6.1, 7.3)
Owner (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md): OP-G3-1/2 「文ごとの 3 層パック。この文と次の文。1 文ずつ重ねて進む」「同じ記事の隣り合う 2 文」 (the windows); G3 4 「軸ごとに判定し、一致した軸がそれぞれラベル付きの答えを出す」 (one labelled entry per agreeing axis, never merged); G3 5 「文法層の十字の回転を「手」にして、助詞の腕をデータの腕に合わせる」 and the G2-f / G3-a/b audit 「入力があると各ソブリンに投入して、そこから両替機のように形でタンクに入れる」 (the question is classified by FORM and the form selects the READ ORDER; candidates are not changed); G3 6 「梯子の順に x、y、z へ。重みは辺の流れと配置の結合と読む順に」 (the weights are slide_ratios'; here only the order of the axes); G3-c 「記事ごとに仮の文を 1 つ足す」 (one padding sentence per article); M-2 / N-18 (the amount of inference is the user's choice; T7b presets fast / standard / full); the evaluation principle (candidates for users to grade, typed abstention, no single confident wrong answer). One new module, an opt-in keyword in ask.py (`structure`, default "flat") and three flags in cli.py (`--structure flat|slide`, `--agreement`, `--slide-members`; `line3 build --structure slide`); slide.py / slide_place.py / slide_ratios.py / grammar.py are only read (G3-c3 edits slide_place.py concurrently: nothing here depends on its new switches; `stable_strict` is read when the record has it). Every existing output is byte-identical (the goldens of test_ask.py / test_matryoshka.py are untouched).

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-640 | Scope. S1: tier RUN only (a WORD / CHAR window is refused, `ValueError`); padding "one" (the owner's one constructed sentence per article: 292 pair windows + 300 padded one-sentence windows = 592 on fulllead); the place spec is `slide_place.make_spec` with its module defaults (`place_kw` passes switches through; G3-c3's are not named here); the budget level is the flat index's (`--level`, mid). PROVENANCE OF THE MEASUREMENT BELOW: run from a scratch copy of the committed tree (HEAD `ede17119`: the G3-c2 `slide_place.py`, i.e. centre in N, arms until the budget stops, Pareto judgement; the concurrent G3-c3 edit of the working tree is NOT in it) plus the uncommitted slide_query.py / ask.py / cli.py of this ticket (their sha256 are in each run's `.meta.json`); corpus sha `725d5ac1a8d2`, slide spec `3df141b12327`, place spec `e2b02c23e287` (per_axis / unit_sid / allow / z_reserved, level mid, padding one). Once G3-c3 lands, `make_spec`'s module defaults may change the place-spec sha: the cache then refuses to load and must be rebuilt, and these are G3-e numbers on G3-c2 placements. The result is `SlideAnswer` (own class: it is not a `Combined`; tiers are not merged because there is one). | slide_query.TIER, WindowIndex.from_space |
| L-641 | Window index and cache. `WindowIndex` = the 592 placement records (`SlidePlacement.doc(members=True)` in canonical JSON form, so that a change of the dataclass cannot break an old cache and `slide_ratios.window_cross` reads it by duck typing) + each window's seated unit set, class size and grammar kind. Cache file `slidewin_<corpus12>_RUN_<slide12>_<place12>.pkl` (pickle of {format, corpus_sha256, slide_spec_sha256, place_spec_sha256, tier, padding, scope, switches, windows, wall_ms, cpu_ms}) in the flat index's cache dir; loading checks format, the three shas, tier and padding (a mismatch = `ValueError`, never read) and then every window's n / sids / title / constructed / place spec sha against the corpus; without the file `from_space(build=False)` raises, with `build=True` it places the windows (fork pool, `imap_unordered`, ordered by window number afterwards: the bytes do not depend on the worker count) and writes the file atomically. fulllead: 592 windows, 39 s wall / 232 CPU-s with 6 workers, 22.5 MB, loaded in 0.65 s. `line3 build --structure slide --cache DIR` builds it; `ask(structure="slide")` places in-process (about 4 core-minutes) if it is absent. | WindowIndex, cache_name |
| L-642 | Intake and FORM. The question is cut like the flat path (`cycle.split_question("RUN")`, then V2 `funcwords.default_filter`), `cycle.make_context` (I-07), and read by `grammar.read_question(question, space)` (the same space and SpanIndex). Form precedence: `slot` (a P7 particle after the interrogative phrase) > `predicate` (the XのYは何ですか slot, L-548) > `standin` (an unknown RUN unit: pattern type T2..T5) > `plain`. fulllead 94 questions: plain 2, predicate 50, slot 25, standin 17. THE FORM SELECTS THE READ ORDER in the simplest literal way: form `slot` passes its particle to `grammar.read_order` (the group whose kind is that single particle is read first), every other form passes None (ladder weight only, no match group; a tied kind never matches); the form is in `answer.grammar_form` and the whole reading in `thought.grammar`. | Intake, classify_form, intake |
| L-643 | Axis order (G3 4.2 step 3). The axis whose arm carries the slot particle first (の: +x -> x; を: +z -> z; は is the centre: no arm), the other axes by the axis weight (`slide_ratios.read_order`); no slot: the weight order. It orders the entries of one window only. | axis_order |
| L-644 | Candidate windows = V1 for windows: a window that HOLDS a question unit, i.e. a unit of `ctx.energy_units` (the first layer, as V1's `q`) on a SEAT of the window (`hold="seats"`, default; budget-stopped units are not in the cross) or, as a variant, in its sentences (`hold="sentences"`). A window with no such unit is never read: the exact skip. The seated set is the same for every member of a class on the committed records (592/592 on fulllead; a window with two growths may differ: L-658). Stand-ins (`standins="on"`, default off): the RUN stand-in units of the question's unknown units (grammar pattern) also make a window a candidate (candidacy only: they are NOT added to the question units, E_Q is unchanged; an entry from a window that holds only stand-ins is marked `via_standin`). | holds, plan_windows |
| L-645 | Read order and the cap. Candidates are ordered by `grammar.read_order(slot_or_None, [(window, kind)])` (kind of a window = `grammar.grammar_cross(records, N, sids).kind`: the six arm particles of the WORD records of its sentences, L-549), then, INSIDE a group, by the number of question units held (more first; stand-ins second) — L-G3-7's "(group, E_Q descending)" in the only form a window has; equal values are ONE BLOCK, read together or not at all (`within="qcount"`, default; `within="none"` = grammar groups only). The cap is T7b's, counted in windows: whole blocks in order, the first block that does not fit stops the read (`ReadPlan` semantics): fast 4 / standard 10 / full unbounded (`ask.EFFORTS`), `nodes N` = N windows. Reported: candidates, windows read, left unread, would read in full, tied block not split, `partial`. A first block larger than the cap reads nothing (UNKNOWN_NOT_READ, partial): fulllead fast, 4 of 94 questions (3 of the 69 answerable). `shuffle=<seed>` (test hook) permutes blocks and windows inside a block. | plan_windows, Plan, Block |
| L-646 | THE ADMISSION GATE (what makes the skip exact). E_Q is the corpus', so a window with no question unit still has flows and agreeing axes: without a rule it would answer (toy: yes; fulllead: every one of the 94 questions would gain on average 20 entries from windows that hold no question unit, 1878 in all, `results/skip_check.txt`). The rule is the window-level N-03: a member's agreement on an axis is admitted only if the window holds a question unit (same predicate as L-644). So reading everything (`skip="none"`) and reading only the candidates give identical entries, abstentions and verdict by construction of the rule, and the test is that the plumbing keeps it so: 94/94 questions equal on fulllead with representatives (read 637 vs 55648 windows, 87 times fewer; `results/skip_check.txt`) and 12/12 with every member (448 extra entries in 347 non-candidate windows; `results/skip_check_all_members.txt`), all toy questions under both agreement rules and both member modes. THE ACCEPTANCE IS MET BY A RULE, not discovered: the test proves the plumbing keeps the rule, and open point 6 asks whether the rule is the intended semantics. `gate=False` (test hook) shows the other side. (review, 2026-10-09) `ask_slide` keeps only the PLANNED windows' entries and abstentions, so "a == b" alone holds whatever the gate does; the fulllead check of the gate is that the windows the exact skip did not read admit no entry under the gate: `skip_check.py` now counts them and the rerun (`results/skip_check.txt`, 94 questions, representatives) finds 0 (the other numbers unchanged); `skip_check_all_members.txt` (12 questions) predates the column (the toy test checked it from the start). Unlike CARRY's exact skip (L-313: a consequence of N-03 on a LOCAL space), here N-03 as defined (L-104: the attached query unit is in the space) holds in almost every window, because attachment, E_Q and the counts are the corpus' (L-G3-1): the skip is exact only because V1 for windows is promoted to an admission rule. | read_window |
| L-647 | Grounded (N-03 on the answer, L-607's open gate). An agreeing axis whose working sections have no in-space query unit attached (`AxisAnswer.grounded == 0`) is not admitted (typed `not_grounded`), EXCEPT when the section walk was not applicable (`section is None`, agreement `two_if_single_edge` on a one-edge axis): there `grounded` is 0 by construction and gating it would delete every answer the owner asked to measure; those are judged by the window gate (L-646) only. | read_window |
| L-648 | `stable_strict` and the members that are read (owner, 「G3-c3 の実装後の決定」: read ONLY the class members that are strictly stable; a record with no strictly stable member is an abstention of type UNSTABLE_AXIS_IMPROVABLE for that window). Flags read from the record: `judgement.member_judgement[i].stable_strict` per member (the members=True form), else the window's `stable_strict` (the representative's; a bool or a mapping axis -> bool); ABSENT = every member strictly stable (the committed G3-c2 records). Policy `strict="abstain"` (default, the owner's rule): an unstable member is NOT READ (no `read_axes`, no trace); it is counted `unstable_axis_improvable` in the typed abstention / `members_not_admitted` of every axis of the window (`members` = the whole class under `members="all"`; L-652); a window with no stable member reads nothing and its three axes are `unstable_axis_improvable` abstentions (verdict `UNKNOWN_UNSTABLE_AXIS_IMPROVABLE` when nothing else is listed). `strict="mark"` (measurement hook only) reads every member and sets `stable_strict: false` on an entry that an unstable member agrees on. An entry's `stable_strict` is the AND over its agreeing members. The S1 numbers below are on committed G3-c2 records, which carry none of these fields: re-run with this code, the 94 records are identical. | WindowRec.stable_member / readable, choose_members |
| L-649 | Members. A window's record is a CLASS of equal-key arrangements and the ratios depend on the arrangement (L-613): `members="all"` (default) reads `read_axes` on EVERY member (fulllead: 109120 members in 592 windows, median class 3, max 960; a question reads 6 windows at the median) and lists, per axis, one entry per distinct agreed unit with `arrangements` = the members that agree on it (the L-180 pattern; `members_agreeing` / `members_read` / `class_size`), `centres` = the centres of those members, the ratios / seats / sources of the first agreeing member (`member`); nothing is chosen between members. `members="representative"` reads ONE member per growth (the first strictly stable member of each centre sentence; one member when the record has one growth, i.e. member 0 for the committed records; `class_size` still reported): 0.10 s per question against 0.54 s. Cost does not demand the switch (all members, 94 questions: median 0.5 s, max 1.9 s at fast; median 1.2 s, max 5.8 s at full, load 16). | read_window |
| L-650 | Entry shape (T7b's keys + the labels). `tier` "RUN", `structure` "slide", `axis`, `label` "slide:x" / "slide:z", `words` [unit], `arrangements`, `centres`, `stability` null (T7b's stability is the cycle's invariance; there is no such quantity here and none is invented: the share `members_agreeing / members_read` is reported as its own fields), `source_sids` (the sentences of the unit's seats and of the counts' rows: x rows (sid, ...), z rows (sid N, sid N+1, ...)), `window` {n, title, sids, idx, constructed}, `stable_strict`, `via_standin`, `ratios` {section, edge_flow, binding} (exact Fractions, "n/d" in the bytes) and `ratios_plain`, `grounded`, `agreement`, `grammar_kind`, `path_words` (diagnostic: the units of the walk steps and of the edge partners; NOT scored in the headline), `seats` (every seat of the unit, keyed (unit, sid), with the occurrence rows), `sources` (axis-prefixed count rows), `trace` {ok, members_checked}. Window index (592-style build on a toy: 5 pairs + 4 padded windows; cache roundtrip; a file for another corpus / slide spec / place spec / tier / padding is refused by its header, a record that does not match the corpus is refused, a missing file with build=False raises, seated sets uniform over a class); intake and FORM (slot / predicate / standin / plain), the read order (the slot's kind first, then the ladder, only an ordering), the axis order; THE EXACT SKIP (entries + abstentions + verdict identical for skip exact / none, both agreement rules, both member modes, all 12 toy questions; without the gate a non-candidate window does answer); order independence (shuffled order, 3 seeds, both `within` modes, same entries); the cap (a prefix of whole blocks for every cap 0..candidates+1, the first block that does not fit stops, a tied block larger than the cap reads nothing and is not split, T7b's presets in windows); partial in the answer; the entry shape (T7b keys + axis / label / window / stable_strict / trace / seats / sources); never merged across axes or windows (the same article twice answers twice); typed abstentions (every axis of every window read is an entry or an abstention, kinds add up); ### G3-e measurement (fulllead_sents, bank2 intra2 69 + unans 25, tier RUN, padding one, level mid, the committed G3-c2 defaults; fast = 4 windows; 6 workers on the Pro, load 4-17; experiments/line3/g3/s1/results/summary.md, raw .jsonl + .meta.json (argv, shas, code sha256) beside it; the 39 s window build in a scratch dir)
Reach first: the gold is a SEATED RUN unit of a window that holds a question unit in **40 / 69** (6 candidate windows per question at the median, max 22): that is the ceiling of a unit-valued entry; 18 of the 69 golds are not one unit of any tier.

What the numbers say (the facts; the reading is the owner's):
- windows read: fast reads 4 windows at the median of 6 candidates; 44 of the 69 questions are partial and 3 read nothing (the first tied block is larger than 4); standard / full leave 14 / 0 partial and add one or two hits, so the cap is not what limits S1.
- Between "seated" (40) and "agreed" (0-2) the loss is in the agreement (review: 40 is counted over every candidate window, no cap; at fast a gold window is READ for 28 of them, the other 12 are lost to the cap first — the grammar order reads the slot's group first: for I2-002 / I2-012 / I2-027 / I2-028 the 4 windows read are the same 4 of the と group, which hold only 呼 of 呼ばれますか): under `three` 2 entries in 94 questions at fast (the 69 answerable questions' (window, axis) pairs: section_disagreement 134, points_nowhere 91, ratio_disagreement 28, mixed 102, y always no_edges 294); under `two_if_single_edge` 164 of the 166 entries (94 questions) are on z — the answer is an end of the ONE real N -> N+1 edge — and they depend on the arrangement: only 2 of the 166 are agreed by every member of the class (`unanimous members only`: 0 hits, 0-5 entries), 3 of 166 are a question unit, 63 are the window's centre, 61 of 166 have a question unit on their path; `grounded` is 0 for 164.
- the other golds are rarely held either (chance: another question's gold in an entry 0.3 % per other question): the entries are mostly units nobody asked for.
- unans: `three` abstains 25 of 25 at fast (the typed verdicts: ratio disagreement 21, section disagreement 2, no evidence 1, not read 1); `two_if_single_edge` gives 7 single confident answers at fast (5 at full) — it breaks "no single confident wrong answer" on the questions that have no answer.
- time: 94 questions in 10-12 s wall with 6 workers at fast; 0.54 s per question at the median (all members), 0.10 s with representatives; the window build is 39 s once.
- the exact skip on the real corpus: 94 / 94 questions equal, 637 windows read instead of 55648; 1878 entries (about 20 per question, 94 of 94 questions) would have come from windows that hold no question unit.

### G3-e open points (owner; meaning level)
1. S1 as specified gives no usable answer: gold in a candidate 0 / 69 at fast for both agreement rules (2 / 69 at full under the two-ratio rule) against 16 for flat-fast and 21 for ssp, with 40 / 69 golds seated in a read window. The loss is the agreement (and, under the two-ratio rule, the arrangement): is the next step a different rule for the z axis (an answer must lie on an evidenced path that starts at a question unit's seat, like T8f's qpath; unanimity over the class; a z edge only counts when it has more than one real N -> N+1 edge), or S2 (y: the WORD units and the stand-ins) before judging the windows?
2. `agreement`: `three` abstains on the unanswerable questions (25 / 25) and answers almost nothing; `two_if_single_edge` answers 28 / 69 questions with a list and gives 7 / 25 confident single answers on the unanswerable ones. The principle (no single confident wrong answer) and the owner's two-ratio idea pull apart on the same data: which one is S1's rule?
3. A window's answer depends on the arrangement of its class (median 3 members, max 960). Listed: any agreeing member (`arrangements` = how many). Alternatives: every member must agree (unanimous: nothing is left), the share as the order of the list, or one arrangement chosen by stability (L-107). Which?
4. `words = [unit]` (one unit, I-11) cannot hold a gold that is several units (18 of 69 are not one unit of any tier; "ハッシュ表" is RUN ハッシュ + 表): the diagnostic `unit+path` gives 2 / 4 at fast / full. Should the entry carry the section-path words (T6w's form) as `words`, as T7b's entries do?
5. The cap counts windows in whole tied blocks, and a block larger than the cap reads nothing (3 of 69 answerable questions read nothing at fast). The within-group key (question units held, L-645) is this ticket's own choice for L-G3-7's "E_Q descending". Reading none of a too-large first block, or splitting it by the number of question units, or reading it anyway?
6. The admission gate (L-646) is a rule: without it windows that hold no question unit answer about 20 times per question. The same question applies to a candidate window: its entry is a unit that the question may not touch (61 of 166 entries have a question unit on their path). Should an entry require a path from a question unit?
7. `stable_strict` / growths (L-648, L-658) now follow the owner's decisions; not measured here because the S1 run is on committed G3-c2 records. Open: on G3-c3 records many windows may become window abstentions (on the toy, 1 of 9 windows has no stable member and 1 has 2 of 8), and every extra growth member is read; the S1 run should be repeated on a G3-c3 cache (`sweep.sh` with a new cache dir, about 1 minute) before the numbers above are used for a decision.
8. The grammar form selects only the match group (form `slot`: 25 of 94 questions) and the axis order; `predicate` (50) and `standin` (17) questions are read in ladder order, like `plain` (L-642). The 両替機 reading may need more than a read order (OP-G3-5): which form should select what?

### G3-e review (2026-10-09, Opus): why the agreement loses the 40 seated golds (member 0 of each gold window; `three` / `two_if_single_edge`; `experiments/line3/g3/s1/gold_loss.py` -> `results/gold_loss.txt`, run on the same G3-c2 window cache; rerun it on a G3-c3 cache with the S1 sweep)
Cause of the gold instance closest to agreeing, per question, the cap counted first at fast: cap (no gold window read) 12 / 12; the section walk passes the gold and ends further in (at the centre, an energy drop or an unproven step) 9 / 9; the gold's arm walk is `empty` (its OUTER seat is empty: G3-c2 fills a short arm from the centre outward, and `_Reader.walk` starts at position 0; 533 of the 1511 seated arms of the 592 windows) 6 / 5; the gold is on a z arm with no evidenced z edge (both ends in N+1: 536 of the 588 outer z edges have n_z = 0; only the edge to the centre carries N -> N+1) 5 / 6; the gold is on the z arm's one evidenced edge 5 / 5; R1 = gold but R3 elsewhere 1 / 1; section disagreement that includes the gold 1 / 1; the walk stops before the gold 1 / 1; the strict-member filter 0 / 0 (the G3-c2 records carry no flag). Without the cap (every candidate window): walk passes the gold 17 / 13, z one evidenced edge 8 / 6, outer seat empty 6 / 5, stops before 3 / 3, section disagreement 2 / 2, gold at the centre with no walk reaching it 2 / 2, R1 = gold 1 / 1, z no evidence 1 / 6, agreed on the gold 0 / 2 (= the 2 hits at full). Up to 40 members per window: no member of a gold window agrees on the gold under `three`; 2 questions under the two-ratio rule. Counterfactual (not a proposal): starting each walk at the first seated cell recovers 0 golds. R2 or R3 points to the gold in 11 of the 40 questions, both in 3. Two-ratio rule at fast: 16 single wrong answers on ANSWERABLE questions too (the table), besides the 7 on unans of open point 2. The binding loss is R1's form: a walk ends at the centre or at a break, so a gold in the middle of an arm, or on a z arm away from the centre, is never R1.

## G3-c4 (2026-10-09): the deeper z-arm edges measured under two evidence rules, `z_deep` "slide" | "order" (verantyx/line3/slide.py `SlideSpec.z_deep`, slide_place.py, slide_ratios.py; tests/line3/test_slide_place4.py; experiments/line3/g3/c4/)
Owner (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md, 「G3-c3 の実装後の決定」, verbatim): 「両方測ってから決める」 for the evidence of the deeper z-arm edges ((a) 今のまま: 奥の辺はスライドの辺として数え、ほぼ証拠なし; (b) 奥の辺は向こうの文の語順の証拠（x と同じ数え方）を持つ), 「両方を候補として残す（同点は一覧）」 for the windows whose two centre growths are incomparable, 「類の中で厳密に安定な並びだけを読む」 for the unit of the strict judgement. The last two are already built (G3-c3 L-622 / L-658 keep both growths and list their answers separately; G3-e L-648 reads only the strictly stable members), so this ticket builds the first only. Three modules are extended (slide.py, slide_place.py, slide_ratios.py) and one test file added; placement.py, ask.py, cli.py, slide_query.py (G3-e, committed b1e42873 while this ticket ran) and test_slide_query.py are untouched. The default (`z_deep` "slide") is byte-identical: the committed G3-c3 run-A records of the first 40 pair windows, the G3-d toy ratios and the slide spec sha are reproduced (tests). Judgement points are numbered L-660.. (L-640..L-658 are G3-e).

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-660 | The switch is the SLIDE spec's: `z_deep` "slide" (default: every z-arm edge is a slide edge, evidence n_z) | "order" (only the innermost z edge is a slide edge; a deeper one carries the pair's word-order count). It is a parameter `deep` of the z axis definition (`default_spec(space, z_deep=...)`, property `SlideSpec.z_deep`, validated by the supported-values table) and exists in the spec document ONLY under "order" (with a sentence of the axis text), so the default spec keeps its bytes and sha256 and "order" has another one; the place spec carries the slide spec sha, so it changes too, and so do the window cache file names (`slidewin_<corpus>_RUN_<slide sha12>_<place sha12>.pkl`). `Slide.counts` stamps the rule on `WindowCounts.z_deep` (new field, default "slide"; the counts' document lists it only under "order"), so that every reader of a window's counts (the placement's weights, the verifier, the ratio reader, slide_query through `counts_evidence`) takes the rule from the one place that was made under the spec; no signature of an existing function changed. Harness consequence: `slide_query.WindowIndex.from_space` builds `SL.Slide(space, rows=rows)` with the default spec, so the S1 script cannot reach the switch; `experiments/line3/g3/c4/measure_c4.py` is a copy of S1's measure_slide.py that replaces `slide.default_spec` for its process (`--z-deep`), the fork workers inherit it, and nothing in slide_query.py / measure_slide.py was edited (open point 4). | slide.default_axes / SlideSpec.z_deep / WindowCounts.z_deep, Z_DEEPS |
| L-661 | What "deeper" means. Positional, from the layout: the INNERMOST z edge is the edge whose inner end is the centre (centre <-> the depth-1 seat of a z arm); every other edge of a z arm (both ends are seats of the arm) is DEEPER. No growth or sentence is looked at (the reservation of growth z_reserved makes both ends of a deeper edge seats of the other sentence, which is the owner's 「向こうの文」; without the reservation a deeper edge may join units of different sentences and then reads the pair's count the same way, open point 2); a padded member (L-623) is read after its padding, the innermost edge being the same edge. The key of an edge is looked up by an ARM CODE: 0..5 the arms as before, `DEEP_Z + 4` / `DEEP_Z + 5` (10, 11) for a deeper +z / -z edge (`_earm(L, deep)`; `ArmWeights.earm(L)` is `_earm(L, self.deep)`); the memo of `ArmWeights` is keyed by the code, so the innermost value is never reused for a deeper edge; with no deep rule the codes are the old ones (checked: the layout tables are equal). Axes are read as `code % 6` (the key split `_edge_vec`, `axis_key_flat`). | slide_place._earm / DEEP_Z / ArmWeights, _edge_vec, axis_key_flat |
| L-662 | The evidence of a deeper z edge under "order": mirror of x. +z (forward: outer before inner): (n_x(o, i), p(o, i)); -z (backward: outer after inner): (n_x(o, i), p(i, o)), read from the x table of the window's counts (`WindowCounts.deep_z(tier, arm, o, i)` = n, omega, the x source rows, before first). So, as for an x arm, (a) the count is of the PAIR over the counts' scope (scope corpus: every sentence of the corpus that holds both units, not only the one sentence of the window: 「x と同じ数え方」; two units of one sentence always have n >= 1, 1144 of 1144 deeper edges in the cache below); (b) the ratio side takes n = n_x (symmetric, as x does; the direction is only in the key's omega); (c) the innermost z edge and all x / y edges are untouched (tested: the weights are equal for every arm code < 6). The key's z entry then SUMS slide counts (innermost) and word-order counts (deeper): the z key of an "order" placement is not comparable with a "slide" placement's, and the per-axis dominance of two growths (L-622) compares keys made under the same rule only. | slide.WindowCounts.deep_z, slide_place.ArmWeights.from_counts |
| L-663 | The record says which rule each edge used. `seats[i].sources.edge` of a z-arm edge gets `rule`: "slide" (the innermost) or "order" (a deeper edge; its `sources` are the x rows, `n` / `omega` the x pair's), and the record gets `z_deep` "order"; both exist ONLY under "order" (the default record has neither, so the committed records are reproduced byte for byte: `NEW_RECORD_FIELDS` is unchanged, `NEW_RECORD_FIELDS_C4 = ("z_deep",)`). The members need no per-edge rule: it is positional. | slide_place._edge_evidence / _seat_rows, SlidePlacement.z_deep |
| L-664 | The independent verifier reads the same rule by its own route: `cross_key` passes the arm name `"+z:deep"` / `"-z:deep"` for a z-arm edge whose inner seat is not `geo.CENTER` when the weighing has the attribute `z_deep` (set by `counts_weight_fn(counts, tier)` from `counts.z_deep`, and by `table_weight_fn(table, deep_table)`), and `counts_weight_fn` answers it through the counts' accessors (`n_x`, `before_x`, `after_x`), not through the search's `ArmWeights` or arm codes (the source-level test of the verifier's names is unchanged and passes). A verifier that does not know the rule reads another z key (tested). | slide_place.cross_key / counts_weight_fn / table_weight_fn |
| L-665 | slide_ratios: one evidence per edge on a z arm. `counts_evidence(counts, tier)` carries `.deep(arm, outer, inner)` when `counts.z_deep == "order"` (n = n_x, rows `("order",) + x row`: the tag keeps these rows from being read as z rows `(sid N, sid N+1, ...)` by slide_query's `_sids_of`); `table_evidence(table, deep_table)` is the hand-table counterpart. `_Reader.edges_of` returns `(outer, inner, arm, n, sources, rule)` and uses `.deep` for a z-arm edge whose inner seat is not the centre; the edge flow F_z, the binding B_z (weights per arm as before: w(+z) 21/377, w(-z) 13/377 multiply the whole edge term), `evidenced_edges` / `seated_edges`, the answer's edge terms, and the section WALK of a z arm (a step over a deeper edge needs n_x(o, i) > 0 and the energy not to drop) all read this one evidence. `EdgeTerm.rule` / `WalkStep.rule` ("slide" default) are listed in their documents only when "order". An evidence function without `.deep` (any 3-argument callable, `label_blind_evidence`, the merged check) reads every z edge the old way. | slide_ratios.counts_evidence / table_evidence / _Reader.edges_of / walk |
| L-666 | Regression gates. (a) The first 40 pair windows of fulllead (RUN, mid, G3-c3 defaults: centre both, budget, strict) under `z_deep` "slide" equal the committed G3-c3 run-A records (experiments/line3/g3/place3, every field, both spec shas; the runner's timing / verifier / statistics fields excluded). Spec: the default spec / counts keep their bytes, "order" changes the spec sha, the place spec sha and lists `deep` / `z_deep` (and nothing else), bad values raise, a place spec made for the other slide spec is refused. Evidence: `deep_z` equals the x table (forward / backward, both scopes, rows included); the arm codes of the layout for L = 1..4 (the old codes unchanged, two z arms x (L - 1) deeper edges); `ArmWeights` with a deep table (the innermost z edge and x read the table, a deeper edge the deep table, a missing key is 0, the memo does not mix them; `score_vec` / `axis_key_flat` of a flat by hand); weights from counts: arm codes < 6 equal under both rules for every ordered pair of the window's units, a deeper edge equals the x pair's (n, p) forward / backward (猫 魚: slide (0, 0), order (2, 2) and (2, 0)). Hand window (犬が走る。/ 猫と魚と鳥が走る。 plus two sentences that put 猫 魚 鳥 in an order): under "order" every deeper z-arm edge of the record says rule "order" and equals `deep_z`, two units only in N+1 have n >= 1; under "slide" the same edges have no `rule` and n = n_z = 0; the innermost edge says "slide" and equals n_z; x / y edges carry no rule (centre_scope n and both). Verifier: 4 toys x 3 growth / centre combinations x all windows: every member legal and stable under the verifier that reads the rule, the record's strict counts and per-axis key equal the verifier's; a verifier without the rule reads another z key; a hand table with a deep table (a z arm ordered by the deep evidence, the class verifier-stable). Ratios: F_z / B_z of a real order placement equal an independent route over `geometry.neighbours` (innermost: slide count, deeper: the pair's), differ from the slide reading of the same cross only on z (x and y axis documents equal), `evidenced_edges` larger, every walk step ends at the centre iff its rule is "slide", `check_trace` holds; a hand cross: the +z walk stops "unproven" at the deeper edge without a deep table and steps over it with one (step rule "order", rows `table_deep`), the binding difference is exactly w(+z) x n / n(u) at both ends, `two_if_single_edge` counts the deeper edge as evidenced (1 -> 2: the section walk applies); a plain 3-argument evidence still works. Regression: L-666 (a), (b). PYTHONHASHSEED 0 / 1 / 12345 give identical bytes over both rules x centre_scope x growth x seat_key x all windows x both agreement rules; no float in the spec, counts, records and ratio documents.
