Reach (window level, the gold a seated unit of a window that holds a question unit): 40 / 69 under slide, 41 / 69 under order (S1 on G3-c2: 40). The windows that seat the gold (gold_diag.py: 73 windows of 40 questions under slide, 78 windows of 41 under order; every member of the class read as the question path reads it): members read 16966 / 7528; the number of member-axes that agree on a unit: x 19 / 12 (none the gold), z 72 / 7 under `three` (gold 0 / 1; the one is not admitted); under `two_if_single_edge` slide z answers 2144 member-axes of which 116 the gold, in 2 windows of 2 questions (admitted, but those windows are not among the 4 read at fast, so an unbounded read would, by inference from these counts (no full run was made here), put the gold in a candidate in 2 / 69 questions under slide and in 0 under order); order z: points_nowhere 1527 / section_disagreement 3520 / ratio_disagreement 672 / no_edges 1802 of 7528 (slide: 7068 / 6816 / 1210 / 1800 of 16966, `three`).

### G3-c4 open points (owner; meaning level)
(1) Neither rule moves the gold into a candidate: gold in a candidate 0 / 69 in all four configurations at fast (S1 on G3-c2: 0 / 69; reach 40 / 41 of 69), so the loss is still the agreement step, not the evidence of the deeper edges. In the 73 (slide) / 78 (order) windows that seat the gold, only 91 / 19 of 16966 / 7528 member-axes agree on any unit under `three` (x: 19 / 12, none the gold). What changed is the mix: on the z axis points_nowhere 42 % -> 20 %, section_disagreement 40 % -> 47 %, ratio_disagreement 7 % -> 9 %; on x nothing but fewer members (the classes are smaller). Which part of the agreement is to be examined next: the section walk (every working section must point to one unit; the sections attach to question units, I-07), the question units that the gold windows hold (V1), or the x axis of those windows? This ticket does not touch any of them.
(2) What "the other sentence's word order" is counted over. Built: the pair's x count over the counts' scope, exactly as an x arm reads it (scope corpus: every sentence that holds both units; two units of one sentence therefore always have n >= 1, so the deeper z edges carry evidence in 1144 of 1144 cases and the information is only the size of n and the direction p(o, i)). The other reading, the order inside THIS window's other sentence (n = 1, direction known from the sentence), would make every deeper edge of the same weight and direction-only; it is a different count (scope window of x restricted to one sid) and is not built (L-667). Also the rule is positional: without growth z_reserved a deeper edge can join a unit of N and a unit of N+1; it then reads the pair's corpus order count, which is not "the other sentence's" (all measurements here use z_reserved).
(3) The growths are compared by a key whose z entry now mixes counts (L-662): the representative's centre in N+1 falls 74 -> 17 of 292, windows that keep both growths rise 106 -> 249 (the cause is not analysed); units seated rise 67 % -> 79 %, class size median 426 -> 101, budget stops 186 -> 115. The G3-c3 open point 1 (compare growths on equal footing) is therefore more pressing under "order", not less.
(4) `two_if_single_edge` no longer differs from `three` under "order" (identical entries and verdicts on all 94 questions): with a deeper edge the z axis has two or more evidenced edges, so the single-edge rule (G3-d 「辺が 1 本の軸」) did not change a single entry or verdict on the 94 questions at fast (a window whose z arms have L = 1 has no deeper edge and could still have exactly one evidenced z edge); under "slide" it is what produced the 111 z entries (and the 9 confident wrong answers on unans). So the owner's choice of evidence rule for the deeper z edges also decides whether z answers at all: order 5 entries over 94 questions, slide `three` 6, slide `two_if_single_edge` 113.
(5) Not comparable with S1: S1 ran on G3-c2 placements; the `c3 placements, z_deep slide` rows are the first S1 numbers on G3-c3 placements (G3-e open point 7): gold 0 / 69 as before, single wrong 2 -> 6 (three) and 16 -> 14 (two_if), unans abstained 25 / 25 and 10 / 25.
(6) Citations: the tag of L-665 keeps the order rows out of `slide_query._sids_of` (it reads z rows by their integer sids), so an order-rule z answer cites its seats' sentences and the innermost edge's (N, N+1) only; the sentences whose word order is the evidence of every deeper edge are in the answer's `sources` rows but not in `source_sids`. If the answer must cite them, `_sids_of` needs a case for the "order" tag (G3-e's file). (7) `z_deep` is not an argument of `WindowIndex.from_space` / `ask_slide` / `ask.py` / `cli.py` (G3-e's files): the S1 script reaches it through `slide.default_spec`. The switch should go through `WindowIndex.from_space(..., slide_kw=...)` (or `z_deep=`), recorded in the cache header and in the answer's `spec` shas (already different).

## G3-e2 (2026-10-09): the answer shape "unit" | "path", the read order "qcount_first" | "grammar_first", and `z_deep` through the window index (verantyx/line3/slide_query.py; ask.py untouched, cli.py options; tests/line3/test_slide_query.py; experiments/line3/g3/s1/: measure_e2.py, sweep_e2.sh, summarize_e2.py, path_ceiling.py, results/summary_e2.md)
Owner (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md, 「G3-e（S1）の監査後の決定」, verbatim): the answer shape per axis (the end unit of the walk, or all the words of the walked path): 「両方測ってから決める」 ((a) the end unit, I-11; (b) all the words of the walked path as a labelled candidate, both in S1); the read order and the fast cap: 「問いの語を多く持つ窓を先に、文法層はその中での順に (Recommended)」. Plus the plumbing the G3-c4 ticket left to this file (G3-c4 open point 7): `z_deep` as an argument of the window index, `ask_slide`, `ask(structure="slide")` and `line3 ask/build --structure slide`. Judgement points are numbered L-680.. (L-660..L-667 are G3-c4). Only slide_query.py, cli.py and the tests are edited; slide.py / slide_place.py / slide_ratios.py (the G3-c4 working tree, under review) are read; ask.py needs no edit (`**kw` already reaches `ask_slide`).

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-680 | The answer shape is a read-time switch `answer_shape` "unit" (default, I-11: `words` = [the end unit of the section walk], what S1 did) | "path" (`words` = the units of the walked section path on that axis, L-681). THE AGREEMENT IS UNCHANGED: whether an axis answers, which unit it agrees on, the admission rules (window gate L-646, grounded L-647, strict stability L-648), the members, the entries' identity (window, axis, agreed unit) and the abstentions are the same under both shapes (tested: same entries, arrangements, centres, ratios, abstentions, verdict). The shape decides only what the candidate shows. The config, the thought and the answer's `read` record the shape. | read_window, ask_slide |
| L-681 | THE PATH. For a member that agrees on unit u on axis a: the section walks of `slide_ratios.AxisRatios.sections` whose section unit is u and whose terminus is u (exactly the walks `AxisAnswer.walk_steps` is taken from; each distinct (arm, path) once), `AxisWalk.path` read outer end first, ending at the centre or at the break. `words` = the units of those walks, walk after walk (section order, then arm order), each unit once at its first occurrence. With two arms of the axis ending on the same unit the union reads arm 1 (outer to u), then the other arm's units that arm 1 did not hold, the centre being where arm 1 reached it (toy: 猫 魚 食 for 魚 on x). PROVENANCE PER WORD (`word_provenance`, aligned with `words`): `basis` "walk", the arms it was walked on, its seats on the walks (unit, sid, arm, position, occurrence rows: the cross's own, as the entry's `seats`), the walk steps that arrive at it (arm, from, to, n, source rows, rule) and the sentences of both (`source_sids`). The walks used are in the entry (`walks`: arm, stop, path as (unit, sid) pairs). NO WALK (agreement `two_if_single_edge` on an axis with one evidenced edge: `section` None, the walk was skipped): the path is the unit alone, `basis` "seat", its seats; so on the one-edge z axis (164 of the 166 S1 entries) the path shape shows the same one word as the unit shape (open point 2). | path_read, PathRead |
| L-682 | Which path an entry shows, and what it cites. An entry stays ONE per (window, axis, agreed unit) in both shapes (the members that agree on the unit are one entry, `arrangements` = their number, L-649). Members of one unit may have walked different paths to it (toy: yes, e.g. 魚 by 海 魚 and by 魚 alone); the entry shows the path of its FIRST agreeing member (as it already shows that member's ratios, seats and sources, L-649), nothing is chosen or merged, and `path_variants` lists every distinct path the agreeing members walked with the number of members ({"words", "arrangements"}, in order of first member; the shown path is the first). Under "path" the entry's `source_sids` is the S1 set plus the sentences of the shown words' provenance (the candidate shows the path words, so it cites their sentences); under "unit" it is the S1 set. | read_window |
| L-683 | Entry keys added to EVERY entry (both shapes, so that the shapes differ only in `words` and `source_sids`): `unit` (the agreed end unit), `word_provenance` (under "unit": the one entry for the unit, taken from the same walks), `path_variants`, `walks`. A path of one unit is therefore the unit entry byte for byte (tested on the toy: whole entry, canonical bytes). `SlideAnswer.entry_keys()` keys on the unit and the shown words. The tally's answer label is the unit. | read_window, SlideAnswer.entry_keys |
| L-684 | The read order is a switch `read_order` "qcount_first" (DEFAULT, the owner's 「問いの語を多く持つ窓を先に、文法層はその中での順に」) | "grammar_first" (L-645's order, kept for comparison). qcount_first: the candidate windows are sorted by the number of question units they hold (more first; then stand-ins when switched on) and only INSIDE a tie of that count by the grammar order (`grammar.read_order`: the slot's kind first, then the ladder weight; equal kinds one group). The BLOCKS are the same sets under both orders (equal count and equal kind group = one block, read together or not at all) and only their sequence differs: qcount_first is the stable sort of the grammar_first blocks by key, descending. `Plan.order` / `read.order` say which ("qcount_first" | "grammar_first" | "shuffle:N"); before this ticket the label was "grammar". Tested against an independent construction of both block lists (toy of 9 sentences and a second toy of 8 sentences in which the two orders differ: 猫の魚は何ですか etc.) for every slot particle. | plan_windows |
| L-685 | The cap is unchanged: whole blocks in order, the first block that does not fit stops the read, a tied block is never split (fast 4 / standard 10 / full unbounded windows, `nodes N`). Consequences: the windows read under a cap are those holding the most question units (tested: at every cap the minimum held among the windows read is at least the maximum held among the unread); `within="none"` (the grammar groups only) is a variant of the old order and is REFUSED with qcount_first (`ValueError`; it has no count to sort by), so the tests and the S1 variant that used it name `read_order="grammar_first"`. | plan_windows |

The cache header gets a `z_deep` key (readability); `_check_header` refuses a mismatching value WHEN the key is present (the G3-c4 caches have none and load: the slide spec sha already refuses the other rule; tested by renaming a slide file to the order name, and by a header that lies about z_deep). The flat path (`--structure flat`, the default) does not read the new flags and keeps its bytes (tested: the same stdout with and without the flags; goldens below). | cli.cmd_line3 |

### G3-e2 tests (tests/line3/test_slide_query.py: 67 passed, 14 s, at PYTHONHASHSEED 0 / 1 / 12345; 44 committed, 45 once the order-independence test became three-way, 22 added; toy of 9 sentences in 4 articles + a second toy of 8 sentences in 4 articles for the read order, level low)
Existing tests that assert the grammar-first block structure pin `read_order="grammar_first"` (the old order reachable): the slot-kind-first test, the cap prefix test, the tied-block test, the preset test is unchanged (same order on both sides), the order-independence test runs for (qcount_first, qcount), (grammar_first, qcount), (grammar_first, none). New: the names and refusals (bad shape, order, z_deep; within none with qcount_first); PATH SHAPE: same (window, axis, unit) entries, arrangements, centres, ratios, walks, abstentions and verdict as "unit" under both agreement rules and both member modes; words = the units of the walks in order of first occurrence, each unit once, the agreed unit among them, every walk ends on the unit, positions on the arm increase outer to inner, a walk that stops "centre" ends on the centre seat, every step of every path is a recorded step in the provenance of the word it enters (n > 0, source rows present), provenance aligned with the words, seats of the word only, `source_sids` a superset of the provenance sentences, trace ok; a path of one unit equals the unit entry in canonical bytes (and a longer path differs); `path_variants` add up to `arrangements`, are distinct, the shown path is the first; no section walk (`section` None) = the unit alone at its seats, `basis` "seat", no walks; exact skip (skip none = exact) and order independence (3 shuffles) under "path"; entry keys. READ ORDER: both block lists equal an independent construction for every toy question and every slot particle (key non-increasing across the whole list, grammar order only inside a tie, the same windows as grammar_first), the two orders differ on the second toy and ties of the count occur, the cap reads whole blocks and never splits a tie at every cap, the windows read hold at least as many question units as the unread (qcount_first), fast reads the two-unit windows of 猫の魚は何ですか first and grammar_first does not, `within="none"` is refused, entries are equal under both orders when everything is read. Z_DEEP: the window index under "order" has another slide spec sha and place spec sha and the placements carry the rule; the default spelled out equals the default; two cache files side by side with distinct names, each rule loads its own (`build=False`), a directory with only the other rule's file raises `FileNotFoundError`, a slide file renamed to the order name is refused by its header, a header that says the other `z_deep` is refused; `ask_slide` records `z_deep` in config and shas, None = the index's, an explicit different value raises; the new switches on an order index; `ask(structure="slide", ...)` through an `ask.Index` keeps one window index per rule and re-places nothing (two files); the flat `ask` does not take the switches. CLI: `build --z-deep order` writes a second file with another slide spec sha; `ask` with the default flags equals the same with every default spelled out; `--answer-shape path --read-order grammar_first --z-deep order` is identical under PYTHONHASHSEED 0 / 1 / 12345 and its thought says so; a bad value is rejected; the flat bytes are unchanged by the flags. Bytes: the hash-seed test now covers both z_deep rules x both agreement rules x both shapes x both orders (a SHA-256 of all the answers' bytes, identical under the three seeds); the AST test finds no float and no division in the module. The goldens (tests/line3/test_ask.py + test_ask_ordered.py + test_matryoshka.py): 113 passed, run once (706 s) with this ticket's cli.py in the tree.

The earlier S1 rows are in summary_e2.md as `ref` rows (G3-c2 placements: gold 0 / 0, G3-c3 placements with the old read order and the unit shape: 0 / 0 / 0 / 0). Read order alone (window level, intra2, the gold a seated unit of a window actually READ at fast): qcount_first 37 / 69 (z slide) and 38 / 69 (z order), grammar_first 27 / 69 and 27 / 69 (no cap: 40 and 41); questions that read nothing (first block larger than 4): 2 vs 8 (slide), 3 vs 10 (order); questions that read 4 windows: 28 vs 31. What the path could reach if the agreement did not decide (`path_ceiling.py`, the windows read at fast, every readable member, agreement three's walks; 69 questions): gold seated in a read window 37 / 38 (slide / order); the gold is the END unit of some section walk 18 / 20; a word of the PATH of some walk 31 / 34; in an agreed entry 1 / 1.

What the numbers say (the reading is the owner's): (1) the gold in a candidate stays 0-2 of 69 in all nine runs (path: 1 / 2 / 1 / 1; unit: 0 / 1 / 0 / 0; in four of the five path hits the gold is a path word and not the end unit). (2) The new read order moves the reach (27 -> 37 / 38 of 69 golds in a window read at fast; the questions that read nothing fall 8 -> 2 and 10 -> 3), not the candidates: the agreement still decides. (3) The walks do pass the gold: 31 / 34 of 69 questions have the gold on the path of some section walk of the windows read, 18 / 20 as the end unit; the agreement lets 1 of them answer. (Review, every gated readable member of the gold windows, agreement three: on z_deep slide 12 of the 18 "end unit" questions have the gold only as the end of a walk that took NO step (the walk stopped at its outer seat, i.e. the gold is merely the outer seat of some member's arrangement), 6 as the end of a walk of at least one step; the gold is a word of a walk of at least two units in 26 of the 31; no (member, axis) picks the gold with all three ratios (two of three in 3 questions); the 1 that answers holds the gold as a path word, not as the agreed unit. On z_deep order: 18 of 20 with at least one step, 34 of 34 in walks of at least two units, one member agrees on the gold (I2-013 県社, z) and is dropped as not grounded, L-647.) (4) The shapes do not change the number of entries (the agreement decides), only the words: 4 -> 12 (slide three), 88 -> 96 (slide two_if: only 4 entries have a walk of more than one unit; the z axis of one edge has none), 6 -> 17 (order). (5) The unans questions: `three` abstains 25 / 25 in every run; `two_if_single_edge` on z_deep slide still gives 8 single confident answers (7 on the G3-c2 placements, 9 with the old read order) and 0 on z_deep order; neither the shape nor the order changes that. (6) Single wrong answers on the answerable questions: unit 4 / 12 / 4 / 4 against path 3 / 11 / 4 / 4.

### G3-e2 open points (owner; meaning level)
1. The shape does not reach the golds because the agreement decides which unit answers, and a unit-level agreement (R1 = R2 = R3 on the END unit) is the wrong gate for a path: the gold is on the path of a walk in 31 / 34 of 69 questions and agreed in 1. Is the next step a path-valued agreement (a walk is a candidate when it is a working section's walk, its edge flow or binding as an order only; or every working walk of an axis whose terminus unit the three ratios do not contradict), i.e. a different rule for what answers, not a different display?
2. Under `two_if_single_edge` the one-edge z axis (the bulk of the entries) has no section walk, so the path is the one unit (L-681). Should its path be the edge (both ends, outer to inner), as the flat readout shows a seat's neighbour, or the end unit only?
3. The entry shows the path of its first agreeing member and lists the others in `path_variants` (L-682). Alternatives: the union of the agreeing members' paths (one candidate, longer), or one entry per distinct path (more entries: tried first in this ticket, 26 entries from 4 unit entries on slide three, 12 per list; dropped because it multiplied the lists).
4. Two arms of an axis that end on the same unit give a union in which the centre sits between the arms (猫 魚 食, L-681). Is that the intended "outer to centre" order, or should the arms be listed apart (the `walks` field already has them)?
5. The new read order still leaves 28 of the 69 questions reading the full 4 windows and 44 partial; 2-3 questions read nothing because the first (most question units) block is larger than 4. Splitting that block, reading it anyway, or a larger fast cap are the earlier open point 5 of G3-e, unchanged.
6. `two_if_single_edge` on z_deep slide: 8 of 25 unanswerable questions get a single confident answer under any shape and order; this is the agreement rule, not the shape (G3-e open point 2).
7. Citations: the order-rule rows of z_deep "order" are not in `source_sids` (G3-c4 open point 6, L-688); under "path" the provenance sentences are.
8. The measurement is on the G3-c3 placements of the working tree (the G3-c4 slide.py / slide_place.py / slide_ratios.py were under review while this ran): if the reviewer changes the placements the cache shas change and the sweep must be rebuilt (`sweep_e2.sh` builds a missing cache; the numbers above are on caches with the shas named). Review: G3-c4 is now committed (0db1f585) and the `code_sha256` of all nine e2 runs' .meta.json equals that tree's slide.py / slide_place.py / slide_ratios.py / ask.py / grammar.py, and slide_query.py is this ticket's file as reviewed (the review changed one comment after the runs), so the numbers are on the committed placements.

## G3-f (2026-10-09): the window placements read as FLAT crosses (verantyx/line3/slide_flat.py; slide_query.py / cli.py / ask.py untouched, no hook yet; tests/line3/test_slide_flat.py; experiments/line3/g3/s1flat/: measure_flat.py, sweep_flat.sh, summarize_flat.py, edge_evidence.py, trace_share.py, subset_ids.py, equiv_check.py, results/summary_flat.md)
Owner (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md, 「S1（G3-e2）の測定後の決定」, verbatim): 「窓を「平らな十字」として読む（軸ごとではなく十字全体で一致を判定） (Recommended)」 -- 「窓の配置は残し、読みは T10 と同じく十字全体で 3 比率を判定し、経路の語を候補にする。軸ごとの答えはラベルとして付ける」. Binding from before: decision 7 and I-11 (the three ratios agree on ONE unit), I-12 (moves), I-14 / N-20 (a list is never shortened, no winner by order), I-15 (stability), I-16 (a tier on its own), M-2 / N-18 (the user's amount of inference, here in windows), the evaluation principle (candidates for users to grade, typed abstention), P-4 (every output word traces), OP-G3-1/2 (the window), G3-c3 (read only the strictly stable members; both centre growths). Judgement points are numbered L-700.. (L-680..L-688 are G3-e2). The reading is the owner's; this is the build and the measurement.

What it is. The window index, the question intake, the read order, the cap, the member choice and the member cross are slide_query's (imported, not copied; committed in fd75b222, G3-e2: every run's .meta.json records its sha, 3b17519d...). Each strictly stable member of a window's class becomes a START (a flat tuple of units, centre + 6 arms, cycle.py's world-view state); the starts of one window are read as the members of one cross by `cycle._ask_tier_once(..., plan_override=...)` with a duck-typed carrier (`FlatCross`: seed, L, members, twin_sets): every start is settled by the T5 moves (23 rotations + seat swaps, key I-12), the answering end states are pooled and adopted by the V3 rule, `readout.read_out_result` reads the path words into T7b entries (merged sections, entries = word sets), `trace_check.trace_answer` traces every word. cycle.py, readout.py, trace_check.py, energy.py, geometry.py, slide*.py, grammar.py are not edited. The per-axis answers of slide_ratios on the placed members are attached to each entry as labels.

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-700 | The reading is the unchanged flat chain over a window: `cycle._ask_tier_once(tier, question, store, units=intake.units, facts=..., plan_override=ReadPlan(...), budget, observe=False)` -> `readout.read_out_result` -> `trace_check.trace_answer`. One call per window; the window's starts (all arm lengths) are its crosses, so the V3 adoption (the end states of all answering starts, the ones sharing the most sentences with the question) pools both centre growths of a window and never pools across windows (a window is a cross, and crosses of one tier are read separately, I-16; the entries of different windows are never merged, as slide_query). `member_rule` stable_any, `state_rule` query_share, `scope` first_layer, function / question words dropped (V2): cycle's own defaults, nothing overridden. `observe=False`: the energy log of the adopted states (design L-20) is not produced (it is a thought-object item the entries do not use). | slide_flat.read_window_flat |
| L-701 | THE LOCAL SPACE is the corpus RUN tier (`flat_facts` = cycle.TierFacts of `space.tiers["RUN"]`), evidence "plain" (default). The slide's corpus-scope count of a pair of seated units IS the tier's same-sentence count (`counts.n_x(o, i) == tier.n_pair(o, i)`) and its `before_x` IS `p_pair` (slide.verify_counts; tested on every pair of every toy window), n(u) and N are the corpus postings, E_Q is cycle.Reader.enq: so the E_Q, n and p of the placement are exactly the ones the flat reader reads. What the flat reader does NOT read is the slide (z) and grain (y) counts: they are properties of an ARM LABEL, and the reader has no labels (the arm a unit sits on is a label that moves with a swap). Consequence, measured by edge_evidence.py (the 241 representative crosses of the windows S1 fast reads, 94 questions, z_deep slide): every one of the 1762 x-arm edges is evidenced for both readers (the same count); of the 577 edges on z arms 387 are evidenced for the flat reader (n_pair > 0) and 192 for the per-axis reader (n_z > 0), 114 of them for the per-axis reader only; of the 240 z-arm edges AT THE CENTRE (the first seat of the other sentence) only 50 are evidenced for the flat reader. The flat reader walks the other sentence's arm into the centre far less often than the slide's counts would let it: that is what evidence "window" (L-714) puts back. | flat_facts |
| L-702 | THE STARTS. Members read: `slide_query.choose_members` (strict "abstain": ONLY the strictly stable members, `readable`, G3-c3 L-648: 135332 of the 138707 members of fulllead z slide; the rest are counted `unstable_axis_improvable`; `members` "all" | "representative" = the first read member of each growth). A member is read at the arm length it was grown at: the pad of `member_L` (empty OUTER seats per arm) is cut, as slide_query.member_cross does (cycle swaps units into empty outer seats; reading the padded member would change the search). Members whose flats are equal after the legs are put in canonical order (`placement.canon`; the leg-to-arm assignment of a flat is a label for the flat reader, L-61 / L-111) are ONE start that remembers all of them (`origin_members`); toy: members a and a2 that differ by a permutation of the legs are one start. With `members` "representative" an entry says what the representative stands for: `growth_members` = the members each growth (centre in N / in N+1) has among those that may be read ({"next": 4, "this": 1} on the toy window 4), next to `members_read`, `class_size` and `starts`. Starts of one arm length form one FlatCross `W<n>/L<L>`, in canonical order of their flats (a label). fulllead (z slide): 103183 starts over the 592 windows (median 8, max 2079) vs 138707 members; representative: 696. | starts_of, member_flat, FlatCross |
| L-703 | TWO SEATS of a unit in both sentences. The record seats it twice (token `unit SEP sid`, G3-c2 seat_key unit_sid); cycle's cross has one cell per seat but a state may hold a string twice (energy.py L-56: F and B sum over the cells of a unit, the pair of two cells of one unit adds nothing; Reader.Base.unique False takes the full evaluation, slower). `two_seat` "both" (DEFAULT): both seats stay, as the same string on two cells. Consequences, tested: a section walk steps from one cell of the unit to the other (n(u,u) = n(u) > 0, the energy does not drop), so a PATH can list the unit twice (toy 魚 魚); the entry's word set lists it once; the trace holds; the z self-edge n_z(u, u) that slide_ratios counts since G3-d (`z_self_edges`) is NOT counted by the flat reader (L-56), a difference of the reading (open point 6). "n_only": one seat per unit: the unit at the centre keeps the centre seat, any other shared unit keeps its seat of the window's first sentence N and loses the other (the centre is never emptied); measured as a variant. 97 of the 592 windows hold a shared unit. | member_flat |
| L-704 | V1 at the level of the start: a start whose seats hold no question unit is not read (counted `no_question_unit`); the windows read are slide_query.plan_windows' (windows that hold a question unit, `hold` seats, read order `qcount_first` | `grammar_first`, the cap in windows: fast 4 / standard 10 / full unbounded / `nodes`). N-03 grounding is cycle's (L-104): a section counts as grounded when its attached question unit is in the space (n >= 1), not when it is in the cross; an agreed state without a grounded section is `ungrounded`. slide_query's separate window-level gate and `NOT_GROUNDED` admission are not repeated. | read_window_flat |
| L-705 | The search budget is cycle's own `QueryBudget()` (512 states, 64 fixed points per start), configurable (`budget`). It is NOT T7b's entrance budget (64 / 8, L-210), and so the flat-cross rows of T10 and the window-cross rows here are NOT budget-matched (open point 2). A window cross has up to 43 seats (L = 7), a flat cross a few, so the climb is longer: under 64 / 8 every start of the first probe window (W1 of fulllead, 78 starts) ran out of its 64 states, and the whole sweep (representative, fast, z slide) reads 0 of 69 golds with 60 questions without a candidate (verdicts of the 69 answerable questions: UNKNOWN_NO_FIXED_POINT 51); under 512 / 64 and under 4096 / 512 the rows are in the measurement below. The budget is not a quality dial the code can set: it decides which starts are NO_FIXED_POINT (a typed abstention, never a silent one). | DEFAULT_BUDGET |
| L-706 | Entries are the read-out's: `AnswerEntry` = a word set (the path words of the adopted states + the centre, sorted by code point), `arrangements`, `centres` (reference), `stability` ("n/d"), `source_sids` (the sentences that hold the two units of an edge of a path, L-160), one entry per distinct word set of the window, stable_any member rule, all stability ties listed. `words_per_entry` is therefore larger than the unit shape's 1 and the per-axis path shape's 3. | read_window_flat |
| L-707 | Entry shape = T7b's (`tier`, `words`, `arrangements`, `centres`, `stability`, `source_sids`) + `structure` "slide_flat", `label` "slide:flat", `window` {n, title, sids, idx, constructed}, `centre_sentence` ("this" = the centre in N, "next" = in N+1, "both" when the members behind it have both) and `centre_sentences` (member counts), `stable_strict` (every member behind the entry is strictly stable: always true under "abstain"), `origin_members`, `members_read`, `class_size`, `starts`, `members_not_read` (unstable / no question unit / `member_cap`: the starts cycle's `member_cap` left unsettled, review), `evidence`, `growth_members`, `axis_labels` (L-708) and `trace` {ok, words_checked, words_traced, edge_words, constructed_edge_words}. | read_window_flat |
| L-708 | LABELS. For each entry, the per-axis answers of slide_ratios (`read_axes` as slide_query reads it: foundation weights, `z_self_edges` on, agreement "three" by default, `label_agreement` to change) on the PLACED members behind it (`origin_members`, the placement as seated on its arms), as {axis: {agreed: members whose axis agrees, of: members, units: {agreed unit: members}, abstained: {kind: members}}}. They are computed on the START member and not on the end state: a rotation or swap of the flat cycle takes units off the arm their axis label sits on, so a label of an end state would be a label of a different cross (open point 4). They are attached after the entries are fixed and read by nothing: `labels=False` and a replaced label function give the same verdict, entries and bytes except the label field (tested). | _axis_labels |
| L-709 | Typed abstentions and the verdict. A window with no entry has one abstention {window, kind, kinds, members, starts}: the member outcomes of the flat cycle (L-106: `ratio_disagreement`, `section_disagreement`, `points_nowhere`, `ungrounded` -> `not_grounded`, `mixed`, `ambiguous`, `no_fixed_point`) plus `unstable_axis_improvable`, `no_question_unit`, `no_path`; the member tally of every window read is in the thought. The verdict: one entry = ANSWER, several = CHOICE (as ask.combine / slide_query.verdict_of); none: UNKNOWN_NO_WINDOW (no window holds a question unit), UNKNOWN_NOT_READ (the cap read none), else the kinds met, in cycle._verdict_from's reporting precedence (not a winner choice): AMBIGUOUS, UNKNOWN_NO_FIXED_POINT, UNKNOWN_RATIO_DISAGREEMENT (also for cycle's member status `mixed`, as _verdict_from reads "none:mixed"; review fix: before it ranked below section disagreement / unstable or fell through to UNKNOWN_NO_EVIDENCE: 13 verdict strings of no-entry questions over 5 rows, no entry, abstention or tally changed), UNKNOWN_SECTION_DISAGREEMENT, then slide_query's UNKNOWN_UNSTABLE_AXIS_IMPROVABLE, UNKNOWN_NO_PATH, UNKNOWN_NO_EVIDENCE. slide_query.verdict_of is not reused because it has no AMBIGUOUS / NO_FIXED_POINT. | verdict_of |
| L-710 | P-4. `trace_check.trace_answer` on the window's PathAnswer: every word of every path of every item: the seat holds the word in the state, the word is a unit of the tier with a sentence, and (unless it opens a leg) its leg predecessor is the neighbouring seat and a sentence holds both; the entry carries the counts, the window read `trace_ok`. Tested: all toy windows trace; a state altered at a seat the path names fails the check (the check bites). | read_window_flat |
| L-711 | Bytes. `FlatAnswer.to_bytes()` = slide.canonical of {answer, thought} (sorted keys, Fractions as "n/d", an inexact number is refused); wall time is not in the bytes; the module has no floating-point number and no division operator (tested by AST). PYTHONHASHSEED 0 / 1 / 12345 give the same bytes over the toy questions in five configurations (subprocess test). | FlatAnswer |
| L-712 | Nothing is hooked: `ask_flat(index, question, ...)` takes an ask.Index or a slide_query.WindowIndex (built / loaded as ask_slide does, `z_deep` checked), `ask.py` / `cli.py` / `slide_query.py` are not edited (the follow-up adds `structure="slide_flat"` and the options). slide_query's answer bytes are unchanged by a flat read (tested). | ask_flat |
| L-713 | `evidence` is a switch: "plain" (DEFAULT, L-701) | "window" (L-714). | ask_flat |
| L-714 | evidence "window" (a MEASURED VARIANT, not the owner's decision, which was "read the window as T10 reads a cross" = L-701): `WindowTier` = the corpus tier with ONE change, `n_pair(u, v)` of two units of the window's pack = n_x + n_y(both ways) + n_z(both ways) of the window's corpus-scope counts (a label-blind sum; a pair outside the pack and n(u) are the corpus tier's; postings, sentence_units, N and n are the corpus's, `WindowFacts` shares n, D, dq). The flat reader reads ONE pair function, so E_Q (n_pair of a question unit and a unit) then carries the slide terms of the pairs inside the pack as well: the formula of E_Q is unchanged (design 8: 「E_Q の式を変えること」 is out of scope) but its pair input is no longer the corpus count, and the per-axis reader's E_Q is the corpus one. P-4 is checked on a WEAKER tier: `trace_tier` = the corpus tier plus one appended "pair sentence" per adjacent pair of the corpus (units of N then of N+1, sid >= N), so that an edge the slide evidences has "a sentence that holds both" (trace_check's rule (c)); real sentences sort first, so a word traces by a real sentence whenever one exists, and the entry's `trace.constructed_edge_words` counts the path words whose edge ONLY a constructed sentence evidences (0 under "plain"). The entry's `source_sids` also cite the sentence pairs of the z rows of the path's edges. | WindowTier, window_evidence, trace_tier |
| L-715 | The measurement covers `members` all only on the questions it can afford: it settles one start per strictly stable member of every window read, 65128 starts for the 94 questions at fast (z slide), 0.4 s each. The questions whose windows read at fast hold at most 700 starts together (57 questions, 17808 starts, 27 % of the work; ids in results/all_subset_ids.txt by subset_ids.py) are run with `members` all, evidence plain and window; the other 37 (47320 starts) are NOT run. `representative` (the first member of each growth: 696 starts for the 592 windows) carries every other row. The 700 is a cost cut, not a property of the questions; a first attempt on all 94 questions (6 finished in 14 minutes, the heaviest window of one question 641 s) was stopped (results_pre/flat_fast_zslide_all_partial6.jsonl). | sweep_flat.sh, subset_ids.py |

`FLAT rep` rows: `members` representative, search budget 512 / 64, evidence plain, both seats of a shared unit (the plain reading of the decision, L-701); `budget`, `one seat`, `evidence window` rows change that one thing. `S1 per-axis` = the G3-e2 runs (answer shape path, agreement three / two_if_single_edge). T10 / B1 / B2 rows are the committed runs on the same 94 questions (T10 ran on a loaded machine; its seconds are not a speed measurement).

Time. Median seconds per question (max): flat rep 1.3 (11.8) at fast and 3.2 (19.1) at standard on z slide, 3.5 (17.9) / 9.5 (31.3) on z order; T10 flat 166 / 486; the per-axis reader 0.6. Budget: 64 / 8 0.6 s, 4096 / 512 2.9 s (max 49 s). The time is the starts: members all settles 17808 starts for the 57 questions of the subset below (0.4 s a start), 65128 for the 94 questions at fast (z slide), 114275 at standard.

What the numbers say (the reading is the owner's). (1) The whole-cross agreement gives candidates where the per-axis agreement gave none: gold in a candidate 9 of 69 at fast and 10 at standard (z slide; 7 and 8 on z order) against 1-2 per-axis; 25 of the 69 answerable questions have an entry at fast (8 ANSWER, 17 CHOICE) against 4 per-axis ANSWERs. It stays below T10's flat read of the same tier (RUN only: 15 fast, 18 standard) and far below B1 / B2 (47 / 57), with shorter lists (entries median 2 / 3 at fast / standard against 17 / 28; words in the list 12 / 16 against 86 / 182) and over a hundred times faster. (2) Where the other 44 end (fast, z slide, every window read; summed over the starts of the 310 settled): 109 starts NO_FIXED_POINT (the budget), 56 ratio disagreement, 33 ungrounded, 23 ambiguous (tied branches end in different results), 17 section disagreement, 12 points nowhere, 10 mixed, against 50 that answer (the 2860 members of those windows that are not strictly stable are not read). The budget is the largest single loss and not the whole of it: at 64 / 8 nothing is read (0 of 69; 244 of 310 starts unsettled), at 4096 / 512 the unsettled starts fall from 109 to 28 and AMBIGUOUS rises from 23 to 56, but the golds stay 9 (the answers move from "no fixed point" to "ambiguous", not to more golds). (3) The gold is in the entry as a path word or as the centre: 14 entries hold it in 9 questions (fast, z slide). THE LABELS AGREE ON NONE OF THEM: of the 69 entries of that run no axis agrees on any placed member (x 0, y 0, z 0), and over the eleven FLAT rep rows 14 of 1796 entries have an axis that agrees on some member: the flat cross agrees where its placement's axes do not, which is the fact behind the owner's decision. (4) The structural reason it stays below T10: of the 577 edges on z arms in the crosses read, 387 are evidenced for the flat reader against 192 for the per-axis reader (L-701), but at the centre only 50 of 240; the flat reader cannot read the other sentence's arm into the centre where the slide would. (5) The unans principle: flat rep at fast has 18 of 25 abstained, 2 list, 5 single confident answers (z order 16 / 3 / 6; standard 16 / 5 / 4 and 13 / 7 / 5) against T10's 5 / 20 / 0 (RUN only) and 1 / 23 / 1 (3 tiers): a window is a small cross and one entry is an ANSWER. This is the evaluation principle's hard rule and the first open point. (6) fast -> standard adds one gold on plain (9 -> 10, 7 -> 8) and doubles the work; z order has 2 golds fewer than z slide on plain (7 / 9, 8 / 10) and within one on window (20 / 19, 21 / 21): no systematic difference. (7) One seat per shared unit (`n_only`) reads 8 of 69 (single 4 right / 7 wrong) and 3 single wrong of 25: within the noise of both seats (9; 5).

evidence window (L-714, a variant, not the owner's decision): gold in a candidate 19 of 69 at fast and 21 at standard on z slide (20 and 21 on z order) = more than T10's flat read of the same tier (15 / 18) and near its layers-ssp row (21 / 27), with 51 / 61 of 69 questions having an entry (against 25 / 38), lists of 3-5 entries (T10 17-28), and 1.3 / 3.6 s. The price is exactly the evaluation principle's: single wrong answers 17 / 12 / 14 / 8 among the answerable (single right 5 / 2 / 1 / 0, in the order z slide fast, z slide standard, z order fast, z order standard), and 10 / 9 / 8 / 6 single confident answers among the 25 unanswerable questions (list only 9 / 12 / 14 / 16, abstained 6 / 4 / 3 / 3). Trace: every window with an entry traces (trace_ok in all 107 + 127 + 195 + 230 windows of the four rows); of the 21650 path words that close an edge in the windows S1 fast reads (z slide, trace_share.py) 3491 (16 %) rest on an edge that only a constructed pair sentence evidences (plain: 0 of 10461).

### G3-f open points (owner; meaning level)
1. Confident wrong answers. A window is a small cross: one entry is an ANSWER. Flat rep gives 5 / 25 single answers to unanswerable questions at fast (4-6 on the other rows; T10 0-1) and 5 wrong against 3 right among the answerable; evidence window 10 / 25 and 17 against 5. The evaluation principle is "no single confident wrong answer". Should a window read ever be an ANSWER alone (rule: a single window's single entry is shown as a list of one with the abstention counts; or an ANSWER needs the same word set from two windows; or the verdict of a flat read is always CHOICE)? Not decided here: the verdict rule is slide_query's / ask.combine's (one entry = ANSWER).
2. The budget. The flat rows of T10 ran under QueryBudget(64, 8) (L-210); that budget settles none of the window starts (0 / 69). The window rows run under cycle's own 512 / 64 and do not compare like for like with 16 / 19. Which budget (states per start? crosses?) is the same amount of inference for a 7 x 6 + 1 seat cross and for a 3 x 6 + 1 one, and is a larger budget worth its AMBIGUOUS (tied branches ending in different results; the cycle abstains, L-106) rather than NO_FIXED_POINT? 4096 / 512 changed no gold.
3. evidence plain (the owner's "like T10") against window (L-714). Plain reads the corpus tier's n_pair on every edge, so the slide (z) evidence of an edge is not read by the flat reader (387 of 577 z-arm edges evidenced, 50 of 240 at the centre); window sums the window's x, y and z counts label-blind, which puts the slide back, doubles the golds (9 -> 19 at fast) and more than doubles the confident wrong answers (5 -> 17 among the answerable, 5 -> 10 among the unanswerable), changes the E_Q of pack pairs (design 8: the E_Q formula is unchanged, its pair input is not) and is traced against a tier with constructed pair sentences (16 % of the edge words). Should the owner's decision be read as plain, window, or neither (a separate evidence rule per arm label is impossible in the flat reader: it has no labels)?
4. Labels. Attached on the PLACED member (the start), not on the end state (the flat cycle moves units off their arms: a label of an end state would be a label of another cross). In every FLAT rep row the per-axis answers of the placed members agree for almost no entry (14 of 1796 entries, agreement three), so they say "disagree" on the entries that hold the gold. Are labels still wanted, with agreement two_if_single_edge (`label_agreement`), or on the end state?
5. members all or representative. The representative (the first member of each growth) is 696 starts for 592 windows; all strictly stable members is 103183 (the default of `ask_flat` is all; the measurement uses the representative). On the 57 questions run: members all with evidence plain reads 8 of 40 golds (243 entries in the lists; single wrong 3 of 40, 4 of 17 unanswerable) against the representative's 5 (18 entries; 3; 2 of 17); members all with evidence window reads 20 of 40 (489 entries, median 10, max 65; single wrong 2 of 40, 4 of 17 unanswerable) against the representative's 13 (42 entries; 7; 8 of 17), and T10 flat RUN-only reads 11 of 40 (773 entries; single wrong 1 of 40, 0 of 17). A question costs a median 77-84 s with all (max 1488 s) against 1.3 s. Which one is the product default; or a cap on the starts (`member_cap`) per window; the other 37 questions and standard are not measured.
6. Two seats. A unit of both sentences keeps both seats (L-703); the flat reader does not count the z self-edge n_z(u, u) that slide_ratios counts since G3-d (L-56), and a path may list the unit twice. `n_only` is measured (8 of 69; 3 single wrong of 25). Is the owner's "count the self-edge" (G3-d) meant for the flat reader as well (it would change cycle.Reader, not this module)?
7. Hook. `ask_flat` is not behind `ask(structure=...)` / the CLI (slide_query.py, cli.py, ask.py were the G3-e2 reviewer's files); the follow-up adds `structure="slide_flat"` (or replaces the per-axis reading as `slide`) and the options (`--members`, `--two-seat`, `--evidence`, `--budget`).
8. Everything is fulllead; S3000 and the held-out set are not read (the held-out folder was not opened). The 94 questions are the ones the earlier S1 / T10 rows used; one run each (the reading is deterministic: the same bytes under PYTHONHASHSEED 0 / 1 / 12345).
9. slide_query helpers are imported: `WindowIndex`, `intake`, `plan_windows`, `choose_members`, `member_cross`, `Plan`, `Intake`, `verdict` constants. A change of their signatures breaks this module's tests.

### G3-f tests, files
tests/line3/test_slide_flat.py: 36 tests (35 + the review's member_cap test): equality with cycle + readout on a hand cross held by a real placement.Placement (one start for two members that differ by a permutation of the legs; the same entries, citations and search digest), equality on the real toy windows (with shared units: entries only, a real Placement counts a cross with a unit twice wrongly), strictly stable members only / `strict="mark"`, pad cut, member-level V1, slide counts = tier counts (n_x == n_pair, before_x == p_pair on every pair of every toy window), two seats (both / n_only, centre never emptied, a unit twice on a path is a word once), labels decide nothing (labels off, labels replaced: same verdict, entries, bytes except the label), the label is slide_ratios' read of the placed member (independently recomputed), entry shape, both growths pooled, typed abstentions, the verdict precedence, V1 / cap / read order are slide_query's, representative + `growth_members`, evidence window (the sum of the counts, shared n and postings, the trace tier, equality with cycle on the WindowTier, the switch changes the reading, the default is plain, `constructed_edge_words`), PYTHONHASHSEED 0 / 1 / 12345 byte equality in five configurations, no floating-point number (AST) and no float in the output, the trace on the window tier holds and a corrupted state fails it, slide_query's bytes unchanged, `ask_flat` through an ask.Index. 88 tests pass with tests/line3/test_slide_ratios.py (52) after the review. Experiments: experiments/line3/g3/s1flat/ (measure_flat.py, sweep_flat.sh, summarize_flat.py, edge_evidence.py, trace_share.py, subset_ids.py, equiv_check.py; results/ the final runs with .meta.json (argv, config, shas, cache files), results_pre/ the earlier runs of the equivalence check and the six questions of the first members-all attempt).

### G3-f review (2026-10-09, auditor; additions only)
Fixes in G3-f's files: (a) `verdict_of` typed cycle's member status `mixed` below section disagreement / unstable or as UNKNOWN_NO_EVIDENCE although L-709 claims cycle._verdict_from's precedence (which reads "none:mixed" with the ratio disagreement); fixed, tested (L-709). (b) `member_cap` (cycle's, per arm length) left starts unsettled without a count; now `member_cap` in the tally and in `members_not_read` (L-707), tested; unused in every run. (c) sweep_flat.sh lacked the `ev` and `seat` phases its header promised (the rows were run by hand); added with the argv of the .meta.json. (d) summarize_flat.py gains the per-question overlap with T10. The implementer's last slide_flat.py (ae42251c3619) reproduced fast z slide rep plain and window for 94 of 94 questions; the eleven rep rows were then re-run with the fixed file (779be502d101): every entry, citation, label, abstention and tally is unchanged, 13 verdict strings of no-entry questions changed (5 rows), so equiv_check.py now reports those 13 against results_pre/ by design; the seconds of summary_flat.md are the re-runs' (the table above keeps the first runs'). 88 tests pass (test_slide_flat.py 36 + test_slide_ratios.py 52).

Checked: every row of the table above re-derives from results/*.jsonl with t9/scorer.py (independent script). L-701 holds on the real corpus: n_x == n_pair and before_x == p_pair on all 53503 pack pairs of the 592 fulllead windows (z slide cache). The flat read is cycle's own (`_ask_tier_once` with `plan_override`, `read_out_result`, `trace_answer`; cycle / readout / trace_check untouched).

evidence window (L-714). The constructed pair sentences do not enter the reading (E_Q, the walk and the flow read `WindowTier.n_pair`; the pair-sentence tier is passed to `trace_answer` only). But the pair input does change, and in a way that empties two checks inside a window: every pair of the window's pack has n_pair >= 1 under "window" (0 of the 53503 pack pairs are 0, against 17687 under "plain"), so the walk's "no edge" stop and trace_check's rule (c) never refuse inside a window; only the energy order and the ratio agreement still decide. The 19-21 of 69 are therefore a reading of a different space (same sentence OR adjacent sentences), traced on a weakened P-4, under a different budget: they can stand in their own row beside T10's numbers with those three caveats, not as "the flat reader of T10 on windows".

Budget (open point 2), measured: T10's flat ask, RUN tier only, fast, on the T10 code (be923510 snapshot) and the T10 placement cache at 512 / 64 (experiments/line3/g3/s1flat/review_t10_budget.py, results/t10_run_fast_b512-64.jsonl; it reads two paths outside the worktree, vera-impl/t10_snapshot and vera-impl/cache/f1b; the same code at 64 / 8 reproduces the recorded T10 RUN entries of 6 of 6 questions whose lists changed): gold in a candidate 16 of 69 (15 at 64 / 8; R2-I016 added, none lost), single right / wrong 2 / 3, no candidate 6, unans 5 / 20 / 0; 42 of the 94 lists change, median list 21 entries (18); 2541 of 92471 members still end NO_FIXED_POINT; median 38 s a question (RUN only, 4 workers). The standard preset of T10 at 512 / 64 is not run (fast took 99 CPU-minutes; standard would be 2-3 times that), so 18 against 21 keeps the budget caveat. So at the window rows' budget T10 flat RUN-only reads 16, not 15 (fast): plain windows 9 / 10 stay below it, window evidence 19 / 21 above it.

| rep z slide fast, window | T10 flat-fast (3 tiers) | 19 | 16 | 11 | 8 | 8 | 27 |
| rep z slide fast, window | T10 layers-ssp-fast | 19 | 21 | 8 | 11 | 10 | 29 |
| rep z slide standard, window | T10 flat-standard RUN | 21 | 18 | 11 | 10 | 8 | 29 |
| rep z slide standard, window | T10 layers-ssp-standard | 21 | 27 | 7 | 14 | 13 | 34 |
| rep z order fast, window | T10 flat-fast RUN (64/8) | 20 | 15 | 9 | 11 | 4 | 24 |

All six window rows (plain and window, fast and standard) together find 28 golds, all six T10 rows together 28, the union 37: 9 golds are found by a window row and by no T10 row (I2-002 I2-004 I2-006 I2-024 I2-030 I2-042 I2-043 I2-045 R2-I018), 9 by a T10 row and by no window row. Six of the nine (I2-004, I2-006, I2-024, I2-030, I2-042, I2-043; all from evidence window rows) are the case the slide was built for: the question's subject is in sentence N (花園町駅, 海浦駅, 近江八幡郵便局, 中之嶽神社, 五十嵐浜村, 宮床村) and the gold in N+1 (Y17, OR08, 集配普通郵便局, 16柱, 新潟市西区, 大和町); the other three (I2-002, I2-045 one-sentence windows; R2-I018 gold in N) come from plain rows and are not a crossing.

Confident wrong answers (open point 1), by cause: every single answer is one entry of one window (entries are never merged across windows). Plain fast: of the 5 single answers to unanswerable questions, 3 are partial reads (UF-010 read 1 of its 11 candidate windows, UF-016, UF-020) and 4 had other windows read that abstained with a typed kind (UF-008 is the only one whose single window was all it had). Window fast: 10 single answers to unanswerable questions, 5 of them from a question that read exactly one window (UF-004, UF-010, UF-012, R2-U003, R2-U004), 3 partial. The ANSWER rule (one entry = ANSWER) sees neither the cap (`partial`) nor the sibling windows' abstentions.

results_pre/ (3.2 MB): its only product is the equivalence count, recorded here and in notes_flat.md, and it predates the verdict fix; recommended git-ignored except flat_fast_zslide_all_partial6.jsonl(.meta.json), which L-715 cites (`experiments/line3/g3/s1flat/results_pre/*` plus `!experiments/line3/g3/s1flat/results_pre/flat_fast_zslide_all_partial6.jsonl*` in .gitignore; not done here, .gitignore is not a G3-f file).

## G3-g (2026-10-09): ONE labelled candidate list from the flat cross, the layers and the windows, `structure="combined"` (verantyx/line3/combined.py; ask.py / cli.py hooks; slide_flat.py opt-in `word_sources`; tests/line3/test_combined.py; experiments/line3/g3/combined/: measure_windows.py, sweep_windows.sh, replay.py, summarize_combined.py, equiv_check.py, results/summary.md)
Owner (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md, 「G3-f の監査後の決定」, verbatim choices): 「両方の候補を 1 つの一覧に並べる（出所のラベル付き）」 (the windows and T10 in ONE list, each candidate labelled by its origin); 「両方を候補に並べて測る」 (the windows' plain evidence and the window-sum evidence variant are BOTH listed, marked); 「窓の候補は常に一覧として出す（1 件でも「答え」にしない）」 (a candidate only windows give is never a single ANSWER). Kept from before: typed abstentions per source, provenance per word, the P-4 trace, no floating-point number, determinism, byte-identical defaults (the combined list is opt-in). Judgement points are numbered L-720.. (L-700..L-715 are G3-f).

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-720 | THE LIST. Sources, each read on its own (I-16: its own search, read-out and abstentions; whether sources agree is only reported): `flat` = ask.ask view "all" (the 3 tiers, T7b), origin `flat/<tier>`; `layers` = matryoshka.ask_layered with T10's measured configuration (candidate stable-seats-path, variant A, granularity compress, feedback none, bounds of the effort) on that SAME layer 0, the UPPER entries only (layer 0 is the flat source; `listed()` would list every flat entry twice), origin `layers/<tier>/<layer><variant>` (e.g. `layers/RUN/1A`); `window/plain` and `window/window-evidence` = slide_flat.ask_flat, evidence plain | window (L-701 / L-714). The list = the candidates of the sources in the FIXED order flat (RUN, WORD, CHAR), layers (tier, layer, variant), window/plain, window/window-evidence, each source in its own order; no ranking, no vote, nothing summed or shortened (I-14 / N-20). | combined.py `origin_key`, `merge`, `combine` |
| L-721 | ONE ENTRY PER WORD SET (the owner's L-235 same-word-set rule, now across sources). Candidates with the same word set (compared as sorted sets) are ONE entry placed where its first candidate stands; it lists all its origins (`origins`, sorted in the fixed order), the families (`flat` / `layers` / `window`) and EVERY contributing candidate as a member with its own centres, stability, arrangement count, sentences and labels (tier, layer, bundles, window n / title / sids, centre_sentence, axis labels, trace). Nothing is summed or compared across members (stabilities of different sources are different quantities). Different word sets are never merged (no subset, no overlap rule). This DEPARTS from two earlier rules and the owner is asked to confirm (open point 1): T7b L-220 (a same word set in two tiers is two entries) and G3-f L-700 (entries are never merged across windows; two windows with the same word set are now two members of one entry). | combined.py `Entry`, `merge` |
| L-722 | PROVENANCE PER WORD. Per word and per contributing candidate: the sentences of the steps into / out of the word. flat: `readout.entry_word_sources` of the live entry; layers: the entry's `word_sources` (L-251); windows: slide_flat gets an OPT-IN `word_sources=False` (read_window_flat / ask_flat): with True each entry dict also carries `word_sources` {word: sids} (the same readout function) and `config.word_sources` is True; with False (default) the entries, the config and the bytes are exactly what they were (tested: default bytes equal word_sources=False; the True entries minus the key equal the default ones). `word_provenance()` rows have level `word`, `none` (the source records no step sentence for that word: a word alone on its path, or, with the window evidence, a word whose edge only the window's constructed pair count evidences, L-714; the entry's trace says how many) or `entry` (a source that keeps only the entry's sentences). A REPLAYED candidate (measurement) has no sentences at all and gives no row. Cited sentences = every sid of the list, with text and source. | combined.py `Entry.word_provenance`, slide_flat.py |
| L-723 | BOTH EVIDENCE VARIANTS, MARKED. `window_evidence` "both" (default of the library and of `--window-evidence`) | "plain" | "window". The windows are read twice (one window index shared); the window-sum variant is a MEASURED VARIANT (L-714), so its candidates carry the origin `window/window-evidence` and every entry that has such a member gets the mark `window_evidence_variant` (`marks`; text form 【窓の証拠の変種】). An entry that both variants (or a variant and another source) give lists all its origins. | combined.py |
| L-724 | VERDICT. Over the combined entries: >= 2 entries = CHOICE; exactly 1 entry that some NON-window origin gives (flat or layers, with or without a window member) = ANSWER; exactly 1 entry that only windows give = CHOICE (a list of one: `answer.answer` is None, `window_only_single` True); no entry = the first verdict a source reports that is not ANSWER / CHOICE, in the order flat, layers, window/plain, window/window-evidence (a REPORTING precedence, it selects nothing; flat's UNKNOWN_NO_STATE / UNKNOWN_NO_PATH wins when the flat cross ran, as ask.combine / ask_layered do today); every source's own verdict stays in `answer.sources`. The layers' verdict with no entry and no layer stacked is `NOT_STACKED` (a report, not an UNKNOWN). Different from t9's count rule (n == 1 = single): a list of one window-only entry is a LIST here. | combined.py `verdict_of` |
| L-725 | TYPED ABSTENTIONS PER SOURCE (`answer.abstentions`, each row with its `source`; `abstention_counts`): flat = per tier without an entry, kind = the tier's verdict (UNKNOWN_NO_STATE / UNKNOWN_NO_PATH / UNKNOWN_NO_FIXED_POINT ...); layers = per run without an entry, kind = the run's verdict (a tier whose layers were not stacked is not an abstention); windows = slide_flat's own per-window typed abstentions (ratio_disagreement, section_disagreement, points_nowhere, not_grounded, mixed, ambiguous, no_fixed_point, unstable_axis_improvable, no_question_unit, no_path), and, when no window was read, one source-level row {kind: UNKNOWN_NO_WINDOW | UNKNOWN_NOT_READ, windows: 0}. A question every source abstains on has verdict = the first report (L-724) and an empty list. | combined.py `flat_source`, `layers_source`, `window_source_of` |
| L-726 | THE ENTRANCE. `ask.ask(index, q, structure="combined", **kw)` (ask.STRUCTURES gains "combined"; the flat and slide paths are not touched) -> combined.ask_combined: `view` must be "all" (the I-16 stable view is a different list), `granularity` is refused, `window_evidence` "both"|"plain"|"window", `slide_members` "representative" (DEFAULT here; slide_flat's own default "all" costs a start per member, L-715), `read_order`, `layer_variants` ("A"), `layer_granularity` ("compress"), `windows` (a WindowIndex made beforehand) or the index's (built / loaded as ask_slide does), `z_deep`, `trace`. `effort` / `nodes` are each source's own amount: crosses per tier for the flat cross, the layer bounds, WINDOWS for the windows (as G3-e); neither = the whole read. Each source keeps its own search budget (flat: T7b 64 / 8, windows: cycle's 512 / 64, L-705: not budget-matched). CLI: `vera line3 ask --structure combined [--window-evidence plain|window|both]` (`--slide-members` default None: "all" for slide, "representative" for combined; `--query-pass`, `--layer-granularity`, `--read-order`, `--z-deep` pass through); `--choose` / `--record` / `--granularity` / `--view stable` / `--layers off` / `--layer-feedback down` / `--layer-down-query seed+question` / another `--layer-candidate` are refused for combined (exit 2; the slide options `--agreement` / `--answer-shape` are not read by combined, as the flat structure does not read them); `vera line3 build --structure combined --cache DIR` builds the flat placements and the window index (two kinds of files in one directory). Text form: the list with origins, a note for a window-only single, per-source counts, abstention kinds, cited sentences. | ask.py, cli.py, combined.py |
| L-727 | P-4. Every source carries its own trace: flat = trace_check.trace_answer on each tier's read-out (live only, `trace=True`), layers = the run's `trace` (bundle level, down to a base state), windows = the entry's `trace` (slide_flat L-710). A candidate has `trace_ok`; an entry is traced only if every member is (one failed member makes it False, a member without a trace leaves it None); `answer.sources[*].trace` and the top-level `partial`. Tested on every toy question (all True) and with a flag that bites (a failed flat member makes every entry it is in False). | combined.py |
| L-728 | BYTES. `CombinedAnswer.to_bytes()` = slide.canonical of {answer, thought} (sorted keys, Fractions as "n/d", an inexact number is refused); no wall time in the bytes; combined.py has no floating-point number and no division operator (AST test); PYTHONHASHSEED 0 / 1 / 12345 give the same bytes (subprocess test over the toy questions in three configurations; and the CLI json output). Defaults byte-identical: `ask.ask` flat bytes equal with and without `structure="flat"`, the slide path is unchanged, slide_flat's default bytes equal `word_sources=False`; tests/line3/test_combined.py has 25 tests (combiner on hand-built candidates, union of the three real sources on the toy corpus with the origins worked out independently, equal word sets, window-only lists, verdicts, abstentions, provenance, P-4, refusals, ask.ask and CLI entrance, hash seeds, no float); goldens run once on this tree: tests/line3/test_ask.py test_ask_ordered.py test_matryoshka.py test_slide_flat.py test_slide_query.py, 216 passed (12 min). | tests/line3/test_combined.py |
| L-729 | MEASUREMENT (experiments/line3/g3/combined/): bank2 fulllead, 69 intra2 + 25 unans, fast and standard. The flat cross and the layers are NOT re-run: T10's raw records (`t10/results/ask_fulllead_{fast,standard}_ordered-stop.jsonl`, T10 code sha be923510340a26a073e6cfda4211404fe361e03b, the ordered cache `~/Projects/vera-impl/cache/f1b`) are replayed through the SAME combiner (`replay.py` rebuilds a Source from a record: words, centres, stability, count, the layers' run trace flag; no sentences). The windows are read LIVE (measure_windows.py: representative members, evidence plain and window, G3-c3 default placements z_deep slide, window cache of the scratch dir, 6 workers, 1-min load 2.9-8.6 with the goldens running at the same time, 85 s per sweep of 94 questions). Grading is t9's scorer; the combined rows' single / list / none follow the verdict (L-724), all other rows t9's count rule. The recomputed T10 and window rows equal the published tables (flat 16 / 19, ssp 21 / 27, windows 9 / 10 plain and 19 / 21 evidence; summary.md "Replay checks"). Small difference: T10's tables list the layers' tiers alphabetically (its records were written with sorted keys), the replay uses the live order RUN, WORD, CHAR, so a first-gold position of ssp can differ by a few (fast max 80 against T10's 82). | experiments/line3/g3/combined/ |
| L-730 | IS THE REPLAY HONEST? equiv_check.py re-ran the LIVE ask_combined (ask.ask + ask_layered stable-seats-path + both window variants, trace on) on the cheapest recorded questions and compared, source by source and entry by entry in order, words, origins and verdict with what the replay builds: fast 12 of 12 identical (I2-040 R2-I003 UF-006 I2-014 I2-003 I2-020 I2-023 UF-004 I2-044 I2-041 R2-I013 UF-002, 43-60 entries each); standard 4 of 4 identical (UF-014 I2-036 I2-044 R2-I003, 21-69 entries each, 300-340 s live against 411-476 s recorded). The current code (G3-f ... G3-g) therefore reproduces T10's flat and layers lists on these questions although T10 ran at be92351 plus the F1c work in progress. A full live sweep (every question, every source) was not run (about an hour at 8 workers). | equiv_check.py, results/equiv_*.txt |

### G3-g measurement (results/summary.md; intra2 n = 69, unans n = 25)

Headline on decision 3. The rule "a candidate only windows give is never a single ANSWER" never fired in the combined list: 0 of 94 questions at fast and at standard end with one entry (the flat cross alone lists something for 91 of 94 questions at fast and 94 of 94 at standard, with lists of tens of entries, so the combined list has several entries or none), hence 0 ANSWER, 0 confident-wrong, 0 abstention but one question. The rule bites only where windows stand alone (windows-alone rows below): 5 / 10 (plain / evidence, fast) and 4 / 9 (standard) of the 25 unanswerable questions would have been single wrong answers under the count rule and are lists under the owner's rule; with both variants merged 8 of 25 are lists of ONE window-only entry at both presets.

Flat cross and layers replayed from T10, windows live, one combined list per question. Gold in a candidate / single right / single wrong / list with gold / list without gold / no candidate (intra2); unans: abstained / list / single (confident wrong).
