* Gold per origin in the owner's list (fast / standard): flat/RUN 15 / 18, flat/WORD 6 / 8, flat/CHAR 0 / 0, layers 11 / 24, window/plain 9 / 10, window/window-evidence 19 / 21; flat any tier 16 / 19; flat + layers 21 / 27; + window/plain 24 / 30; all 29 / 34. Found ONLY by windows: fast 8 (I2-002 I2-004 I2-006 I2-009 I2-030 I2-045 R2-I016 R2-I018), standard 7 (I2-002 I2-004 I2-006 I2-030 I2-043 I2-045 R2-I018); found only by flat + layers: 10 / 13. The windows add +8 (fast) and +7 (standard) golds to T10's ssp (21 / 27); plain alone adds 3 of those; the window-sum evidence is what adds the rest.
* The 9 B2-hard items: gold in a candidate 4 of 9 at fast (I2-014 I2-029 I2-031 R2-I013) and 6 of 9 at standard (+ I2-033 R2-I006), the same sets as T10's ssp; the windows also hold the gold of I2-014, I2-031, R2-I013 (fast) and I2-014, I2-031, R2-I006, R2-I013 (standard) but of no item the flat + layers part misses. Found by no origin: I2-011, I2-033, I2-039, R2-I003, R2-I006 at fast; I2-011, I2-039, R2-I003 at standard. Per origin per item: summary.md.
* Verdicts (owner's list): ANSWER 0 of 94 at both presets (UNKNOWN 1 at fast: a question no source lists; every other question is a CHOICE), so the confident-wrong count is 0 and the unanswerable questions are all rejectable lists (25 of 25) and none abstains: the flat cross lists something for 24-25 of 25 even in T10, and the other sources add to it. A one-entry list that only windows give never occurred in the combined lists (0); windows alone it occurs: 8 of 25 unans questions at both presets with both variants merged (the count rule would have made them single answers: windows alone single answers, plain / evidence, fast: 8 (3 right) / 22 (5 right) on intra2 and 5 / 10 wrong on unans; standard: 10 / 14 and 4 / 9).
* The merge changes little: entries before merge median 28.5 / max 151 (fast) and 68 / 239 (standard) against 28.5 / 147 and 60 / 227 after. Of the 27 (fast) / 60 (standard) entries over the 94 questions that list several origins, 25 / 58 are the two window variants giving the same word set; the coincidences across families are two per preset (fast: I2-008 flat/RUN + layers/RUN/1A, UF-010 flat/RUN + window/plain; standard: I2-014 flat/RUN + layers/RUN/1A + layers/RUN/2A, UF-010); no word set is given by two flat tiers. The word sets of the families almost never coincide (flat / layers entries hold 100s of words, a window entry 3-10), so the merge does not show agreement between flat, layers and windows.
* Size: the list is dominated by the flat cross and the layers: words in the list median 484 / max 1801 (fast), 1956 / 5384 (standard); the windows add 2-17 entries (both variants merged: median 2, max 10 at fast; median 4, max 17 at standard). The windows' candidates stand LAST (L-720): the gold of the golds only windows find stands at position 1-65 (fast) and 9-120 (standard) of lists of 2-122 entries (summary.md).
* Time: flat + layers = T10's recorded times (10 workers, load 20-40: median 292 s fast / 922 s standard, max 755 / 1602 s); the windows add a median 3.0 s (max 31 s) at fast and 8.9 s (max 44 s) at standard for BOTH variants on this machine (load 2.9-8.6); a live combined question is the sum of its parts.
* P-4: window/plain 69 / 69 (fast) and 141 / 141 (standard) entries trace; window/window-evidence 147 / 147 and 285 / 285 trace, but 106 of 147 (fast) and 195 of 285 (standard) hold a word whose edge only a constructed pair sentence evidences (level `none` in the provenance); layers 166 / 166 and 382 / 382 runs trace (T10 flag).

### G3-g open points (owner questions; behaviour decided only as written above)
  (1) Merge scope (L-721). One entry per word set across tiers, layers and windows departs from L-220 (a same word set in two tiers is two entries) and from L-700 (never merged across windows). Measured effect: small (median 0 fewer entries at fast, 8 at standard; no ANSWER arises from a merge; of the 27 / 60 multi-origin entries 25 / 58 are the two window variants agreeing, and two per preset cross families). Is the cross-source merge wanted as it is, kept only across origins (L-700 restored inside a window origin), or replaced by something that shows containment (a short window set inside a long flat set), which "never merged across different word sets" forbids? Review note: the recorded decision says only 「1 つの一覧に並べる（出所のラベル付き）」; the cross-source merge is not derivable from it (L-235 merges inside one layer run and keeps labels apart; L-220 and L-700 keep two entries). No switch for an unmerged list is built (it would run merge -> combine -> ask_combined -> ask -> CLI); nothing is lost meanwhile: every entry keeps every member with its origin, so the unmerged list is the members in list order, a display choice, not a rerun.
  (2) Order (L-720). The fixed order puts the windows last, so a gold only windows find stands at position up to 120 of 122 behind the flat and layers entries, although the windows list 2-17 entries and the flat part 28-60 (median). No ranking was added (I-14). Windows first, one block per family with a header, or a per-family display cap are product choices; the data (origin on every entry) allow any of them without a rerun.
  (3) Length. The owner's list has median 28.5 / 60 entries and up to 147 / 227 (median 484 / 1956 words) at fast / standard, nearly all from the flat cross and the layers (T10's lists); it never is a single answer, so the gain over B1 / B2 (31 / 20 single right answers) is nil and a user must read a long list to reach 29 / 34 of 69 golds against B2's 57.
  (4) No abstention. No question is answered with an empty list but one at fast (1 of 94); the unanswerable ones are 25 / 25 rejectable lists, 0 confident wrong. The typed abstentions per source are there (`abstention_counts`), but the combined verdict is CHOICE whenever any source lists something. Whether a question that only the window-evidence variant lists (the weaker P-4) should be marked more strongly than the entry mark is open.
  (5) Weaker P-4 of the window-sum evidence (L-714): 106 of 147 (fast) of its entries hold a word evidenced only by a constructed pair sentence; the mark `window_evidence_variant` is on every such entry, and the per-word level `none` shows which word. It is the variant that adds the 5 extra golds.
  (6) Replay. The flat and layers candidates of the measurement are T10's records: no sentences, no per-word provenance, no flat P-4 flag; 12 of 12 (fast) live re-runs were identical (L-730); a full live sweep is not run. cross2 and s3000 are not run.
  (7) Effort and budgets. `effort` counts crosses per tier for the flat cross and WINDOWS for the windows; the search budgets differ (64 / 8 vs 512 / 64, L-705); the windows are read with representative members (L-715). A single node budget across the sources, or members "all", is not decided.
  (8) `--choose` / `--record` (the memory record of a chosen candidate) is not built for the combined list: what a chosen merged entry records (all its origins? the member the user clicked?) is not decided.
  (9) Layers: T10's stable-seats-path, variant A, compress is fixed (`--query-pass both` is accepted: A and B). Memory (corrected at review): the 19 GB probe was ONE call, not growth over a loop. 50 calls of ask_combined in one process on the toy corpus at fast stay at 52-54 MB resident (at fast / standard 52-58 MB; the stacks' read caches `_down` hold 5-34 entries and clear themselves above 4000). What explodes is matryoshka.ask_layered at effort `full` on the toy question 東京は何ですか (same on HEAD 1fbc76f2 without G3-g, also with the default `--layer-candidate path`): building an upper-layer cross, placement.build_cross takes its representative arrangement with `next(expand_flats(state[:1], L, twin_sets))` (placement.py, since T4d), and expand_flats feeds `itertools.permutations` of each twin set into `itertools.product`, which materialises every input before the first output: a twin set of 14 bundles = 14! (8.7e10) tuples (measured: 10 units already take 0.5 GB). Not a G3-g defect and not fixed here (placement.py is not a G3-g file); it is reachable from `vera line3 ask --effort full` with the layers on (the default), so it needs its own ticket. The layer stacks kept on the index (`_t8_stacks`) are bounded and not the cause.

## G3-g2 (2026-10-09): the combined list without merging across sources, in per-origin blocks, the typed abstentions first (verantyx/line3/combined.py `merge`, `also_in`, `header`, `blocks`; cli.py `--merge`; ask.py untouched, it passes `**kw`; tests/line3/test_combined.py; tests/line3/golden_combined_g3g.json.gz; experiments/line3/g3/combined/: summarize_g2.py, summarize_combined.py `merge="word_set"`, results/summary_g2.md, results/g2_win_*.jsonl)
Owner (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md, 「G3-g の監査後の決定」, verbatim choices): 「まとめず、出所ごとに別の候補のまま並べる（I-16 どおり）」 (equal word sets from different origins stay separate; an agreement may be shown as a mark only); 「出所ごとの型付きの保留を一覧の先頭に表示し、全体の判定は今のまま」; 「出所ごとに区切って見せ、各区切りは安定の順」 (blocks flat RUN, flat WORD, flat CHAR, layers, window/plain, window/window-evidence; flat/layers: the order the source already gives, T7b; windows: the order slide_flat gives; no ranking across blocks). Kept from before: P-4 per source, typed abstentions per source, no floating-point number, determinism, byte-identical defaults of the other structures. The G3-g merged form stays reachable as `merge="word_set"`. Judgement points are numbered L-740.. (L-720..L-730 are G3-g).

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-740 | NO MERGE BY DEFAULT. `combine(..., merge="none"\|"word_set")`, `ask_combined(..., merge=...)`, default `"none"` (a bad value is refused: ValueError). `"none"`: every candidate of every source is its OWN entry (one member), including two windows of one origin with the same word set (L-700 restored) and the same word set from two flat tiers (L-220 restored); `listed == listed_before_merge`. `"word_set"`: exactly G3-g (L-721: one entry per word set, placed where its first candidate stands, all members kept). This reverses L-721 as the default; the open point (1) of G3-g is answered by the owner. The function that builds the list is `build_entries(sources, how)`; `merge` stays as its alias (G3-g's name). `CombinedAnswer.merge`, `answer.merge` and `thought.merge` say which form a list is; `thought.rule` names it. | combined.py `build_entries`, `combine`, `ask_combined` |
| L-741 | `also_in` = THE AGREEMENT MARK. Each entry of a `"none"` list carries `also_in`: the OTHER origins (sorted in the fixed origin order) that give an entry with the same word set (compared as sorted sets, as L-721). It is a mark only: nothing reads it (not the verdict, the order, the blocks, the marks, the provenance, the trace), nothing is counted or summed from it, and no stability of different origins is compared. A twin entry of the SAME origin (two windows with one word set) is not an "other origin" and is not marked (two entries, each with its own window detail; open point 2). Entry objects of a `"none"` list gain `block` and `also_in`; the entries of a `"word_set"` list have exactly their G3-g keys (they list all their origins). `agreement()` (report only) is counted over distinct word sets, so it is the same in both modes (corrected at review: it read `also_in` as well, redundantly, since every candidate is an entry; that read is removed, the output is unchanged, so outside the display nothing reads the mark). | combined.py `Entry.also_in`, `build_entries` |
| L-742 | THE HEADER (typed abstentions first). `answer.header` = one row per flat tier read (flat/RUN, flat/WORD, flat/CHAR), the layers, and each window variant read, in the fixed source order: `{source, listed, kind, kinds}`. `listed` = the candidates of that source; `kind` = null when it listed something, else its typed abstention: flat/<tier> = the tier's verdict (UNKNOWN_NO_STATE / NO_PATH / NO_FIXED_POINT / AMBIGUOUS ...), layers and windows = the source's verdict (the layers' NOT_STACKED is a report, L-724; the window variant's typed verdict by slide_flat's reporting precedence, or UNKNOWN_NO_WINDOW / UNKNOWN_NOT_READ); `kinds` = {kind: n} over the typed abstentions of the parts of the source (flat: the tier's own; layers: runs without an entry; windows: windows without an entry), also where the source listed something. A flat source that records no tier is one row `flat`. Derived from the sources' abstentions only, so it is equal in both merge modes; the overall verdict rule (L-724) does not look at it. The text form prints it before everything else (「出所ごとの状況」); the later 「答えなし（種類別）」 line of G3-g is dropped (the header carries the counts). | combined.py `CombinedAnswer.header`, `format_text` |
| L-743 | BLOCKS. `merge="none"`: the candidates are taken in the source order flat, layers, window/plain, window/window-evidence and then ARRANGED BY BLOCK with a stable sort (`block_key`): flat/RUN, flat/WORD, flat/CHAR, layers (all layers/<tier>/<layer><variant> origins are ONE block, in the layers source's own run order), window/plain, window/window-evidence. Nothing inside a block is re-ordered: flat and layers keep the order the source gives (T7b: the order of the tier's entries; the layers' run order), the windows the order slide_flat gives; there is no sort by stability, size or anything else, and no ranking across blocks (tested: the stabilities of the entries of a block are in the order of the source, not sorted). The only change against G3-g's order is that a flat source which gives its tiers in another order than RUN, WORD, CHAR is shown in the fixed block order; only a hand-built `Source` can do that (corrected at review: the `tiers` argument chooses which tiers are read, never their order, since ask.combine sorts the tier outcomes RUN, WORD, CHAR, ask.py:511), so every live list (and every replayed one) has the candidate order of G3-g. Also corrected at review: "the order the source gives" is NOT a stability order: a flat tier's entries are in word-set order (readout.read_answer, `similar="word_set"`, sorts them by their words, readout.py:604), a window block is in window read order and, inside a window, word-set order (slide_flat.ask_flat concatenates the reads, slide_flat.py:585), the layers block in run order; measured on bank2 (merge none, the replayed records), a flat/RUN block of >= 2 entries is not in non-increasing stability in 61 / 78 (fast) and 71 / 82 (standard) questions, window/window-evidence 23 / 38 and 44 / 59; whether this order meets the owner's 「各区切りは安定の順」 is G3-g2 open point (8). `answer.blocks` = [{block, listed, first}] (first = index of its first entry; entries stay ONE list with global indices, so overall and within-block positions are both derivable); `Entry.block` = block of the first member. `merge="word_set"` does not arrange (G3-g's order, byte for byte); its blocks are the runs of first-member blocks. | combined.py `block_of`, `block_key`, `build_entries`, `CombinedAnswer.blocks` |
| L-744 | VERDICT ON THE ENTRIES AS LISTED (the rule is unchanged, L-724, but its input changed). >= 2 entries = CHOICE; exactly 1 entry that some non-window origin gives = ANSWER; exactly 1 window-only entry = CHOICE; none = the first report. With no merge, two candidates that happen to have the same word set (flat {a,b} and window {a,b}) are TWO entries, so the verdict is CHOICE where G3-g's merged list said ANSWER (tested on hand-built sources; `also_in` does not change the verdict). So a single ANSWER now requires exactly one candidate in the whole list (t9's count rule). Measured effect on bank2: none (0 verdicts change at fast and standard, intra2 and unans), because the combined lists were never short. | combined.py `verdict_of` |
| L-745 | `merge="word_set"` REPRODUCES G3-g. tests/line3/golden_combined_g3g.json.gz holds the full `to_json_obj()` (answer and thought) of the committed G3-g code (cc8be195) for the 12 toy questions x 3 configurations (both variants; plain; window evidence with members all) = 36 objects, generated BEFORE any edit. The test builds the same lists with `merge="word_set"`, removes the keys G3-g2 adds (answer: `merge`, `header`, `blocks`; thought: `merge`) and requires equality: every entry, origin, member, centre, stability, provenance row, abstention, cited sentence, `thought.rule` text and agreement count is as committed, and the new keys are exactly those. The default list is checked against it from the other side: the multiset of members (with their details) of the G3-g entries equals the multiset of members of the default list. The summary tables of G3-g (results/summary.md) regenerate byte for byte but the code-state line when summarize_combined.py builds its lists with `merge="word_set"`. | tests/line3/test_combined.py, tests/line3/golden_combined_g3g.json.gz |
| L-746 | ENTRANCE AND TEXT. `ask.ask(index, q, structure="combined", merge=...)` (through `**kw`, ask.py unchanged); `vera line3 ask --structure combined --merge none\|word_set` (default none; ignored by the other structures like `--window-evidence`). Text form: the header block first, then (CHOICE) the list with a heading per block 「 == flat/RUN (N 件) == 」 and numbered entries; an entry of the unmerged list shows 「ほかの出所にも同じ語の集合: <origins>」 when `also_in` is not empty, and 【窓の証拠の変種】 as before; then the per-source counts, verdicts and cited sentences. `--choose` / `--record` stay refused for combined. | combined.py `format_text`, cli.py |
| L-747 | TESTS (tests/line3/test_combined.py): 40 tests (25 of G3-g: 7 that assert the merge now say `merge="word_set"`, 2 extended to both modes (hash seeds, no float); 15 new). New: merge modes and default; equal word sets from different origins stay separate (also two windows of one origin) and `merge="word_set"` joins them; `also_in` = the other origins only, none for a twin window, none in word_set mode, never changes the verdict (an ANSWER of G3-g becomes a CHOICE, flagged); the default list on the toy equals the MULTISET of (word set, origin) of the three sources run on their own; `merge="word_set"` equals the G3-g golden; blocks in the fixed order for any order the sources are handed over, each block in its source's own order (stabilities deliberately not sorted), flat tiers arranged into RUN, WORD, CHAR, entry sequences of a block equal the source's candidate sequence on the toy, nothing of another block moves a block's entries; header rows in the fixed order with kinds (hand-built; and on the toy against the sources and blocks); text form (header first, block headings, marks); `ask.ask` passes `merge` and refuses a bad one; CLI `--merge`; hash seeds 0 / 1 / 12345 over both merge modes (subprocess) and CLI json; no float or division in combined.py, none in any json value of either mode. Goldens: tests/line3/test_ask.py run once on this tree: 26 passed (7 min); the flat defaults are byte-identical (test_flat_and_slide_are_the_default_and_nothing_leaks_into_them). | tests/line3/test_combined.py |

### G3-g2 open points (owner questions; behaviour decided only as written above)
  (1) A single ANSWER now needs exactly one candidate in the whole list (L-744): the same word set from two origins is two entries and a CHOICE, where G3-g's merged list showed an ANSWER (the owner's 「全体の判定は今のまま」 applied to the entries as listed). On bank2 this never happens (0 verdicts change). Is that the intended reading, or should an agreement of origins on a lone word set stay a single answer (that would need a verdict that reads `also_in`, which the owner's "a mark only" forbids)?
  (2) Twin windows. Two windows of one origin with the same word set are two entries (L-700) and carry no `also_in` (the mark names OTHER origins): 5 / 22 such word sets at fast / standard. They are visible only as two entries of a block with the same words. A mark for them (`also_in` listing the origin itself, or a separate `twin_windows`) is not built.
  (3) Layers are ONE block (the owner lists six blocks): the layers' runs (tier, layer, variant) are in the source's order inside it; the origin of each entry names its run. A block per layer tier or per layer would make 3-9 blocks. The header also has one layers row, whose `kinds` count the runs that found nothing (55 / 60 of 94 questions at fast / standard list the layers and still have a run that ended without a fixed point).
  (4) The list got longer by the entries the merge had joined: 28.5 / 68 entries median (151 / 239 max), still dominated by flat and layers; the unmerged list makes the two window variants' equal sets two adjacent-block entries (25 / 58 word sets). The user's reading cost for a window gold is now 'go to the window block': the gold stands at position 1-2 (median 1) INSIDE its block, but the window blocks come last, at overall position up to 124 of 126. Block order (the owner chose it) is not a ranking, so no change was made; a collapsed / foldable display of the blocks that hold no gold is a product choice (summary_g2: a block lists something without gold in 6-54 of 69 questions).
  (5) The header says which source abstained but the verdict is CHOICE whenever any source lists (L-724 unchanged): 93 / 82 of 94 questions have at least one abstaining source, and the 25 unanswerable questions are 25 lists, none abstained (as G3-g). Whether a question on which the flat cross (or all three tiers) abstains while only window variants list should be marked more strongly than the header row is open (unans: flat/RUN lists 20 / 21 of 25, window/plain 7 / 9).
  (6) Text form: the header precedes the verdict line (「答え」 / 「候補 N 件」) for an ANSWER too; the 「答えなし（種類別）」 line was dropped as the header carries the counts. The `--format json` output has `header` and `blocks` in the answer.
  (7) Replay (as G3-g open point 6): flat and layers candidates in summary_g2 are T10's records (no sentences, no flat P-4 flag); the layers block order is the live order RUN, WORD, CHAR (T10 lists its tiers alphabetically). The window records were re-read and are identical to G3-g's.
  (8) (added at review) Block order. The owner chose 「出所ごとに区切って見せ、各区切りは安定の順」; the build keeps each block in its source's own order (L-743), which is word-set order for the flat tiers and window-then-word-set order for the windows, not an order by stability (bank2: flat/RUN blocks out of stability order in 61 / 78 and 71 / 82 questions with >= 2 entries). Either the owner confirms the source's own order, or each block is arranged by its own stability (most stable first, the source's order breaking ties: a stable sort inside the block, still no ranking across blocks); that changes the within-block positions of L-748 and the tests of Part 7, and needs the layers' stability, which T10's replayed records do not hold (replay.py gives the layers candidates no stability). Taken up in the G3-g2 addendum: L-749 arranges each block by its stability (layers replayed without stability stay order="source").

### G3-g2 addendum (review, 2026-10-09): the order INSIDE a block is the block's stability order (L-749; verantyx/line3/combined.py `order_blocks`, `blocks()`; tests/line3/test_combined.py; experiments/line3/g3/combined/summarize_g2.py, results/summary_g2.md)
Review finding: L-743 left the order inside a block as the source's own order (flat tiers: the word-set order of readout; windows: read order, then word order) and said there is no sort by stability, but the owner's choice is 「各区切りは安定の順」. L-743's clause "nothing inside a block is re-ordered ... there is no sort by stability" is SUPERSEDED by L-749; the rest of L-740..L-748 stands.

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-749 | EACH BLOCK IN ITS STABILITY ORDER (`merge="none"`). Inside every block the entries stand most stable first, by the candidate's own `stability` as an exact Fraction (never a float), the source's order breaking ties: a stable sort, so equal stabilities stay in the order the source gave and nothing is picked among them by position (the tie is visible as equal `stability` values of adjacent members). Stabilities of different blocks are never compared, and the layers block mixes runs of several tiers and layers by its members' own stabilities (open point 1). A block in which some candidate carries no stability (a layers record replayed from T10, replay.py: no stability recorded) is NOT sorted: it stays in the source's order and `answer.blocks[i].order` is `"source"`; a sorted block says `"stability"`. Live layers carry a stability (e.stability of the layers entry) and are sorted; in the replay measurement the layers block is `order="source"` at both presets. `merge="word_set"` is G3-g's list and never sorts (every block `"source"`; the G3-g golden still matches byte for byte). `answer.blocks` entries are now {block, listed, first, order}; the text form says 「各区切りは安定の高い順（同じ安定はその出所が出した順）…安定の記録がない区切りは出所の順」 and `thought.rule` says so. Flat defaults untouched: `ask.ask` structure "flat" bytes over the 12 toy questions (effort full) have the same sha256 on HEAD 6b62f0fd and on this tree. | combined.py |
| L-749 tests | 43 tests in test_combined.py. Replaced / added: blocks in the fixed order each most stable first with ties in the source order and the layers block `order="source"` (hand-built, stabilities deliberately unsorted, a tie 1/3 = 1/3, twin windows); (a) every sorted block is non-increasing in stability on every toy question and on the hand-built list, stabilities are exact Fractions, live toy layers are never `"source"`; (b) ties keep the source order (also 2/6 against 1/3 and against 333333333333/10^12), a block with one stability-less candidate is not sorted and the other blocks still are; (c) the flat defaults are byte-identical (sha256 above, and structure="flat" equals the default); the toy blocks equal the source's candidates sorted by (-stability, index in the source) worked out without build_entries; the always-true second assertion of test_no_ranking_across_blocks was removed. | tests/line3/test_combined.py |
| L-749 measurement | Same inputs as L-748 (flat and layers replayed from T10, the four window sweeps of L-748). Gold in a candidate is unchanged: 29 / 34 of 69 (fast / standard); unans 0 abstained / 25 list / 0 single wrong; list size median 28.5 / 68, max 151 / 239 (the entries are the same, only their order inside a block changed). First gold in the list: position median 2 / 4.5 and max 80 / 125 (source order, L-743: 3 / 8 and 80 / 124; merged G3-g: 3 / 8 and 80 / 120); WITHIN ITS BLOCK median 1 / 1 and max 39 / 39 (source order: 38 / 43). Per block the gold is found in the same questions (flat/RUN 15 / 18, flat/WORD 6 / 8, flat/CHAR 0 / 0, layers 11 / 24, window/plain 9 / 10, window/window-evidence 19 / 21). Blocks that changed order (block instances over the 94 questions that list the block): fast 149 of 264 sorted blocks (353 listed, the 89 layers blocks are `order="source"`), standard 207 of 328 (422 listed, 94 layers); the first entry of the block changed in 117 / 170; flat/RUN changed in 61 of 81 / 71 of 83 questions, flat/WORD 50 of 72 / 55 of 83, flat/CHAR 6 of 9 / 18 of 33, window/plain 9 of 32 / 19 of 47, window/window-evidence 23 of 70 / 44 of 82; 153 / 198 sorted blocks hold at least one tie of equal stability. Golds only windows find (8 / 7): in the whole list at positions 1-68 of 2-71 (fast) and 10-125 of 10-126 (standard) (source order 1-67 / 9-124), inside their first-gold block at 1-2 (fast) and 1-3 (standard) (source order 1-2 / 1-2): the stability order does not move the window golds forward (the earlier overall median comes from flat/RUN golds, which stand earlier in their block). | summary_g2.md |

### G3-g2 addendum open points (owner questions)
  (1) The stability of a candidate is its source's own quantity: a layers block mixes runs of different tiers, layers and variants, so its stability order interleaves candidates whose stabilities came from different crosses (the owner asked for the order of the source; for the layers the "source's order" is run order, which L-749 replaces by the stability across runs). Sort the layers by run first and by stability inside a run instead?
  (2) Ties are kept in the source's order and are visible only as equal `stability` values; there is no tie-group field in the JSON and no tie mark in the text. 153 / 198 sorted blocks hold ties (flat/RUN 73 of 81 at fast): is a labelled tie group wanted?
  (3) The replay measurement cannot sort the layers block (T10's records carry no stability): the 11 / 24 layers golds stand at their source-order position (1-2 inside the block in both orders). A live re-run of the layers (an hour or more at 8 workers) would show whether the layers' stability order moves them.
  (4) Window candidates have stabilities that are strict-axis fractions of a window class; whether they are comparable to each other inside window/plain (different windows, different classes) is the owner's rule (「安定の順」) applied as written.

## G3-h (2026-10-09): the scope of the deeper z-edge count, `z_deep` "order_window", and the grid of the three pending "measure both" switches measured through the window-flat read (verantyx/line3/slide.py `WindowCounts.deep_z_window`, slide_place.py, slide_ratios.py; tests/line3/test_slide_place5.py; experiments/line3/g3/h/: build_caches.py, measure_h.py, cache_stats_h.py, summarize_h.py, results/summary.md, results/cache_stats.md)
Owner (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md, verbatim): 「両方測ってから決める」 for (a) the arm length (「G3-c2 の実装後の決定」; arm_cap "budget" | "x", G3-c3 L-624) and for (b) the scope of the count of the deeper z-arm edges (「G3-c4 の監査後の決定」: 「(a) コーパス全体の回数と前後（今の実装）、(b) 向こうの文の中だけ（回数 1、前後だけ）」; G3-c4 built (a), `z_deep` "order", and left (b) unbuilt, L-667; the deeper edges' word-order evidence itself is (b) of 「G3-c3 の実装後の決定」); 「両方を比較する」 for (c) the seating on evidence-less arms (「G3-c の実装後の決定」; `seat_empty_axis` "allow" | "deny", built). This ticket builds (b)'s second reading and measures all three together with `z_deep` "slide", through the reading rule the owner chose after G3-e (「窓を平らな十字として読む」: slide_flat.py, G3-f; the combined list of G3-g2 sits on top of it and is not changed). Only the WINDOW rows of the combined list are measured (window/plain and window/window-evidence); the flat cross and the layers are unaffected by these switches. Three modules are extended (slide.py, slide_place.py, slide_ratios.py), docstrings of slide_query.py, one assertion of tests/line3/test_slide_query.py (`Q.Z_DEEPS`), one test file added; placement.py, ask.py, cli.py, slide_flat.py and the behaviour of slide_query.py are untouched (they take the value through `Z_DEEPS`). The default ("slide") and "order" are byte-identical (a digest computed with the committed code, below). Judgement points are numbered L-760.. (L-740..L-749 are G3-g2).

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-760 | `z_deep` gets a third value, "order_window" (spec text `Z_DEEP_WINDOW_TEXT`, param `deep` = "order_window", so the slide spec sha and with it the place spec sha and the cache file name change; the default spec and "order" keep their bytes). The innermost z edge (the centre's neighbour) stays the slide edge, n_z. A DEEPER z edge (both ends are seats of the arm; L-661's positional definition, unchanged) carries the word order of its two units INSIDE the one sentence of the window both of its ends lie in (under growth z_reserved: the OTHER sentence, 「向こうの文」): `WindowCounts.deep_z_window(tier, arm, o, i, sid_o, sid_i)` = (n, omega, rows) with n = 1 when that sentence holds both units, else 0 (COUNT 1, not the corpus pair count of "order"), omega = 1 when the sentence shows the arm's direction (+z: o's first occurrence before i's; -z: after) else 0 ("direction only"), rows = the one x row of that sentence (before-row or after-row). The corpus is not counted: a pair that the corpus has in other sentences but the window's sentence does not is n 0. The scope of the counts (corpus | window) makes no difference: the sentence is a sentence of the window and both scopes hold its x row. | slide.WindowCounts.deep_z_window, Z_DEEPS, Z_DEEP_WINDOW_TEXT |
| L-761 | The sentence of an end. `WindowCounts.end_sid(tier, unit, sid)`: the seat's own `sid` when it has one (it must be a sentence of the window), else the unit's ONLY sentence of the window (`z_side`); a unit of both sentences with no sid of its own has none. The edge has evidence only when both ends name the same sentence (ends in different sentences, an end without a sentence, a unit outside the window, one unit twice: n 0, omega 0, no rows). Different sentences are possible only under a growth other than z_reserved or for a shared unit's second seat; there the edge is a plain "no evidence", NOT a fall-back to the corpus count. | slide.WindowCounts.end_sid |
| L-762 | Where a reader gets the sid. The placement and the verifier read the seat TOKEN (`slide_place.sid_of`: unit + SEP + sid for a unit of both sentences under seat_key unit_sid, else None, then L-761's z_side rule); the record writer reads the tokens of the two ends too (so the record states what the search weighed); slide_ratios reads the SeatRec's sid. Under the default seat_key unit_sid the three agree for every seat (a unit of one sentence has that sentence's sid, a shared unit has one seat per sentence). Under seat_key "unit" (the G3-c legacy, one seat per shared unit, not measured here) a shared unit's token carries no sid, so the placement gives every deeper edge that touches it no evidence while slide_ratios reads the seat's sid: the two can differ there. It is recorded, not guarded (no ValueError): the existing hash-seed tests build every seat_key. | slide_place.sid_of / ArmWeights.from_counts / _seat_rows, slide_ratios.counts_evidence |
| L-763 | The record. A deeper z-arm edge of the representative says `rule` "order_window" (with its n, omega and the one x row as `sources`); the innermost z edge says "slide" (every z_deep other than "slide" tags it, as "order" did); the record gets `z_deep` "order_window"; x / y edges and everything else are as before. The members need no per-edge rule (positional). `ArmWeights` keeps one deep function per rule: the weight of a deeper edge by arm code DEEP_Z + arm is (n, omega) of L-760 with the tokens' sids. | slide_place._edge_evidence / ArmWeights.from_counts, SlidePlacement.z_deep |
| L-764 | The independent verifier. `counts_weight_fn` answers a "+z:deep" / "-z:deep" edge under "order_window" through `slide_place.window_order`, which reads the x table's rows of the pair and filters them by the sentence (the token's sid, else `counts.z_side`); it does not call `deep_z_window` and does not read ArmWeights or the arm codes. It is the G3-c4 precedent (the verifier reads the counts' accessors and tables), NOT a route independent of `counts.z_side` and of the x rows: both routes read those two tables. A test compares the two routes for every ordered pair, both arms and every sid combination of four toys (equal), and `cross_key`, `verify_class_slide`, `verify_fixed_point_slide` on toys under every growth / centre_scope / seat_key. | slide_place.window_order / counts_weight_fn |
| L-765 | slide_ratios. `counts_evidence(counts, tier)` carries `.deep(arm, o, i, sid_o, sid_i)` and `.deep_sids` = True under "order_window" (n = `deep_z_window`'s n, rows ("order_window", sid, o_start, o_end, i_start, i_end): the tag keeps them from being read as x or z rows); `_Reader` passes the two SeatRecs' sids to it and tags the edge term / the walk step with the rule ("order_window"; `EdgeTerm.rule` / `WalkStep.rule`, listed in their documents only when not "slide"). The edge flow, the binding, `evidenced_edges` and the section walk of a z arm read this one evidence (a step over a deeper edge needs n 1 and the energy not to drop). An evidence function without `.deep` keeps the old reading; `table_evidence(table, deep_table)` (hand tables) is unchanged and reads as "order". | slide_ratios.counts_evidence / _Reader._deep / edges_of / walk |
| L-766 | Plumbing. Every `counts.z_deep == "order"` test that meant "has a rule for the deeper edges" became `!= "slide"` (the verifier's `fn.z_deep`, the innermost edge's rule tag); `slide_query.Z_DEEPS` is `slide.Z_DEEPS`, so `WindowIndex.from_space(z_deep=)`, `ask_slide` and `ask_flat` accept the new value with no code change (the cache file name carries the slide spec sha; the header's `z_deep` is "order_window"); the one assertion of tests/line3/test_slide_query.py that listed the values is updated. | slide_place, slide_query (docstrings only) |
| L-767 | Regression gates. (a) "slide" and "order": a sha256 over the "order" spec, counts, placements (4 toys x (centre n z_reserved | centre both z_reserved | centre n n_then_n1), padding one) and slide_ratios documents (both agreement rules) is `53f589a985637dae5e88e55da78c4849f48f11bbe79db60160a4a68c2c291156` with the committed code (git HEAD e4706982) and with this tree (tested); test_slide_place4.py (G3-c4: the first 40 pair windows equal the committed G3-c3 run-A records, the G3-d toy ratios and spec sha) passes unchanged, and so do test_slide_place.py / 2 / 3, test_slide_ratios.py, test_slide.py, test_slide_flat.py, test_combined.py. (b) the real corpus: the two window caches of G3-f (z_deep slide and order, G3-c3 defaults: slide spec 3df141b12327 / place spec 07db87511ff1 and 6b36bcb7306d / 9280bce0035a) are reproduced by a fresh build with this tree in all 592 windows of each (experiments/line3/g3/h/verify_rebuild.py, results/verify_rebuild.md). | tests/line3/test_slide_place5.py |
| L-768 | NOT built here: citing the order_window rows in an entry's `source_sids` (`slide_query._sids_of` reads x and z rows only and skips the ("order_window",) tag as it skips ("order",): G3-c4 open point 6, L-688); a `z_deep` option in ask.py / cli.py; a different weight for the deeper z edges; a deeper rule for the y arms; making a choice between the settings (the owner decides from the measurement); a z_deep-aware comparison of the two centre growths on equal footing (G3-c3 open point 1: the z entries of the three rules' keys are not comparable with one another). | - |

### G3-h tests (tests/line3/test_slide_place5.py: 38 passed, 4 s; the broader set test_slide_place.py + 2 + 3 + 4 + 5, test_slide_ratios.py, test_slide.py, test_slide_query.py, test_slide_flat.py, test_combined.py: 381 passed under each of PYTHONHASHSEED 0 / 1 / 12345 (one seed after the other, the Pro))
Spec: Z_DEEPS has three values (slide.Z_DEEPS == slide_query.Z_DEEPS), the default spec / counts keep their bytes (`b"deep"` is not in them), the three slide specs and the three place specs have three distinct shas, the place spec differs from "order"'s only in the slide sha, the text of the z axis names the rule, a bad value raises, a placement made for another slide spec is refused; the counts' document says `z_deep` only under a rule. Evidence (toy of 4 sentences, both scopes): `deep_z_window` gives n 1 (not the corpus count 2 of "order") and omega by direction for both arms and both orders of a pair, its one row is the x row of that sentence, a unit of both sentences with its own sid reads that sentence, ends in different sentences / a shared unit with no sid / a unit outside the window / one unit twice / a sid that is not a sentence of the window / a pair that only another sentence of the corpus holds: (0, 0, ()), a non-z arm raises; the verifier's `window_order` equals `deep_z_window` for every ordered pair of the window's units, both arms and every sid combination (None, both sentences, a foreign sid) over four toys; the deeper evidence never exceeds the corpus reading (n <= n_x, omega <= before / after). Placement: `ArmWeights` from counts equal under every rule for the arm codes < 6 and the deeper codes equal `deep_z_window` (with tokens carrying SEP + sid); the record of a hand window says "order_window" on every deeper z edge with n in {0, 1}, equal to `deep_z_window` with the tokens' sids and one row in the sentence both ends lie in, "slide" on the innermost, no rule on x / y; the independent verifier accepts every member of every class and its strict counts and per-axis keys equal the record's (4 toys x 5 growth / centre_scope / seat_key combinations incl. seat_key "unit"); a verifier of the other rule reads another key. Ratios: the edge flow and binding of a real order_window placement equal an independent route over `geometry.neighbours`, only the z axis differs from the "order" reading, every deeper step says "order_window" with n 1 and rows tagged in the step's sentence, `check_trace` holds; a hand cross: the +z walk steps over a deeper edge when both seats lie in sentence 1 and stops "unproven" when they lie in different sentences; the rows are tagged and `slide_query._sids_of` does not cite them (L-768). Regression: L-767 (the "order" digest). The window index builds, saves, loads and refuses by the new slide spec sha, `ask_slide` carries `config.z_deep` and refuses a mismatching explicit value. PYTHONHASHSEED 0 / 1 / 12345 give identical bytes over the rule x centre_scope x growth x seat_key x arm_cap x seat_empty_axis x all windows x both agreement rules; no float in the spec, counts, records and ratio documents.

### G3-h measurement (fulllead_sents, bank2 intra2 69 + unans 25, tier RUN, level mid, padding one (592 windows, 292 pairs), centre_scope both, strict judgement, growth z_reserved, seat_key unit_sid in every cell; the window-flat read of G3-f (`slide_flat.ask_flat`: members = the first member of each growth, search budget 512/64, both seats of a shared unit, read order qcount_first, per-axis labels attached), evidence plain | window, windows read fast 4 / standard 10; t9 scorer as G3-f; the Pro, 6 workers, load 4-11 during the cache builds (logs/build_caches.log) and 7-10 during the question runs (load1 6.8-9.7 in the 4512 records); experiments/line3/g3/h/: build_caches.py, measure_h.py, sweep_h.sh, cache_stats_h.py, verify_rebuild.py, summarize_h.py, results/summary.md, cache_stats.md, verify_rebuild.md, logs/; raw question runs git-ignored)
The twelve caches (slide spec 3df141b12327 / 6b36bcb7306d / 3a9ef919a0bb for slide / order / order_window; place specs in results/cache_stats.md) were built in 52-87 s each (cpu 293-509 s; arm_cap x is the faster build under order / order_window, not under slide: 80-86 s for both caps) or, for the two G3-f cells (budget / slide / allow, budget / order / allow), loaded from the G3-f caches, whose spec shas this tree reproduces; a fresh build of those two with this tree equals them in all 592 windows of each (results/verify_rebuild.md). The cells budget / slide / allow and budget / order / allow give, in all four runs, the very verdicts and entries of the G3-f records (94 of 94 questions each, summary.md "Check against the G3-f records"; 9 / 10 plain and 19 / 21 window-evidence gold at fast / standard, unans 18 / 2 / 5 and 6 / 9 / 10 at fast, as G3-f).

seat_empty_axis allow against deny: in every one of the six (arm_cap, z_deep) pairs the two caches have the same seats, members, sizes, keys and steps in ALL 592 windows (pair and padded one-sentence windows alike); they differ only in the record field `seatless_arms` (in 1 pair window and in all 300 padded windows, the arms denied for lack of evidence) and the spec fields. The four runs of every deny cell were nevertheless made and give the same verdicts and entries as the allow cell in 94 of 94 questions in each of the 24 comparisons (summary.md "allow against deny"). The deny rows are therefore omitted below (identical to the allow rows in every column but the time).

Per run, the allow cells (the deny cell of every row is identical but for the time). Gold in a candidate = the gold is a substring of one word of one candidate; single / list by the number of candidate entries as in G3-f (under the combined list G3-g2 a window-only single entry is a list of one entry, never an ANSWER); s/question = wall seconds with 6 workers at load 7-10, not comparable to a quiet machine.

The "other sentence seated, strict" share is 241 / 292 in every cell (it does not depend on the three switches).

What the numbers show (facts; the owner decides; the run order below is fast plain / fast window / standard plain / standard window; gold = gold in a candidate of 69). (1) arm_cap, over the same z_deep: x has the gold in a candidate in as many or more questions than budget in 10 of the 12 (z_deep, run) pairs (slide 10 / 20 / 11 / 22 against 9 / 19 / 10 / 21; order 11 / 23 / 12 / 24 against 7 / 20 / 8 / 21; order_window 10 / 21 / 11 / 22 against 9 / 22 / 10 / 24, of which two pairs are lower: window evidence 21 against 22 and 22 against 24). Summed over the three z_deep rules (allow cells): gold x 31 / 64 / 34 / 68 against budget 25 / 61 / 28 / 66; unans abstained x 55 / 23 / 45 / 17 against budget 51 / 11 / 43 / 9; unans single wrong x 12 / 17 / 12 / 15 against 16 / 26 / 15 / 21; answerable single wrong x 17 / 37 / 34 / 22 against 16 / 38 / 22 / 22 (higher in fast plain by 1 and standard plain by 12, lower in fast window by 1, equal in standard window). The x caches are built faster (52-54 s against 70-77 s for order / order_window), their classes are smaller (median 13.5 / 20 against 101 / 206 for order / order_window) and a question costs less (order, median s/question: fast plain 2.00 against 3.61, standard plain 4.65 against 9.22; slide: no difference). (2) z_deep, over the same arm_cap: order_window against slide: gold 9 / 22 / 10 / 24 against 9 / 19 / 10 / 21 (budget) and 10 / 21 / 11 / 22 against 10 / 20 / 11 / 22 (x); against order: 9 / 22 / 10 / 24 against 7 / 20 / 8 / 21 (budget), 10 / 21 / 11 / 22 against 11 / 23 / 12 / 24 (x). Answerable single wrong with window evidence (fast / standard): budget slide 17 / 12, order 14 / 8, order_window 7 / 2; x slide 18 / 10, order 8 / 6, order_window 11 / 6; with plain evidence: budget slide 5 / 10, order 7 / 7, order_window 4 / 5; x slide 6 / 11, order 5 / 11, order_window 6 / 12. Unans single wrong with window evidence (fast / standard): budget slide 10 / 9, order 8 / 6, order_window 8 / 6; x slide 7 / 7, order 5 / 4, order_window 5 / 4; unans abstained with window evidence: budget slide 6 / 4, order 3 / 3, order_window 2 / 2; x slide 7 / 5, order 8 / 6, order_window 8 / 6 (plain evidence: summary.md). The order_window lists are the longest by the median under budget (the largest single list is order's; standard window: median 6 entries / 33.5 words, max 17 entries; order 5 / 29, max 24; slide 4 / 22, max 11). (3) seat_empty_axis: no difference in seats, members or answers (above). (4) Evidence plain: every cell has 7 to 12 questions with the gold and 41 to 46 (fast) / 27 to 32 (standard) with no candidate, the cells differing by 1 to 4 questions. By question (summary.md "Which answerable questions"): R2-I013 and R2-I018 have the gold in a candidate under slide and not in any order or order_window cell (all four runs); I2-035 is gained in every order and order_window cell. (5) The cell with the most golds per run: x / order in fast plain (11), fast window (23, next budget / order_window 22) and standard plain (12); budget / order_window and x / order tie in standard window (24 each; next 22). The fewest answerable single wrong in a run: budget / order_window in all four (4 / 7 / 5 / 2). The fewest unans single wrong in a run: 4 (the x cells) / 5 (x / order, x / order_window) / 3 (x / slide) / 4 (x / order, x / order_window); no cell has fewer than 3.

### G3-h open points (owner; meaning level)
(1) The deeper-edge count of "order_window" gives n 1 to every deeper edge with both ends seated (1098 / 1098 budget, 755 / 755 x: under growth z_reserved both ends always lie in the other sentence, so n carries no information) and the direction (omega 1) to 98.9 / 98.8 %: with a z arm that holds one sentence the edge is a pure word-order edge, so it is not a "more evidence" or "less evidence" question but which of two readings of 「向こうの文」 the reader should use; the numbers above do not decide it. (2) The three rules' z keys are not comparable (G3-c4 open point 3 and G3-c3 open point 1 stay open, more pressing: the share of windows that keep both growths is 249 / 292 under order and 233 / 292 under order_window against 106 under slide at budget). (3) arm_cap x seats fewer units (65.9 / 73.2 / 73.2 % of the pairs' units against 67.2 / 78.8 / 77.7 % under budget) and leaves 351 / 486 / 455 units unseated by the cap (slide / order / order_window); the question runs above do not show a loss in the gold counts from this. (4) seat_empty_axis: since allow and deny seat the same units everywhere (above), the choice has no effect on any measured output; "deny" only records the denied arms. (5) Window-only single entries never ANSWER in the combined list (G3-g L-724); the single right / wrong columns are the flat grader's and are the lists of one entry there. (6) The sample is 69 + 25 questions of one corpus and the differences between cells are mostly 1 to 3 questions; no significance test was made. (7) `source_sids` does not cite the order_window rows (L-768).

## G3-i (2026-10-09): the owner's defaults of the window placements after G3-h, `z_deep` "order" and `seat_empty_axis` "deny" (verantyx/line3/slide.py `DEFAULT_Z_DEEP`, `default_spec`; slide_place.py `DEFAULTS`, `DEFAULTS_G3H`; slide_query.py / slide_flat.py / combined.py default plumbing; cli.py `--z-deep` default and help; tests/line3/test_slide_defaults.py and the pinned fixtures of the G3-c..G3-h, G3-f and G3-g tests)
Owner (ops/decisions/2026-10-06_line3_faithful_build.md, 「G3-h の測定後の決定」, verbatim choices): the arm length 「予算で止まるまで（今の既定）」 (arm_cap "budget", unchanged); the evidence of the deeper z-arm edges 「コーパス全体の語順（x と同じ数え方）を既定に (Recommended)」 (z_deep "order", was "slide"); seating on an arm with no evidence 「座らせないを既定に（字義どおり。結果は同じ） (Recommended)」 (seat_empty_axis "deny", was "allow"). Everything else stays as the owner chose before: centre_scope "both", growth z_reserved, stability "per_axis" with the "strict" judgement, seat_key "unit_sid", and one constructed padding sentence per article. Only defaults change: no value, rule or reading is added or removed, every former default stays reachable by naming its value, and the flat `ask` path is untouched. Judgement points are numbered L-770.. (L-760..L-768 are G3-h).

| id | decision | where |
|---|---|---|
| L-770 | `z_deep` defaults to "order". `slide.DEFAULT_Z_DEEP = "order"`; `slide.default_spec(space, ..., z_deep=DEFAULT_Z_DEEP)`, so `Slide(space)` without a spec, `WindowIndex.from_space` / `from_jsonl`, `window_index_for`, `ask_slide`, `ask_flat` and `combined.ask_combined` (their `z_deep=None` means the default, and an index of another rule is still refused when the value is named) build and read the "order" spec. `WindowIndex.from_space` always builds `default_spec(space, z_deep=z_deep)` (its former shortcut `Slide(space, rows=rows)` for "slide" would now have built "order"). The former default is `z_deep="slide"`: its spec bytes are the G3-a..G3-h default spec's (no `deep` parameter, no `z_deep` in the counts and records; fulllead slide spec sha 3df141b12327), nothing else about "slide" changed. `slide.default_axes` keeps "slide" as its own default (it is the G3-a axes definition; `default_spec` always passes the value). | slide.DEFAULT_Z_DEEP, default_spec, slide_query.WindowIndex.from_space / window_index_for / ask_slide, slide_flat.ask_flat, combined.ask_combined |
| L-771 | `seat_empty_axis` defaults to "deny". `slide_place.DEFAULTS` = per_axis, unit_sid, deny, z_reserved (`PlaceSpec` and `make_spec` take it from there); `DEFAULTS3` is unchanged (both, budget, strict). New named configuration `DEFAULTS_G3H` = `dict(DEFAULTS, seat_empty_axis="allow")` (the four switches as they were from G3-c2 through G3-h); `LEGACY4`, `C2_EQUIV`, `LEGACY` and `DEFAULTS3` are untouched, so the G3-c / G3-c2 reproductions need nothing new. G3-h measured that allow and deny seat the same units, with the same members, sizes, keys and steps, in all 592 windows of every (arm_cap, z_deep) pair; the placement record differs only in `seatless_arms` and the spec document (`switches`, `rules`: the place spec sha and with it the cache file name change). | slide_place.DEFAULTS, DEFAULTS_G3H |
| L-772 | Unchanged defaults, stated once: arm_cap "budget", centre_scope "both", stability_judgement "strict", stability "per_axis", seat_key "unit_sid", growth "z_reserved", tier RUN, budget level "mid" (the flat index's). Padding: the question path says "one" everywhere already (`WindowIndex.from_space` / `from_jsonl`, `window_index_for`, `ask_slide`, `ask_flat`, `ask_combined`; the command line has no padding option), the owner's one constructed sentence per article (592 windows on fulllead: 292 pairs + 300 padded one-sentence windows); nothing was changed for it.
